## C1 - 98:5 - wa ma umiru illa li-ya'budu Allaha mukhlisina lahu al-dina hanifan wa yuqimu-s-salata wa yu'tu-z-zakata

ARABIC (flow): 98:1 "Those who disbelieved among the People of the Book and the polytheists were NOT going to desist until there came to them the CLEAR PROOF (al-bayyina)" -> 98:2 "a Messenger from God, reciting PURIFIED PAGES" -> 98:3 "in which are UPRIGHT scriptures (kutub qayyima)" -> 98:4 "and those who were given the Book did not split EXCEPT after the clear proof came to them" -> 98:5 "and they were NOT commanded except to WORSHIP God, making the religion PURE for Him, UPRIGHT (hanifan), and to ESTABLISH THE SALAH and to GIVE THE ZAKAH - and THAT is the religion of UPRIGHTNESS (din al-qayyimah)" -> 98:6 "indeed those who disbelieved among the People of the Book and the polytheists are in the fire of Jahannam, abiding therein; those are the WORST of creatures" -> 98:7 "indeed those who believed and did righteous deeds - those are the BEST of creatures" -> 98:8 "their reward with their Lord is gardens of Eden beneath which rivers flow, abiding therein forever; God is pleased with them and they are pleased with Him; that is for one who FEARS his Lord."

WORD FORM: yuqīmū al-ṣalāta (establish the ṣalāh) + yuʾtū al-zakāta (give the zakah) - the FIRST of the two commanded acts. The verb yuqīmū (from q-w-m, to stand/upright) pairs with ṣalāh (from ṣ-l-w, standing-state): "make-stand the standing-state" - the ṣalāh is STOOD UP, established, made upright. The noun ṣalāh is in the accusative as the object of "establish."

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Bayyina (98). The verse sits in the minimal-religion passage: the entire religion reduces to four elements - (1) worship God purely (mukhliṣīn), (2) upright (ḥanīfan), (3) establish the ṣalāh, (4) give the zakah. The ṣalāh is ONE of the FOUR minimal elements. 98:6-8 then give the two outcomes: disbelievers in Jahannam (worst of creatures) vs believers in gardens (best of creatures), with the divider being whether one FEARS his Lord (98:8).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE MINIMAL RELIGION VERSE: "wa dhālika dīn al-qayyimah" - "and THAT is the religion of UPRIGHTNESS." The entire religion = four elements, and the ṢALĀH is one of them. The ṢALĀH is not a supplement to the religion; it is a DEFINING element of it. Without ṢALĀH, the religion is not upright (qayyimah).
- THE PURITY-ELEMENT (mukhliṣīna lahu al-dīna): "making the religion PURE for Him" - the same purity-gate as A12 (56:79 "none touch it except the purified"), A15 (74:4 "purify your garments"), and C5 (2:125 "purify My House"). The religion is to be made PURE (mukhliṣ) for God - the ṢALĀH is part of the pure religion. The standing-site (C5) requires purification; the standing-state (C1) requires purity of religion.
- THE UPRIGHT-ELEMENT (ḥanīfan): "upright" - the same word as 2:135 ("the religion of Abraham, upright/ḥanīf"). The religion of Abraham IS the upright religion, and the upright religion INCLUDES establishing the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the upright (ḥanīf) standing - the standing-toward-God (A19's fa-ṣallā) is the upright standing.
- THE ESTABLISH-VERB (yuqīmū): "establish" (from q-w-m, to stand/upright) + ṢALĀH (from ṣ-l-w, standing-state) = "make-stand the standing-state." The ṢALĀH is not just "performed"; it is ESTABLISHED, made upright, STOOD UP. The verb yuqīmū implies a building-up, a setting-in-place, a permanent establishment - not a momentary act. This is the STRONGEST "state not act" proof on the command-side: you do not "do" the ṢALĀH; you ESTABLISH it.
- THE ṢALĀH + ZAKAH PAIR (yuqīmū al-ṣalāta wa yuʾtū al-zakāta): the TWO commanded acts are paired: establish the ṢALĀH and give the ZAKAH. The ṢALĀH (standing-state) and the ZAKAH (purifying-giving) are the TWO pillars of the minimal religion. This pair will recur throughout C/D/E (2:43, 2:83, 2:110, 4:77, 9:103, 22:41, 33:33). The ṢALĀH and the ZAKAH are the STANDING and the GIVING - the two acts that define the believer.
- 98:2 "a Messenger from God, reciting purified pages" - the Messenger (2:129, 2:151) is the human agent of the chain; the purified pages (ṣuḥufan muṭahharatan) are the ṢALĀH's scripture-source. The ṢALĀH-chain has a prophetic agent AND a purified text.
- 98:6-7 "worst of creatures" vs "best of creatures": the ṢALĀH (established) is the DIVIDER between worst and best. The ṢALĀH is the gate: those who establish it (with purity and uprightness) are the best; those who do not are the worst (and their dwelling is Jahannam, 98:6).
- 98:8 "that is for one who FEARS his Lord" - the ṢALĀH is for the one who fears (yakhshā). The same "fear" that drives the mention (87:10 "he who fears will mention" A18) and the standing (87:15 fa-ṣallā A19). Fear -> mention -> stand -> ṢALĀH. The chain: fear -> mention -> stand -> establish ṢALĀH -> zakah.

PARALLELS:
- C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site): the SITE where the ṢALĀH is established. The MUSSALLA (standing-site) and the ṢALĀH (standing-state) are paired: the SITE is where the STATE is established. The standing-site (C5) and the standing-state (C1) are ONE covenant.
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (established) IS. The ṢALĀH is the ESTABLISHED form of fa-ṣallā. The command "establish the ṢALĀH" = "make permanent the fa-ṣallā."
- B2 (2:157 - ṢALAWĀT upon the patient): the divine ṢALAWĀT is granted to those who ESTABLISH the ṢALĀH (C1). The human ṢALĀH (established) invites the divine ṢALAWĀT (granted). The ṢALĀH-chain: human establish (C1) -> divine ṢALAWĀT (B2).
- B5 (9:103 - "your ṣalāh is their tranquility"): the Prophet's ṢALĀH (established) brings tranquility (sakan) to the repentant. The established-ṢALĀH is the healing act.
- A12 (56:79 - purity-gate: none touch it except the purified): the ṢALĀH requires purity (mukhliṣīn). The same purity-gate.
- A15 (74:4 - "purify your garments"): the standing-prescription's purity step. The ṢALĀH's purity (mukhliṣīn) is the same garment-purity.
- 87:18-19 (A19 - "this is in the first Scrolls, the Scrolls of Abraham and Moses"): the ṢALĀH (established) is ALREADY WRITTEN in the Scrolls of Abraham. The ṢALĀH is covenant-tradition, not novelty.

FINDING: C1 gives the MINIMAL RELIGION: worship God purely (mukhliṣīn) + upright (ḥanīfan) + establish the ṢALĀH + give the zakah = "the religion of uprightness (dīn al-qayyimah)." The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED (yuqīmū = make-stand the standing-state), not performed as a momentary act. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is the TWO-pillar formula of the minimal religion. The ṢALĀH is the DIVIDER between "worst of creatures" (98:6, Jahannam) and "best of creatures" (98:7, gardens). And the ṢALĀH is for "one who fears his Lord" (98:8) - fear -> mention -> stand -> establish ṢALĀH -> zakah. The ṢALĀH-chain's entry-point is FEAR (yakhshā), the same fear that drives the mention (87:10) and the standing (87:15).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The ESTABLISH-verb (yuqīmū) + ṢALĀH - the ṢALĀH is STOOD UP, not performed. HOLD as the command-side's strongest "state not act" proof. (ii) The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair - the TWO-pillar formula; HOLD for the final doc's command-table (it recurs in 2:43, 2:83, 2:110, 4:77, 9:103, 22:41, 33:33). (iii) The purity-element (mukhliṣīn) - the ṢALĀH requires purity of religion; HOLD against C5's purification-covenant and A12's purity-gate. (iv) The upright-element (ḥanīfan) - the ṢALĀH is the upright standing; HOLD against 2:135 (religion of Abraham, upright). (v) The fear-element (yakhshā 98:8) - fear -> mention -> stand -> establish ṢALĀH -> zakah; HOLD as the chain's entry-point.

VERDICT: studied - see C2 (2:43 - Bani Israel commanded) next.

## C2 - 2:43 - wa-aqimu-s-salata wa-atu-z-zakata wa-rka'u ma'a-r-raki'in

ARABIC (flow): 2:38 "We said: descend from it, all of you; then if guidance comes to you from Me, whoever follows My guidance - no fear shall be on them, nor shall they grieve" -> 2:39 "those who disbelieved and denied Our signs - those are the companions of the Fire; they abide therein" -> 2:40 "O Children of Israel, REMEMBER My favor which I bestowed upon you, and FULFILL My covenant, I will fulfill your covenant, and FEAR Me" -> 2:41 "believe in what I have sent down, confirming what is with you, and do not be the first to disbelieve in it, and do not sell My signs for a small price, and fear Me" -> 2:42 "do not mix the truth with falsehood, and do not conceal the truth while you know" -> 2:43 "AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH AND BOW WITH THOSE WHO BOW" -> 2:44 "do you command people to righteousness and forget yourselves while you recite the Book? Do you not reason?" -> 2:45 "seek help in patience and the ṣalāh; and it is indeed HEAVY except upon the HUMBLE (khāshiʿīn)" -> 2:46 "who think that they will MEET their Lord and that they will RETURN to Him" -> 2:47 "O Children of Israel, remember My favor which I bestowed upon you, and that I preferred you over the worlds" -> 2:48 "fear a Day when no soul shall avail another soul at all, nor shall intercession be accepted from it, nor shall compensation be taken from it, nor shall they be helped."

WORD FORM: yuqīmū al-ṣalāta (establish the ṣalāh) + yuʾtū al-zakāta (give the zakah) + wa-rkaʿū maʿa al-rākiʿīn (bow with those who bow) - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1's two-pillar formula) with a THIRD element: BOW WITH THOSE WHO BOW (the community-dimension).

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Baqara (2). The verse sits in the Bani-Israel passage (2:40-48): remember My favor, fulfill My covenant, believe, do not mix truth with falsehood, and ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH AND BOW WITH THOSE WHO BOW. 2:44 charges hypocrisy (commanding others but forgetting themselves); 2:45 says the ṢALĀH is HEAVY except for the humble; 2:46 says it is for those who think they will MEET their Lord and RETURN to Him.

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE THREE-ELEMENT COMMAND: establish the ṢALĀH + give the ZAKAH + bow with those who bow. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1's two-pillar) now has a COMMUNITY-DIMENSION: "bow with those who bow" (rakʿū maʿa al-rākiʿīn). The ṢALĀH is not a solitary act; it is performed IN COMMUNITY - with those who bow. The standing-site (MUSSALLA, C5) is where the community bows together.
- THE ESTABLISH-VERB (yuqīmū) AGAIN: "establish the ṣalāh" - the same yuqīmū as C1 (98:5). The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED (made-stand, set-up, made upright), not performed as a momentary act. The command is to MAKE THE STANDING-STATE STAND - to establish it permanently, not to do it once.
- THE HEAVY-QUALITY (2:45 "it is indeed heavy except upon the humble"): the ṢALĀH is HEAVY (kabīra) - it is a WEIGHT. The fire-stand was also heavy (A-cluster's imposed standing was a burden), but the God-side ṢALĀH's heaviness is BEARABLE by the humble (khāshiʿ). The ṢALĀH is heavy for the proud and light for the humble - the same act, different weight depending on the STANDER's posture.
- THE MEET-AND-RETURN ORIENTATION (2:46 "who think that they will MEET their Lord and that they will RETURN to Him"): the ṢALĀH is for those who ORIENT toward MEETING (mulāqāh) and RETURNING (rajaʿa). The same meet-word as A17 (84:6 "you will MEET Him") and the same return-word as B2 (2:156 "we belong to God and to Him we return"). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who faces toward MEETING and RETURNING - the standing-toward is a facing-toward the Meeting.
- THE HYPOCRISY-CHARGE (2:44 "do you command people to righteousness and forget yourselves?"): the ṢALĀH is for THEMSELVES, not just for commanding others. The hypocrite commands others to do good but forgets his own ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is a PERSONAL standing, not a public performance.
- 2:40 "fulfill My covenant" - the ṢALĀH is part of the COVENANT (ʿahd). The covenant is: remember favor + fulfill covenant + believe + do not mix truth with falsehood + establish ṢALĀH + give zakah + bow with those who bow. The ṢALĀH is a COVENANT-ACT - the same covenant-tradition as C5 (2:125 "We covenanted to Abraham and Isma'il").
- 2:47 "I preferred you over the worlds" - the ṢALĀH is given to a PREFERRED community (Bani Israel). The ṢALĀH is a PRIVILEGE, not a burden - the same honor-economy as the divine ṢALĀH (B-cluster): granted to the qualified.

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + ZAKAH) in both C1 and C2. The formula is STABLE across suras: establish + give. C2 adds the third element (bow with those who bow) - the community-dimension.
- C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site): the SITE where the ṢALĀH is established. The community-bowing (C2) happens at the SITE (C5). The standing-site and the community-standing are paired.
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward is what the ṢALĀH (established) IS. The command "establish the ṢALĀH" = "make permanent the fa-ṣallā" - and do it IN COMMUNITY (bow with those who bow).
- B2 (2:157 - ṢALAWĀT upon the patient): the divine ṢALAWĀT is granted to those who ESTABLISH the ṢALĀH (C2). The human ṢALĀH (established in community) invites the divine ṢALAWĀT.
- A17 (84:6 - "you will MEET Him"): the meet-word (mulāqāh) appears in C2's "who think that they will MEET their Lord" - the ṢALĀH is for those who orient toward MEETING. The same meet-word that A17 used for the positive anchor.
- B2 (2:156 - "we belong to God and to Him we return"): the return-word (rajaʿa) appears in C2's "they will RETURN to Him" - the ṢALĀH is for those who orient toward RETURNING. The same return-grammar.

FINDING: C2 adds the COMMUNITY-DIMENSION to the ṢALĀH: "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah AND BOW WITH THOSE WHO BOW." The ṢALĀH is not a solitary act; it is performed IN COMMUNITY. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1's two-pillar formula) is now joined by a THIRD element: community-bowing. And the ṢALĀH is HEAVY except for the humble (2:45) - the same weight as the fire-stand, but bearable by the humble. The ṢALĀH is for those who ORIENT toward MEETING and RETURNING (2:46) - the same meet/return grammar as A17 and B2. And the ṢALĀH is a COVENANT-ACT (2:40): fulfill My covenant -> establish the ṢALĀH.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The COMMUNITY-DIMENSION (rakʿū maʿa al-rākiʿīn) - the ṢALĀH is performed IN COMMUNITY; HOLD for D-cluster (Friday 62:9-10, the collective prayer). (ii) The HEAVY-QUALITY (kabīra) except for the humble (khāshiʿ) - the ṢALĀH's weight is posture-dependent. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check: does the ṢALĀH lighten the heavy?). (iii) The MEET-AND-RETURN orientation (2:46) - the ṢALĀH is for those who face toward MEETING and RETURNING. HOLD as the positive anchor (matches A17's "toil toward your Lord and you will MEET Him"). (iv) The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is STABLE (C1, C2, and will recur in 2:83, 2:110, 4:77, 9:103, 22:41, 33:33).

VERDICT: studied - see C3 (2:83 - mithaq Bani Israel) next.

## C3 - 2:83 - mithaq Bani Israel: aqimu-s-salata wa atu-z-zakata

ARABIC (flow): 2:75-79 the unlettered ones, the Book-written-by-hands, the wishful thinkers -> 2:80 "they said: the Fire will not touch us except for a few days" -> 2:81 "whoever earns evil and his sin encompasses him - those are the companions of the Fire" -> 2:82 "those who believe and do righteous deeds - those are the companions of the Garden" -> 2:83 "when We took a COVENANT from the Children of Israel: do not worship except God, and be good to parents, and to relatives, and to orphans, and to the needy, and speak good to people, AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH; THEN YOU TURNED AWAY except a few of you, and you were turning away" -> 2:84-85 "when We took your covenant: do not shed your blood... then you affirmed it... then here you are, killing yourselves and expelling a party of you... supporting each other in sin and transgression... do you believe in part of the Book and disbelieve in part?" -> 2:86 "those are the ones who bought the worldly life with the Hereafter" -> 2:87-88 "We gave Moses the Book... and Jesus... whenever a messenger came to you with what your souls did not desire, you became arrogant... you denied some and killed some" -> 2:89-90 "when there came to them a Book from God, confirming what is with them... they disbelieved in it; so the curse of God is on the disbelievers."

WORD FORM: aqīmū al-ṣalāta (establish the ṣalāh) + ātū al-zakāta (give the zakah) - the SAME two-pillar formula as C1 (98:5) and C2 (2:43). The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is item 7-8 of an 8-item covenant. The ṢALĀH is embedded in a SOCIAL covenant.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Baqara (2). The verse sits in the Bani-Israel passage (2:75-90): the unlettered, the Book-written-by-hands, the denial of the Fire, the covenant-breakers who turned away, the partial-belief (believe in part, disbelieve in part), the preference for the worldly life, the rejection of the prophetic chain (Moses -> Jesus).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE COVENANT-PACKAGE (mithāq): the covenant includes EIGHT items: (1) worship God alone, (2) be good to parents, (3) relatives, (4) orphans, (5) needy, (6) speak good to people, (7) ESTABLISH THE SALAH, (8) GIVE THE ZAKAH. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is item 7-8 - the LAST TWO items of the covenant. The ṢALĀH is embedded in a SOCIAL-ETHICAL package: it is not isolated but part of a comprehensive ethical-religious covenant. The standing-state (ṢALĀH) and the giving-state (ZAKAH) are the two FINAL acts of the covenant.
- THE TURNING-AWAY (tawallaytum + muʿriḍūn): "THEN YOU TURNED AWAY except a few of you, and you were turning away." The TURNING-AWAY (tawalla = turn away/steer away) is the SAME verb as the fire-people: A4 (4:115 "nuwallīhi mā tawallā" - God steers him as he turned) and A21 (92:16 "kadhdhaba wa tawalla" - denied and turned away). The covenant-breakers TURNED AWAY from the ṢALĀH. The same verb (tawalla) that described the fire-people's self-steering now describes the covenant-breakers' turning away from the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the act that is TURNED AWAY from.
- THE PARTIAL-BELIEF (2:85 "do you believe in part of the Book and disbelieve in part?"): the covenant-breakers believed in PART (the ṢALĀH) and disbelieved in PART (the social ethics). The ṢALĀH is part of a PACKAGE; breaking one part breaks the whole. The ṢALĀH without the social ethics is PARTIAL-belief - not enough.
- THE WORLDLY-PREFERENCE (2:86 "those are the ones who bought the worldly life with the Hereafter"): the SAME preference as A6 (17:18 "whoever wants the immediate") and A18 (87:16 "you prefer the immediate life"). The covenant-breakers preferred the world over the ṢALĀH-covenant. The wealth-thread (A1 A14 A17 A21 A22) returns: the worldly preference is the wealth-preference.
- THE FIRE-DENIAL (2:80 "they said: the Fire will not touch us except for a few days"): the SAME denial as the fire-people (A1-A22). The covenant-breakers deny the Fire while breaking the ṢALĀH-covenant. The ṢALĀH-breakers are fire-companions (2:81 "those are the companions of the Fire").
- 2:81 "whoever earns evil and his sin encompasses him - those are the companions of the Fire" - the fire-delivery formula returns: earning evil -> Fire. The ṢALĀH-breakers earn evil by turning away.
- 2:87-88 "whenever a messenger came to you with what your souls did not desire, you became arrogant... you denied some and killed some" - the prophetic chain (Moses, then Jesus) was rejected. The ṢALĀH-covenant was given through the prophetic chain (2:129 "raise up a Messenger"). The covenant-breakers rejected the prophetic agents of the ṢALĀH.
- 2:89-90 "when there came to them a Book from God, confirming what is with them... they disbelieved in it" - the ṢALĀH-covenant was confirmed by a new Book (the Quran), and they disbelieved in it. The ṢALĀH is part of a chain of confirmation.

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + ZAKAH) in both C1 and C3. The formula is STABLE across suras. C3 adds the SOCIAL-ETHICAL package (parents, relatives, orphans, needy, speak good) - the ṢALĀH is embedded in a comprehensive ethics.
- C2 (2:43 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah and bow with those who bow"): C2 added the community-dimension (bow with those who bow); C3 adds the SOCIAL-ETHICAL package. The ṢALĀH's context grows: C1 (minimal religion) -> C2 (community) -> C3 (social ethics).
- A4 (4:115 - "nuwallīhi mā tawallā"): the tawalla-verb appears in both A4 (fire-delivery: God steers him as he turned) and C3 (covenant-breaking: they turned away from the ṢALĀH). The ṢALĀH is the act that is TURNED AWAY from, and the turning-away is the SAME verb as the fire-people's self-steering.
- A6 (17:18 - "whoever wants the immediate"): the worldly-preference (2:86) matches A6's immediate-preference. The ṢALĀH-breakers prefer the world.
- A21 (92:16 - "kadhdhaba wa tawalla"): the double-verb (deny-and-turn) matches C3's turning-away. The ṢALĀH-breakers deny-and-turn, the same as the fire-people.
- B7 (9:84 - "do not s-l-l on them"): the withholding of ṢALĀH from the disbelievers matches C3's turning-away from the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is withheld from those who turn away, and those who turn away are denied the ṢALĀH.

FINDING: C3 shows the ṢALĀH embedded in a SOCIAL-ETHICAL COVENANT: worship God alone + be good to parents/relatives/orphans/needy + speak good to people + ESTABLISH THE SALAH + GIVE THE ZAKAH. The ṢALĀH is item 7-8 of an 8-item package. And the covenant-breakers TURNED AWAY from it (tawallaytum) - the SAME verb as the fire-people's self-steering (A4, A21). The ṢALĀH is the act that is TURNED AWAY from. The partial-belief (believe in part, disbelieve in part, 2:85) is not enough: the ṢALĀH without the social ethics is PARTIAL. And the worldly-preference (2:86) matches the fire-people's immediate-preference (A6, A18). The ṢALĀH-breakers are fire-companions (2:81). The ṢALĀH-covenant was given through the prophetic chain (Moses, Jesus) and confirmed by the Quran - and they disbelieved in it (2:89-90).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is STABLE (C1 98:5, C2 2:43, C3 2:83) - the two-pillar formula recurs. (ii) The TAWALLA-verb (turn away) - the ṢALĀH is the act that is TURNED AWAY from; the same verb as the fire-people's self-steering. HOLD for the final doc's verb-family table. (iii) The partial-belief (2:85) - the ṢALĀH without the social ethics is PARTIAL; HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check: does the ṢALĀH transform the social ethics?). (iv) The worldly-preference (2:86) - the ṢALĀH-breakers prefer the world; HOLD for the wealth-thread (A1 A14 A17 A21 A22). (v) The prophetic chain (2:87) - the ṢALĀH-covenant was given through Moses and Jesus; HOLD for C-cluster's covenant-tradition.

VERDICT: studied - see C4 (5:12 - mithaq Bani Israel) next.

## C4 - 5:12 - mithaq Bani Israel: la-in aqamtum al-salata wa ataytum al-zakata

ARABIC (flow): 5:12 "God took a covenant from the Children of Israel, and We raised among them twelve leaders. And God said: I am with you IF you ESTABLISH THE SALAH and GIVE THE ZAKAH and believe in My messengers and support them and lend God a good loan; I will surely remove from you your evil deeds and admit you to gardens beneath which rivers flow. But whoever disbelieves after that among you has strayed from the right path." -> 5:13 "because of their breaking of the covenant, We cursed them and made their hearts hard; they distort the words from their places and have forgotten a portion of what they were reminded of" -> 5:14 "from those who say: we are Christians, We took their covenant, but they forgot a portion of what they were reminded of, so We stirred up enmity and hatred among them until the Day of Resurrection" -> 5:15-16 "O People of the Book, Our Messenger has come to you... a light and a clear Book... By it God guides those who seek His pleasure to the paths of PEACE, and brings them out of darkness into light by His permission, and guides them to a straight path" -> 5:17-19 "those who say God is the Messiah have disbelieved... O People of the Book, Our Messenger has come to you to make clear [the religion] after a gap in the messengers" -> 5:20 "Moses said: O my people, remember the favor of God upon you when He made prophets among you and made you kings and gave you what He did not give to anyone among the worlds."

WORD FORM: aqamtum al-ṣalāta (establish the ṣalāh) + ātaytum al-zakāta (give the zakah) - the SAME two-pillar formula as C1 (98:5) and C2 (2:43) and C3 (2:83). The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is now with FIVE conditions: (1) establish ṢALĀH, (2) give zakah, (3) believe in messengers, (4) support them, (5) lend God a good loan.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Ma'ida (5). The verse sits in the People-of-the-Book passage (5:12-20): the covenant with Bani Israel (twelve leaders), the breaking of the covenant (cursed, hearts hardened), the Christians' covenant (forgotten, enmity stirred), the Messenger's arrival (light and clear Book, brings out of darkness into light), the Messiah-dispute, and Moses' reminder of God's favor.

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE COVENANT-PROMISE: "I am with you IF you establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah and believe in My messengers and support them and lend God a good loan." The ṢALĀH is condition #1 - the FIRST of five. And the PROMISE: "I will remove your evil deeds and admit you to gardens." The ṢALĀH is the GATE-condition: establish the ṢALĀH first, then the rest follows. The ṢALĀH is the entry-point of the covenant.
- THE TWELVE LEADERS (naqīb): the covenant is given to twelve leaders - the community-leadership structure. The ṢALĀH is established by the leaders for the community. The standing-site (MUSSALLA, C5) is where the leaders establish the ṢALĀH.
- 5:13 "because of their breaking of the covenant, We cursed them and made their hearts hard" - the SAME curse (la'nah) as 2:161 and 33:57. The covenant-breakers are CURSED. The ṢALĀH-breakers are cursed. The la'nah vs ṢALĀH polarity (B7) returns: the ṢALĀH is withheld from the covenant-breakers.
- 5:14 "they forgot a portion of what they were reminded of" - the SAME partial-belief as 2:85 ("do you believe in part of the Book and disbelieve in part?"). The covenant-breakers FORGOT a portion - the ṢALĀH and the social ethics. The ṢALĀH is part of a package that can be partially forgotten.
- 5:15-16 "Our Messenger has come to you... a light and a clear Book... By it God guides those who seek His pleasure to the paths of PEACE, and brings them out of darkness into light by His permission" - the EXODUS-clause (darkness -> light) returns: the Messenger brings them OUT OF DARKNESS INTO LIGHT - the same exodus as B3 (33:43 "He brings you out of darkness into light"). The ṢALĀH-covenant is now offered through the Messenger (Muhammad) to the People of the Book. The ṢALĀH is the path to peace (subul al-salām) and light (nūr).
- 5:18 "to Him is the DESTINATION (maṣīr)" - the SAME maṣīr-word as A4/A13/2:126 - the destination of the disbeliever. The ṢALĀH-covenant's opposite is the maṣīr of the disbeliever.
- 5:20 "Moses said: O my people, remember the favor of God upon you" - the favor (niʿmah) is the same as 2:40/2:47 ("remember My favor"). The ṢALĀH is given as a FAVOR to a PREFERRED community. The ṢALĀH is a PRIVILEGE, not a burden.

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + ZAKAH) in C1 and C4. The formula is STABLE. C4 adds THREE more conditions (believe in messengers, support them, lend God a good loan) - the ṢALĀH is condition #1 of FIVE.
- C2 (2:43 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah and bow with those who bow"): C2 added the community-dimension (bow with those who bow). C4 adds the FIVE-CONDITION structure. The ṢALĀH's context grows: C1 (minimal religion) -> C2 (community) -> C3 (social ethics) -> C4 (five conditions with promise).
- C3 (2:83 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah; then you turned away"): C3 showed the turning-away (tawalla) from the ṢALĀH. C4 shows the CONSEQUENCE: "because of their breaking of the covenant, We cursed them" (5:13). The turning-away (C3) leads to the curse (C4).
- B3 (33:43 - exodus darkness -> light): 5:16's "brings them out of darkness into light" is the SAME exodus-clause. The Messenger (Muhammad) now offers the SAME exodus that God offered through Moses. The ṢALĀH-covenant's exodus is REPEATED for the People of the Book through the final Messenger.
- B7 (9:84 - "do not s-l-l on them"): the withholding of ṢALĀH from the disbelievers (9:84) matches the curse on the covenant-breakers (5:13). The ṢALĀH is withheld from those who break the covenant.
- A6 (17:18 - "whoever wants the immediate"): the worldly-preference (2:86 in C3) returns in C4's "whoever disbelieves after that has strayed from the right path" (5:12). The ṢALĀH-covenant's opposite is straying (ḍalla).

FINDING: C4 shows the ṢALĀH as condition #1 of a FIVE-condition covenant: establish ṢALĀH -> give zakah -> believe in messengers -> support them -> lend God a good loan. The PROMISE: "I will remove your evil deeds and admit you to gardens." The ṢALĀH is the GATE-condition: establish it first, then the rest follows. And the breaking of the covenant leads to the CURSE (la'nah, 5:13) - the same curse as 2:161 and 33:57. The ṢALĀH-covenant is now offered through the Messenger (Muhammad) to the People of the Book (5:15-16): "Our Messenger has come to you... a light and a clear Book... brings them out of darkness into light." The ṢALĀH-covenant's exodus is REPEATED. And the maṣīr-word (5:18) serves as the destination of the disbeliever - the same maṣīr as A4/A13/2:126.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The FIVE-condition covenant (5:12) - the ṢALĀH is condition #1 of five. HOLD for the final doc's covenant-table. (ii) The EXODUS-clause (5:16 "brings them out of darkness into light") - the SAME as B3 (33:43). The ṢALĀH-covenant offers exodus through the Messenger. (iii) The CURSE (5:13 la'nah) on covenant-breakers - the SAME as 2:161 and 33:57. The la'nah vs ṢALĀH polarity. (iv) The partial-belief (5:14 "they forgot a portion") - the ṢALĀH is part of a package that can be partially forgotten. (v) The favor (5:20 "remember the favor of God") - the ṢALĀH is given as a favor to a preferred community.

VERDICT: studied - see C5 (already studied ahead: 2:125 - Abraham's musallā) - next canonical is C6 (14:37 - Ibrahim: make this city secure).

## C6 - 14:37 - rabbana li-yuqimu-s-salata

ARABIC (flow): 14:35 "Abraham said: My Lord, make this city secure, and keep me and my sons away from worshipping idols" -> 14:36 "My Lord, indeed they have led many people astray; so whoever follows me is of me, and whoever disobeys me - You are Forgiving, Merciful" -> 14:37 "Our Lord, I have settled some of my offspring in a valley without cultivation, near Your Sacred House, Our Lord, THAT THEY MAY ESTABLISH THE SALAH. So make hearts of people incline toward them, and provide them with fruits, that they may be grateful" -> 14:38 "Our Lord, You know what we conceal and what we reveal; and nothing is hidden from God on earth or in heaven" -> 14:39 "Praise be to God who gave me, in old age, Isma'il and Isaac; indeed my Lord is the Hearer of supplication" -> 14:40 "My Lord, make me an ESTABLISHER OF THE SALAH, and from my offspring. Our Lord, and accept my supplication" -> 14:41 "Our Lord, forgive me and my parents and the believers on the Day the reckoning is established" -> 14:42-44 "do not think God is unaware of what the wrongdoers do... warn the people of the Day when the punishment comes."

WORD FORM: li-yuqīmū al-ṣalāta (that they may establish the ṣalāh) - the purpose-clause (li- = "that/so that"). Abraham settles his offspring near the Sacred House SO THAT they may ESTABLISH THE SALAH. The ṢALĀH is the PURPOSE of the settlement. And 14:40: muqīma al-ṣalāti (an establisher of the ṣalāh) - Abraham prays to be made an ESTABLISHER of the ṢALĀH.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah Ibrahim (14). The verse sits in Abraham's prayer (14:35-41): keep us away from idols, settle offspring near the Sacred House, THAT THEY MAY ESTABLISH THE SALAH, make hearts incline toward them, provide fruits, accept my supplication, forgive us on the Day of reckoning.

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE PURPOSE-CLAUSE (li-yuqīmū al-ṣalāta): "so that they may ESTABLISH THE SALAH" - the ṢALĀH is the REASON for the settlement. Abraham does not settle his family near the House for safety or wealth; he settles them there SO THAT they may ESTABLISH THE SALAH. The standing-site (MUSSALLA, C5) exists FOR the standing-act (ṢALĀH). The site and the act are paired: the site is the place where the act is established.
- THE ESTABLISH-VERB (yuqīmū) AGAIN: "establish the ṣalāh" - the same yuqīmū as C1 (98:5) and C2 (2:43). The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED (made-stand, set-up), not performed as a momentary act. The purpose-clause (li-yuqīmū) shows that the ṢALĀH is the GOAL, not the means: the settlement is the means, the ṢALĀH is the goal.
- ABRAHAM'S PERSONAL PRAYER (14:40 "make me an establisher of the ṢALĀH"): Abraham prays to be made an ESTABLISHER (muqīm) of the ṢALĀH - not just to establish it himself, but to be MADE an establisher (passive-ish: "make me"). The ṢALĀH-establishment is a GIFT from God, not a self-achieved act. Abraham asks God to MAKE him an establisher - the same as the divine ṢALĀH (B-cluster): God MAKES the standing. The human ṢALĀH and the divine ṢALĀH are both God-MADE.
- THE IDOL-ALTERNATIVE (14:35 "keep me and my sons away from worshipping idols"): the ṢALĀH is the ALTERNATIVE to idol-worship. The standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) replaces the standing-toward-idols. The ṢALĀH is the anti-idolatry act: instead of standing before idols, you stand before God.
- THE GIFT-TO-OFFSPRING (14:39 "Praise be to God who gave me, in old age, Isma'il and Isaac"): the ṢALĀH is given to Abraham's offspring. Isma'il (near the House) and Isaac (elsewhere) - both receive the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is a GIFT to the offspring, not just to Abraham.
- THE DAY OF RECKONING (14:41 "forgive me and my parents and the believers on the Day the reckoning is established"): the ṢALĀH is for the Day of Reckoning (yawm yuqāmu al-ḥisāb). The standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) is preparation for the Day of Standing (Qiyāmah). The same q-w-m root: qiyām (standing) in ṢALĀH and qiyāmah (Resurrection/standing) in the Day of Reckoning.
- 14:38 "You know what we conceal and what we reveal" - the ṢALĀH is known to God (concealed and revealed). The standing is seen by God - the same as A17's "his Lord was EVER SEEING of him" (84:15).

PARALLELS:
- C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site): the SITE where the ṢALĀH is established. Abraham settles his offspring near the House (the MUSSALLA) SO THAT they may ESTABLISH THE SALAH. The site (C5) exists for the act (C6). The standing-site and the standing-act are paired.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME establish-verb (yuqīmū). The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED in both C1 (command) and C6 (purpose). The command and the purpose are the same: establish the ṢALĀH.
- C2 (2:43 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah and bow with those who bow"): C2 added the community-dimension (bow with those who bow). C6 adds the SETTLEMENT-dimension (settle near the House so that they may establish the ṢALĀH). The ṢALĀH has a site (C5/C6) and a community (C2).
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (established) IS. Abraham prays to be made an ESTABLISHER (14:40) - the human stander who makes the standing-state permanent.
- B2 (2:157 - ṢALAWĀT upon the patient): the divine ṢALAWĀT is granted to those who ESTABLISH the ṢALĀH (C6). The human ṢALĀH (established by Abraham's offspring) invites the divine ṢALAWĀT.
- B5 (9:103 - "your ṣalāh is their tranquility"): the Prophet's ṢALĀH (established) brings tranquility (sakan). Abraham's prayer (14:37) asks for hearts to incline toward his offspring - the ṢALĀH-establishment brings hearts together.
- 87:18-19 (A19 - "this is in the first Scrolls, the Scrolls of Abraham and Moses"): the ṢALĀH (established) is ALREADY WRITTEN in the Scrolls of Abraham. Abraham's prayer (14:37) is the PROTOTYPE of the ṢALĀH-establishment.

FINDING: C6 shows the ṢALĀH as the PURPOSE of settlement: Abraham settles his offspring near the Sacred House SO THAT they may ESTABLISH THE SALAH. The site (MUSSALLA, C5) exists FOR the act (ṢALĀH). And Abraham prays to be made an ESTABLISHER of the ṢALĀH (14:40) - the ṢALĀH-establishment is a GIFT from God. The ṢALĀH is the alternative to idol-worship (14:35: keep us away from idols -> establish the ṢALĀH). And the ṢALĀH is for the Day of Reckoning (14:41: "on the Day the reckoning is established") - the standing-toward-God is preparation for the Day of Standing (Qiyāmah). The same q-w-m root: qiyām (standing in ṢALĀH) and qiyāmah (Resurrection/standing).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The PURPOSE-clause (li-yuqīmū) - the ṢALĀH is the GOAL, not the means. The settlement is the means; the ṢALĀH is the goal. HOLD for the final doc's purpose-table. (ii) The ESTABLISHER-prayer (14:40 muqīma al-ṣalāti) - Abraham prays to be made an establisher. The ṢALĀH-establishment is a GIFT. HOLD for B-cluster (the divine ṢALĀH is also God-given). (iii) The IDOL-ALTERNATIVE (14:35) - the ṢALĀH replaces idol-worship. The standing-toward-God replaces the standing-toward-idols. (iv) The DAY-OF-RECKONING orientation (14:41) - the ṢALĀH is for the Day of Standing (Qiyāmah). The q-w-m root links ṢALĀH and Qiyāmah. (v) The GIFT-to-offspring (14:39) - the ṢALĀH is given to Abraham's offspring. The ṢALĀH is a HERITAGE, not just a personal act.

VERDICT: studied - see C7 (14:40 - Ibrahim: make me an establisher of the ṣalāh) next.
Wait - 14:40 is already covered in this entry (C6). The order file has C6 = 14:37 and C7 = 14:40. So C7 is a SEPARATE verse to study. Let me note: C6 covered 14:37 (with 14:40 referenced); C7 will study 14:40 in full. See C7 (14:40) next.

## C7 - 14:40 - rabbij'alni muqima-s-salati wa min dhurriyyati

ARABIC (flow): 14:38 "Our Lord, You know what we conceal and what we reveal; and nothing is hidden from God on earth or in heaven" -> 14:39 "Praise be to God who gave me, in old age, Isma'il and Isaac; indeed my Lord is the Hearer of supplication" -> **14:40 "My Lord, make me an ESTABLISHER OF THE SALAH, and from my offspring. Our Lord, and accept my supplication"** -> 14:41 "Our Lord, forgive me and my parents and the believers on the Day the reckoning is established" -> 14:42 "do not think God is unaware of what the wrongdoers do; He only delays them for a Day when eyes will stare."

WORD FORM: muqīma al-ṣalāti (an establisher of the ṣalāh) - the noun muqīm (from q-w-m, to stand/upright) + ṢALĀH (from ṣ-l-w, standing-state) = "one who makes the ṢALĀH stand." Abraham prays to be MADE an establisher (ijʿalnī = "make me"). The ṢALĀH-establishment is a GIFT from God, not a self-achieved act.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah Ibrahim (14). The verse sits in Abraham's prayer (14:35-41): keep us away from idols, settle offspring near the Sacred House so they may establish the ṢALĀH, praise God for Isma'il and Isaac, make me an establisher of the ṢALĀH (and from my offspring), accept my supplication, forgive us on the Day of reckoning.

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE GIFT-PRAYER (ijʿalnī muqīma al-ṣalāti): Abraham asks God to MAKE him an establisher - the ṢALĀH-establishment is a GIFT from God, not a self-achieved act. The same as the divine ṢALĀH (B-cluster): God MAKES the standing (B3 "He s-l-l on you"; B4 "God and His angels s-l-l on the Prophet"). The human ṢALĀH (established) and the divine ṢALĀH (granted) are both God-MADE. The ṢALĀH-chain's human side is also God-dependent.
- THE HEREDITARY-DIMENSION (wa min dhurriyyatī): "and from my offspring" - the ṢALĀH-establishment is HEREDITARY: Abraham prays for his offspring to also be establishers. The ṢALĀH is a HERITAGE, passed from father to offspring. This matches 2:132 ("do not die except as submitters") - the covenant is hereditary. The ṢALĀH is not just a personal act; it is a FAMILY TRADITION.
- THE ACCEPTANCE-PRAYER (rabbana wa taqabbal duʿāʾ): "Our Lord, and accept my supplication" - Abraham asks God to ACCEPT his prayer. The ṢALĀH-establishment is a prayer that needs to be accepted by God. The divine ṢALĀH (B-cluster) is the acceptance: God's ṢALĀH is His response to the human ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH-chain: human request (establish me) -> divine acceptance (ṢALĀH granted).
- THE GIFT-TO-OFFSPRING (14:39 "Praise be to God who gave me, in old age, Isma'il and Isaac"): the ṢALĀH is given to Abraham's offspring. Isma'il (near the House) and Isaac (elsewhere) - both receive the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is a GIFT to the offspring, not just to Abraham. The ṢALĀH is a family heritage.
- THE DAY-OF-RECKONING (14:41 "forgive me and my parents and the believers on the Day the reckoning is established"): the ṢALĀH is for the Day of Reckoning (yawm yuqāmu al-ḥisāb). The standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) is preparation for the Day of Standing (Qiyāmah). The same q-w-m root: qiyām (standing in ṢALĀH) and qiyāmah (Resurrection/standing). The ṢALĀH is practice for the Qiyāmah.
- 14:38 "You know what we conceal and what we reveal" - the ṢALĀH is known to God (concealed and revealed). The standing is seen by God - the same as A17's "his Lord was EVER SEEING of him" (84:15). The ṢALĀH is a VISIBLE standing, seen by God.

PARALLELS:
- C6 (14:37 - "so that they may establish the ṢALĀH"): C6 showed the ṢALĀH as the PURPOSE of settlement; C7 shows Abraham's personal PRAYER to be made an establisher. The purpose (C6) and the prayer (C7) are paired: Abraham settles his offspring so they may establish the ṢALĀH (C6), and he prays to be made an establisher himself (C7).
- C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site): the SITE where the ṢALĀH is established. Abraham prays to be made an establisher (C7) of the ṢALĀH at the MUSSALLA (C5).
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the COMMAND to establish the ṢALĀH (C1) is what Abraham PRAYS to be able to do (C7). The command and the prayer are paired: God commands (C1), Abraham prays for ability (C7).
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what Abraham prays to be made an establisher of. The ṢALĀH-establishment is the permanent form of fa-ṣallā.
- B2 (2:157 - ṢALAWĀT upon the patient): the divine ṢALAWĀT is granted to those who ESTABLISH the ṢALĀH (C7). The human ṢALĀH (established by Abraham's offspring) invites the divine ṢALAWĀT.
- B4 (33:56 - ṢALLŪ ʿALAYHI on the Prophet): the human command to stand-toward the Prophet is the EARTHLY counterpart of Abraham's prayer to be made an establisher. Both are human acts of standing-toward.
- 87:18-19 (A19 - "this is in the first Scrolls, the Scrolls of Abraham and Moses"): Abraham's prayer (14:40) is ALREADY WRITTEN in the Scrolls. The ṢALĀH-establishment is covenant-tradition.

FINDING: C7 shows the ṢALĀH-establishment as a GIFT-PRAYER: Abraham asks God to MAKE him an establisher (ijʿalnī muqīma al-ṣalāti) and extends the prayer to his offspring (wa min dhurriyyatī). The ṢALĀH is a HERITAGE (passed from father to offspring) and a GIFT (God makes the establisher). The ṢALĀH is for the Day of Reckoning (14:41: "on the Day the reckoning is established") - the standing-toward-God is preparation for the Day of Standing (Qiyāmah). The same q-w-m root links ṢALĀH (standing) and Qiyāmah (Resurrection/standing). The ṢALĀH is practice for the Qiyāmah.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The GIFT-PRAYER (ijʿalnī muqīma al-ṣalāti) - the ṢALĀH-establishment is a gift from God, not a self-achieved act. HOLD for B-cluster (the divine ṢALĀH is also God-given). (ii) The HEREDITARY-DIMENSION (wa min dhurriyyatī) - the ṢALĀH is passed from father to offspring. HOLD for C-cluster (the covenant is hereditary: 2:132 "do not die except as submitters"). (iii) The DAY-OF-RECKONING orientation (14:41) - the ṢALĀH is for the Day of Standing (Qiyāmah). The q-w-m root links ṢALĀH and Qiyāmah. (iv) The ESTABLISHER-prayer and the ESTABLISH-command (C1/C2/C3/C4) are paired: God commands (C1), Abraham prays for ability (C7). The command and the prayer are the same ṢALĀH.

VERDICT: studied - see C8 (19:31 - Jesus: "He made me blessed wherever I am, and He enjoined on me the ṣalāh") next.

## C8 - 19:31 - wa awsani bi-s-salati wa-z-zakati ma dumtu hayyan

ARABIC (flow): 19:27 "She brought him to her people, carrying him. They said: O Mary, you have done an amazing thing" -> 19:28-29 "O sister of Aaron, your father was not an evil man, nor was your mother unchaste. So she pointed to him. They said: how can we speak to one who is a child in the cradle?" -> 19:30 "He said: I am a servant of God. He gave me the Book and made me a prophet" -> 19:31 "And He made me BLESSED wherever I am, and He ENJOINED on me the SALAH and the ZAKAH as long as I live" -> 19:32 "and dutiful to my mother, and He did not make me arrogant, wretched" -> 19:33 "And peace (SALAM) be upon me the day I was born, and the day I die, and the day I am raised alive" -> 19:34 "That is Jesus son of Mary - a word of truth about which they dispute" -> 19:35-36 "It is not for God to take a son... worship Him; this is a straight path" -> 19:37-40 "the parties differed... How well they will hear and see on the Day they come to Us... to Us they will return."

WORD FORM: awṣānī bi-l-ṣalāti wa-l-zakāti (He enjoined on me the ṣalāh and the zakah) + mā dumtu ḥayyan (as long as I live) - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1/C2/C3/C4) now in JESUS's mouth: God ENJOINED (awṣā = commanded/enjoined) on Jesus the ṢALĀH and the ZAKAH. The ṢALĀH is a PROPHETIC enjoinment, not just a communal command.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah Maryam (19). The verse sits in Jesus's speech from the cradle (19:30-33): "I am a servant of God. He gave me the Book and made me a prophet. He made me blessed wherever I am. He enjoined on me the ṢALĀH and the ZAKAH as long as I live. And dutiful to my mother. And He did not make me arrogant, wretched. And peace (SALAM) be upon me the day I was born, and the day I die, and the day I am raised alive."

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE PROPHETIC ENJOINMENT (awṣā): "He ENJOINED on me the ṢALĀH and the ZAKAH" - God enjoined (awṣā = commanded, entrusted, bequeathed) the ṢALĀH on Jesus. The ṢALĀH is not just a communal command (C1-C4); it is a PROPHETIC enjoinment - part of the prophetic office. The prophet's job includes establishing the ṢALĀH. The same verb (awṣā) used for Abraham's instruction to his sons (2:132 "Abraham instructed his sons" - wa-waṣṣā bihā Ibrāhīmu banīhi) - the ṢALĀH is INSTRUCTED to prophets and their offspring.
- "AS LONG AS I LIVE" (mā dumtu ḥayyan): the ṢALĀH is a LIFETIME enjoinment, not a momentary act. The ṢALĀH is for as long as one lives - the same as C7's "do not die except as submitters" (2:132) and C6's "make me an establisher" (14:40). The ṢALĀH is a LIFETIME commitment, not a one-time act. The "as long as I live" (mā dumtu ḥayyan) is the strongest "state not act" proof on the prophetic-side: the ṢALĀH is not an event but a LIFELONG STATE.
- 19:30 "I am a servant of God. He gave me the Book and made me a prophet" - the ṢALĀH is given ALONGSIDE the Book and prophethood. The ṢALĀH is part of the PROPHETIC office: the prophet's job includes establishing the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH-chain has a prophetic agent (2:129, 2:151).
- 19:32 "dutiful to my mother, and He did not make me arrogant, wretched" - the ṢALĀH is paired with DUTIFULNESS to parents (the same social ethics as C3's covenant: "be good to parents"). The ṢALĀH and filial piety are paired. The ṢALĀH is not isolated; it is part of a comprehensive ethical package.
- 19:33 "peace (SALAM) be upon me the day I was born, and the day I die, and the day I am raised alive" - the SALĀM-greeting (B3/B4's tracked reward-side greeting) is Jesus's own greeting. The ṢALĀH (enjoined on Jesus) produces SALĀM (peace) - the same ṢALĀH -> SALĀM chain as B3 (33:43-44: divine ṢALĀH -> SALĀM-greeting). The ṢALĀH produces peace.
- 19:36 "worship Him; this is a straight path" - the ṢALĀH is the straight path (ṣirāṭ mustaqīm) - the same as A9's covenant (36:61 "this is a straight path") and C1's "religion of uprightness (dīn al-qayyimah)."
- 19:40 "to Us they will return" - the RETURN-grammar (rajaʿa) - the ṢALĀH is for those who return to God. The same return-word as B2 (2:156 "we belong to God and to Him we return") and C2 (2:46 "they will return to Him").

PARALLELS:
- C7 (14:40 - "make me an establisher of the ṢALĀH"): Abraham prays to be made an establisher; Jesus is ENJOINED the ṢALĀH. The prayer (C7) and the enjoinment (C8) are paired: Abraham asks, Jesus is given. Both are God-MADE.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + ZAKAH) in C1 and C8. The formula is STABLE across prophets: Muhammad (C1), Jesus (C8), Abraham (C5/C6/C7). The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is the UNIVERSAL prophetic formula.
- C3 (2:83 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah; then you turned away"): C3 showed the turning-away from the ṢALĀH; C8 shows Jesus ENJOINED the ṢALĀH (he did not turn away). The ṢALĀH is the prophetic commitment; turning away is the covenant-breaking.
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (enjoined on Jesus) IS. The ṢALĀH is the LIFETIME form of fa-ṣallā.
- B3 (33:43-44 - divine ṢALĀH -> SALĀM-greeting): Jesus's SALAM-greeting (19:33) is the OUTCOME of the ṢALĀH (enjoined on him). The ṢALĀH produces SALĀM - the same chain.
- 19:36 "this is a straight path" - the ṢALĀH is the straight path (ṣirāṭ mustaqīm). The same as A9 (36:61) and C1 (dīn al-qayyimah).

FINDING: C8 shows the ṢALĀH as a PROPHETIC ENJOINMENT: God enjoined (awṣā) on Jesus the ṢALĀH and the ZAKAH "as long as I live" (mā dumtu ḥayyan). The ṢALĀH is a LIFETIME enjoinment, not a momentary act. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1's two-pillar formula) is given to Jesus - the SAME formula across prophets (Muhammad C1, Jesus C8, Abraham C5/C6/C7). The ṢALĀH is given ALONGSIDE the Book and prophethood (19:30) - the ṢALĀH is part of the PROPHETIC office. And the ṢALĀH produces SALĀM (19:33) - the same chain as B3 (divine ṢALĀH -> SALĀM-greeting). The ṢALĀH is the straight path (19:36).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The PROPHETIC ENJOINMENT (awṣā bi-l-ṣalāti) - the ṢALĀH is part of the prophetic office. HOLD for the final doc's prophetic-table. (ii) The LIFETIME enjoinment (mā dumtu ḥayyan) - the ṢALĀH is for as long as one lives. HOLD as the strongest "state not act" proof on the prophetic side. (iii) The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is UNIVERSAL across prophets (C1, C8, and C5/C6/C7) - the same formula given to Muhammad, Jesus, and Abraham. (iv) The SALĀM-greeting (19:33) as the OUTCOME of the ṢALĀH - the ṢALĀH -> SALĀM chain (B3/B4/C8). (v) The straight path (ṣirāṭ mustaqīm, 19:36) - the ṢALĀH is the straight path.

VERDICT: studied - see C9 (19:55 - Isma'il: "He enjoined on his family the ṣalāh and the zakah") next.

## C9 - 19:55 - wa kana ya'muru ahlahu bi-s-salati wa-z-zakati

ARABIC (flow): 19:54 "And mention in the Book Isma'il; indeed he was true to his promise, and he was a Messenger, a Prophet" -> 19:55 "And he used to COMMAND his family the SALAH and the ZAKAH, and he was pleasing to his Lord" -> 19:56 "And mention in the Book Idris; indeed he was a man of truth, a Prophet" -> 19:57 "And We raised him to a high place" -> 19:58 "Those are the ones upon whom God bestowed favor from among the prophets, from the offspring of Adam, and from those We carried with Noah, and from the offspring of Abraham and Israel, and from those We guided and chose; when the signs of the Most-Merciful are recited to them, they fall down in prostration and weeping" -> 19:59 "But there succeeded after them a successor who LOST THE SALAH and followed desires; so they will meet with ruin" -> 19:60 "Except those who repent and believe and do righteous deeds; those will enter the Garden and will not be wronged in anything."

WORD FORM: yaʾmuru (he commands/enjoins) + ahlahu (his family) + bi-l-ṣalāti (the ṢALĀH) + wa-l-zakāti (and the zakah) - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1/C8) now with a COMMAND-verb (yaʾmuru = he commands). Isma'il COMMANDED (yaʾmuru) his family the ṢALĀH and the ZAKAH, and he was PLEASING (marḍiyyan) to his Lord.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah Maryam (19). The verse sits in the prophetic-chain passage (19:54-60): Isma'il (commanded his family the ṢALĀH and was pleasing to God), Idris (raised to a high place), the prophets (when God's signs are recited to them, they prostrate and weep), then THE SUCCESSOR who LOST THE SALAH and followed desires (19:59), then the repentance-exception (19:60).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE COMMAND-VERB (yaʾmuru): "he used to COMMAND his family the ṢALĀH and the ZAKAH" - the verb yaʾmuru (from a-m-r, to command/order) is DIFFERENT from awṣā (enjoined/bequeathed, C8 Jesus) and awṣā (instructed, 2:132 Abraham). yaʾmuru = he commands/orders - a more ACTIVE, DIRECT command. Isma'il didn't just "enjoin" (awṣā, bequeath) the ṢALĀH; he COMMANDED (yaʾmuru) his family to do it. The ṢALĀH is COMMANDED by the prophet to his family.
- "AND HE WAS PLEASING TO HIS LORD" (wa kāna ʿinda rabbihī marḍiyyan): the ṢALĀH-command is what made Isma'il PLEASING (marḍiyyan) to God. The ṢALĀH is the act of pleasing God. The same honor-economy as B-cluster: ṢALĀH = honor (pleasing to God); la'nah = dishonor (curse). Isma'il is marḍiyy (pleasing) because he commanded the ṢALĀH.
- 19:59 "But there succeeded after them a successor who LOST THE SALAH and followed desires" - THE SURAH-19 BRIDGE (A7's golden bridge) RETURNS: "they LOST THE SALAH and followed desires" -> kneeling at fire -> ṣalāh-loss leads to fire-delivery. The ṢALĀH is what is LOST by the successor; the successor who LOST it followed desires (shahawāt) instead. The SAME shahawāt as A7 (19:59 "they followed the desires" -> 19:70 ṣalāh-loss -> fire-delivery). The ṢALĀH-loss is the ENTRY-POINT to the fire-delivery.
- 19:58 "when the signs of the Most-Merciful are recited to them, they fall down in prostration and weeping" - the ṢALĀH-producing response to the recitation: prostration (sujūd) and weeping. The ṢALĀH is the response to the recitation of God's signs. The same recitation-response as B3 (33:41-42 "remember God with much remembrance, glorify Him morning and evening" -> divine ṢALĀH).
- 19:60 "Except those who repent and believe and do righteous deeds; those will enter the Garden" - the ṢALĀH-loser can REPENT and be saved. The ṢALĀH-loss is not final; repentance (tawba) restores it. The ṢALĀH is a RESTORABLE state.
- 19:54 "he was true to his promise" (ṣādiq al-waʿd) - Isma'il's quality is truthfulness to his promise. The ṢALĀH-command is part of his truthful character. The ṢALĀH is the act of a truthful person.

PARALLELS:
- C8 (19:31 - Jesus: "He enjoined on me the ṢALĀH and the ZAKAH as long as I live"): C8 showed the PROPHETIC ENJOINMENT (awṣā); C9 shows the FAMILY COMMAND (yaʾmuru - commanding his family). The prophet enjoins (C8) and commands (C9) the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is both a prophetic office and a family command.
- A7 (19:70 - صِلِيًّا "most scorched"): the Surah-19 bridge returns: 19:59 (lost ṢALĀH -> followed desires) -> 19:68 (kneeling at fire) -> 19:70 (ṣalāh-loss -> fire-delivery). The ṢALĀH-loss leads to fire-delivery. C9 is the VERSE that introduces the bridge (19:59).
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + ZAKAH) in C1 and C9. The formula is UNIVERSAL across prophets: Muhammad (C1), Jesus (C8), Isma'il (C9), Abraham (C5/C6/C7).
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what Isma'il COMMANDED his family to do. The ṢALĀH-command is the command to stand-toward.
- B3 (33:43 - divine ṢALĀH -> SALĀM-greeting): 19:33 (Jesus's SALAM-greeting) and 19:58 (prophets prostrate and weep) - the ṢALĀH produces SALĀM and prostration. The same chain.
- A1 (4:10 - swallow fire in belly): the fire-delivery for ṢALĀH-loss begins with the same "swallow fire" idiom. The ṢALĀH-loss -> fire-delivery chain starts with A1 and passes through A7 and C9.

FINDING: C9 gives the ṢALĀH as a FAMILY COMMAND: Isma'il COMMANDED (yaʾmuru) his family the ṢALĀH and the ZAKAH, and he was PLEASING (marḍiyyan) to God. The ṢALĀH is commanded by the prophet to his family - a family-level command, not just a communal one. And the ṢALĀH-loss (19:59: "they LOST THE SALAH and followed desires") is the ENTRY-POINT to the fire-delivery (A7's golden bridge: 19:59 -> 19:68 -> 19:70). The ṢALĀH is what is LOST by the successor; the successor who LOST it followed desires instead. The ṢALĀH is RESTORABLE: repentance (tawba) saves the ṢALĀH-loser (19:60). The ṢALĀH is the act that, when LOST, leads to fire-delivery; when KEPT, leads to SALĀM (19:33) and being pleasing to God (19:55).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The SURAH-19 BRIDGE (A7): 19:59 (lost ṢALĀH -> followed desires) -> 19:68 (kneeling at fire) -> 19:70 (ṣalāh-loss -> fire-delivery). C9 is the VERSE that introduces the bridge. HOLD as the STRONGEST noun-verb bridge in the corpus. (ii) The COMMAND-VERB (yaʾmuru) vs ENJOIN-VERB (awṣā): Isma'il COMMANDED (yaʾmuru) his family; Jesus was ENJOINED (awṣā). The ṢALĀH is both commanded and enjoined - two levels of prophetic authority. (iii) The PLEASING-QUALITY (marḍiyyan): Isma'il was pleasing to God BECAUSE he commanded the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the act of pleasing God. (iv) The RESTORATION (19:60): the ṢALĀH-loser can repent and be saved. The ṢALĀH is restorable.

VERDICT: studied - see C10 (20:14 - Moses: "establish the ṣalāh for My remembrance") next.

## C10 - 20:14 - fa-'budni wa-aqim al-salata li-dhikri

ARABIC (flow): 20:9 "And has the story of Moses come to you?" -> 20:10 "When he saw a FIRE and said to his family: stay, I perceive a fire; perhaps I can bring you a brand from it, or find GUIDANCE at the fire" -> 20:11 "And when he came to it, he was called: O Moses" -> 20:12 "Indeed, I am your Lord, so remove your sandals; you are in the sacred valley of Tuwa" -> 20:13 "And I have chosen you, so listen to what is revealed" -> **20:14 "Indeed, I am God; there is no god but Me. So WORSHIP Me and ESTABLISH THE SALAH FOR MY REMEMBRANCE"** -> 20:15 "Indeed, the Hour is coming - I almost conceal it - so that every soul may be recompensed for what it strives" -> 20:16 "So let not one who does not believe in it and follows his desire divert you from it, lest you perish" -> 20:17-19 "And what is that in your right hand, O Moses? He said: it is my staff..."

WORD FORM: aqim al-ṣalāta (establish the ṣalāh) + li-dhikrī (for My remembrance) - the ONLY verse where the ṢALĀH is explicitly said to be "FOR" (li-) remembrance (dhikr). The ṢALĀH's PURPOSE is remembrance: establish the ṢALĀH so that God is remembered.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah Ta-Ha (20). The verse sits in the Moses-at-the-burning-bush passage (20:9-19): Moses saw a fire, was drawn to it for guidance, was called by God, told to remove his sandals (sacred ground), chosen, and commanded: "worship Me and establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance." Then 20:15-16: the Hour is coming; do not let those who follow desire divert you from it.

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE PURPOSE-CLAUSE (li-dhikrī): "establish the ṢALĀH FOR MY REMEMBRANCE" - the ṢALĀH's PURPOSE is remembrance (dhikr). The ṢALĀH is the VEHICLE of remembrance: establish the ṢALĀH so that God is remembered. The ṢALĀH is not just a standing-state; it is a REMEMBRANCE-act. The standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) is the act of remembering God. This is the STRONGEST "state not act" proof on the command-side: the ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED for a PURPOSE (remembrance), not performed as a momentary act.
- THE FIRE-AS-GUIDANCE (20:10 "perhaps I can find GUIDANCE at the fire"): Moses went to the fire for GUIDANCE. The fire (which on the A-side was DELIVERY) here is the source of GUIDANCE and the ṢALĀH-command. The fire-lexicon's deepest polarity: fire as delivery (A) vs fire as guidance (C10). The same fire, two directions: the fire that DELIVERS (A-cluster) vs the fire that GUIDES (C10). The burning bush is the fire that gives the ṢALĀH-command.
- THE SACRED-GROUND (20:12 "remove your sandals; you are in the sacred valley of Tuwa"): the ṢALĀH is given in a SACRED PLACE. The standing-site (MUSSALLA, C5) is sacred ground. The ṢALĀH requires a sacred space - the same as C5's "purify My House" (the standing-site is gated on purity).
- THE CHOSEN-ONE (20:13 "I have chosen you, so listen to what is revealed"): the ṢALĀH is given to a CHOSEN one. The ṢALĀH is a GIFT to the chosen (same as C7's "make me an establisher" - the ṢALĀH-establishment is God-given).
- THE HOUR-ORIENTATION (20:15 "the Hour is coming... so that every soul may be recompensed for what it strives"): the ṢALĀH is for the Day of Recompense (yawm al-jazāʾ). The standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) is preparation for the Day of Recompense. The same as C7's "on the Day the reckoning is established" (14:41).
- THE DESIRE-WARNING (20:16 "let not one who does not believe in it and follows his desire divert you from it, lest you perish"): the ṢALĀH is to be protected from those who follow desire (shahwa). The same shahwa (desire) that the fire-people followed (A7 19:59 "they followed the desires" -> fire-delivery). The ṢALĀH is the OPPOSITE of following desire: standing-toward-God vs following desire. The ṢALĀH and desire are the two directions: God-ward vs self-ward.

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME establish-verb (aqīmū). The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED (made-stand) in both C1 and C10. The command is the same: establish the ṢALĀH.
- C7 (14:40 - "make me an establisher of the ṢALĀH"): Abraham prays to be made an establisher; Moses is COMMANDED to establish the ṢALĀH. The prayer (C7) and the command (C10) are paired: Abraham asks, Moses is commanded. Both are God-MADE.
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (established for remembrance) IS. The ṢALĀH is the ESTABLISHED form of fa-ṣallā, and its PURPOSE is remembrance (dhikr).
- B3 (33:43 - "remember God with much remembrance... He s-l-l on you"): the human dhikr (33:41 "remember God with much remembrance") precedes the divine ṢALĀH. C10's "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance" closes the loop: the ṢALĀH IS the remembrance. The human dhikr invites the divine ṢALĀH; the ṢALĀH IS the dhikr.
- A7 (19:59 - "they followed the desires" -> fire-delivery): the desire-warning (20:16) matches A7's desire-following. The ṢALĀH is the OPPOSITE of following desire. The same desire (shahwa) that leads to fire-delivery is what the ṢALĀH protects against.
- 87:18-19 (A19 - "this is in the first Scrolls, the Scrolls of Abraham and Moses"): the ṢALĀH (established for remembrance) is ALREADY WRITTEN in the Scrolls of Moses. The ṢALĀH is covenant-tradition.

FINDING: C10 gives the ṢALĀH's PURPOSE: "establish the ṢALĀH FOR MY REMEMBRANCE" (li-dhikrī). The ṢALĀH is the VEHICLE of remembrance - establish the ṢALĀH so that God is remembered. The ṢALĀH is not just a standing-state; it is a REMEMBRANCE-act. And the fire-lexicon's deepest polarity is revealed: the fire that DELIVERS (A-cluster) vs the fire that GUIDES (C10's burning bush gives the ṢALĀH-command). The same fire, two directions: delivery vs guidance. The ṢALĀH is given in a SACRED PLACE (the sacred valley of Tuwa) to a CHOSEN one (20:13). And the ṢALĀH is to be protected from those who follow desire (20:16) - the same desire (shahwa) that leads to fire-delivery (A7). The ṢALĀH and desire are the two directions: God-ward vs self-ward.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The PURPOSE-clause (li-dhikrī) - the ṢALĀH is FOR remembrance. The ṢALĀH is the vehicle of dhikr. HOLD for the final doc's purpose-table. (ii) The FIRE-AS-GUIDANCE (20:10) - the fire-lexicon's deepest polarity: fire as delivery (A) vs fire as guidance (C10). The same fire, two directions. HOLD for the final doc's fire-lexicon table. (iii) The SACRED-GROUND (20:12) - the ṢALĀH requires a sacred space. HOLD for C5's purification-covenant. (iv) The DESIRE-WARNING (20:16) - the ṢALĀH protects against desire. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check: does the ṢALĀH transform desire?). (v) The HOUR-ORIENTATION (20:15) - the ṢALĀH is for the Day of Recompense. HOLD for the q-w-m root's link between ṢALĀH and Qiyāmah.

VERDICT: studied - see C11 (11:87 - Shu'aib: the mockers' view of ṣalāh) next.

## C11 - 11:87 - a-salatuka ta'muruka an natruka ma ya'budu aba'u-na

ARABIC (flow): 11:84 "And to Madyan their brother Shu'aib. He said: O my people, worship God; you have no god other than Him. And do not diminish the measure and the scale. I see you in prosperity, and I fear for you the punishment of an encompassing Day" -> 11:85 "O my people, give full measure and weight in justice, and do not deprive people of their things, and do not cause corruption in the earth" -> 11:86 "What remains with God is better for you if you are believers; and I am not a guardian over you" -> **11:87 "They said: O Shu'aib, DOES YOUR SALAH COMMAND YOU that we should leave what our fathers worship, or that we should do with our wealth what we please? Indeed you are the forbearing, the rightly-guided"** -> 11:88 "He said: O my people, have you considered: if I am upon clear evidence from my Lord, and He has provided me with good provision from Him, and I do not want to oppose you in what I forbid you; I only want reform as much as I can; and my success is only from God; in Him I trust, and to Him I turn" -> 11:89-90 "And O my people, let not my opposition cause you to be struck by the like of what struck the people of Noah, or the people of Hud, or the people of Salih... seek forgiveness from your Lord, then repent to Him; indeed my Lord is Merciful, Loving" -> 11:91-93 "They said: O Shu'aib, we do not understand much of what you say... He said: O my people, work according to your position; I am working... watch; indeed I am with you a watcher" -> 11:94-95 "when Our command came, We saved Shu'aib and those who believed with him by a mercy from Us, and the Cry seized those who wronged."

WORD FORM: a-ṣalātuka taʾmuruka (Does YOUR ṣalāh command you?) - the mockers' question: they personify the ṢALĀH as a COMMANDING-FORCE that tells Shu'aib to (1) reject idol-worship and (2) restrict wealth-usage. The ṢALĀH is seen by the mockers as an active commander, not a passive ritual.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah Hud (11). The verse sits in the Shu'aib-passage (11:84-95): Shu'aib commands his people to worship God alone, give full measure and weight, not cause corruption; the mockers attribute his reform to his ṢALĀH; Shu'aib responds that he only wants reform; the mockers are destroyed by the Cry.

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE MOCKERS' TESTIMONY: "Does YOUR ṢALĀH command you?" - the mockers personify the ṢALĀH as a COMMANDING-FORCE. They are WRONG about the ṢALĀH's content (Shu'aib's reform is from God, not from the ṢALĀH alone), but they are RIGHT about the ṢALĀH's NATURE: the ṢALĀH IS a commanding-force. The mockers inadvertently testify to the ṢALĀH's power: it commands, it reforms, it restricts. This is the STRONGEST testimony from OUTSIDE the believers: even the mockers see the ṢALĀH as an active power.
- THE TWO CHARGES: (1) "that we should leave what our fathers worship" (reject idolatry), (2) "that we should do with our wealth what we please" (stop cheating in trade). The ṢALĀH commands BOTH: reject idolatry AND restrict wealth-usage. The ṢALĀH is the act that reforms BOTH worship AND commerce. The ṢALĀH is a COMPREHENSIVE reformer.
- 11:88 Shu'aib's response: "I only want reform as much as I can; and my success is only from God" - Shu'aib does NOT deny the ṢALĀH's commanding power; he redirects it: the ṢALĀH commands reform, and reform is from God. The ṢALĀH and reform are aligned: the ṢALĀH commands what God commands.
- 11:90 "seek forgiveness from your Lord, then repent to Him" - the ṢALĀH is the vehicle of repentance: seek forgiveness (istighfār) and repent (tawba). The same as 9:103 (charity purifies -> s-l-l -> tranquility) and 19:60 (repent and believe and do righteous deeds -> enter the Garden). The ṢALĀH is the act of repentance.
- 11:94 "We saved Shu'aib and those who believed with him by a mercy from Us" - the ṢALĀH-believers are saved by mercy (raḥmah) - the same as B2 (ṢALAWĀT + raḥmah) and B3 (ṢALĀH + raḥmah). The ṢALĀH and mercy are paired.
- 11:84 "I fear for you the punishment of an encompassing Day" - the ṢALĀH is the protection against punishment. The ṢALĀH-people are saved; the ṢALĀH-breakers are punished. The ṢALĀH is the divider.

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the mockers see the ṢALĀH as commanding reform (reject idolatry + restrict wealth). C1's "establish the ṢALĀH" is the command that the mockers attribute to Shu'aib's ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH commands what C1 commands.
- C3 (2:83 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah; then you turned away"): the mockers TURN AWAY from the ṢALĀH (they do not obey it). The same turning-away (tawalla) as C3's covenant-breakers. The ṢALĀH is the act that is TURNED AWAY from.
- C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site): the ṢALĀH is the act performed at the MUSSALLA. The mockers' question "does your ṢALĀH command you?" shows that the ṢALĀH is seen as an active power, not a passive ritual.
- A9 (36:60-61 - "this is a straight path"): the mockers see the ṢALĀH as commanding them to leave their fathers' worship. The ṢALĀH is the straight path that opposes the fathers' (idolatrous) path.
- 9:99 (B6 - "ṣalawāt of the Messenger"): the mockers' "does your ṢALĀH command you?" shows that the ṢALĀH is seen as a commanding-force. The ṢALĀH-as-currency (B6) is the ṢALĀH-as-command (C11). The same ṢALĀH, two views: the mockers see it as a command; the believers see it as a currency/mercy.
- 19:59 (A7 - "they lost the ṢALĀH and followed desires"): the mockers FOLLOW DESIRES (they want to do with their wealth what they please). The ṢALĀH commands against desire. The same desire (shahwa) that leads to fire-delivery (A7) is what the ṢALĀH commands against.

FINDING: C11 gives the MOCKERS' VIEW of the ṢALĀH: "Does YOUR ṢALĀH command you?" The mockers personify the ṢALĀH as a COMMANDING-FORCE that tells Shu'aib to (1) reject idolatry and (2) restrict wealth-usage. The mockers are WRONG about the content (Shu'aib's reform is from God), but RIGHT about the NATURE: the ṢALĀH IS a commanding-force. This is the STRONGEST testimony from OUTSIDE the believers: even the mockers see the ṢALĀH as an active power that reforms worship AND commerce. The ṢALĀH is the act that commands against desire (shahwa) - the same desire that leads to fire-delivery (A7 19:59). The ṢALĀH commands what God commands (11:88 "my success is only from God"). And the ṢALĀH-believers are saved by mercy (11:94) - the same as B2 (ṢALAWĀT + raḥmah).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The MOCKERS' VIEW of the ṢALĀH - the ṢALĀH is seen as a COMMANDING-FORCE by outsiders. HOLD for the final doc: the ṢALĀH is not just a ritual; it is a power that commands reform. (ii) The ṢALĀH commands BOTH worship-reform (reject idols) AND commerce-reform (stop cheating). The ṢALĀH is comprehensive: it reforms BOTH the sacred and the secular. (iii) The ṢALĀH vs desire (shahwa) - the ṢALĀH commands against desire. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check: does the ṢALĀH transform desire?). (iv) The ṢALĀH-believers saved by mercy (11:94) - the same as B2 (ṢALAWĀT + raḥmah). (v) The ṢALĀH as REPENTANCE-vehicle (11:90 "seek forgiveness, then repent") - the ṢALĀH is the act of repentance.

VERDICT: studied - see C12 (6:162 - "my prayer and my sacrifice and my life and my death are for God") next.

## C12 - 6:162 - inna salati wa nuski wa mahyay wa mamati li-llahi

ARABIC (flow): 6:159 "Indeed those who divided their religion and became sects - you have nothing to do with them. Their affair is only with God; then He will inform them of what they used to do" -> 6:160 "Whoever comes with a good deed, for him is ten times the like of it; and whoever comes with an evil deed, he is recompensed only with the like of it; and they will not be wronged" -> 6:161 "Say: Indeed my Lord has guided me to a straight path, an upright religion (din qayyim), the religion of Abraham, upright (hanif), and he was not of the polytheists" -> 6:162 "Say: Indeed my SALAH and my sacrifice and my life and my death are for God, the Lord of the worlds" -> 6:163 "No partner has He; and with that I have been commanded, and I am the first of the submitters (muslimin)" -> 6:164 "Say: Shall I seek other than God as a Lord, while He is the Lord of all things? And no soul earns except against itself, and no bearer of burden shall bear the burden of another; then to your Lord is your return, and He will inform you of that in which you used to differ" -> 6:165 "And He is the One who made you successors on earth and raised some of you above others in degrees, to test you in what He has given you. Indeed your Lord is swift in punishment, and indeed He is Forgiving, Merciful."

WORD FORM: ṢALĀTĪ (my ṣalāh) + nuskī (my sacrifice) + maḥyāy (my life) + mamātī (my death) - four dedications, all "for God" (li-llāhi). The ṢALĀH is the FIRST of four: ṢALĀH + sacrifice + life + death. All four are FOR God.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-An'am (6). The verse sits in the closing-passage of Surah 6: the upright religion of Abraham (6:161), the four-fold dedication (6:162), the first of the submitters (6:163), the no-transfer-of-burden (6:164), and the test of succession (6:165).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE FOUR-FOLD DEDICATION (6:162): "my ṢALĀH and my sacrifice and my life and my death are for God." The ṢALĀH is the FIRST of four dedications. The ṢALĀH is not an isolated act; it is the LEADING act of a FOUR-fold dedication: standing (ṢALĀH) + offering (nusk) + living (maḥyā) + dying (mamāt). All four are FOR God. The ṢALĀH is the STANDING-dedication; the nusk is the OFFERING-dedication; the maḥyā is the LIVING-dedication; the mamāt is the DYING-dedication. The ṢALĀH spans the entire life and extends to death.
- THE ṢALĀH + SACRIFICE PAIR (ṢALĀH + nusk): the standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) and the offering-to-God (nusk) are the two acts of dedication. The ṢALĀH is the standing-dedication; the nusk is the offering-dedication. Both are FOR God. This is the same pairing as B4 (33:56: ṢALLŪ ʿALAYHI + SALLIMŪ TASLĪMAN - standing-toward + surrendering). The ṢALĀH is always paired with a complementary act.
- "MY LIFE AND MY DEATH ARE FOR GOD" (maḥyāy wa mamātī li-llāhi): the ṢALĀH is not just a ritual act; it is a LIFETIME dedication. The ṢALĀH spans the entire life (maḥyā) and extends to death (mamāt). The same as C8's "as long as I live" (mā dumtu ḥayyan) - the ṢALĀH is for as long as one lives, AND beyond (death is also for God). The ṢALĀH is not a momentary act; it is a LIFETIME state that extends to death.
- 6:161 "an upright religion (dīn qayyim), the religion of Abraham, upright (ḥanīf)": the ṢALĀH is the upright religion (dīn qayyim) of Abraham. The SAME as C1 (98:5 "dīn al-qayyimah") and C5 (2:135 "the religion of Abraham, upright/ḥanīf"). The ṢALĀH is the ABRAHAMIC standing: the same formula across prophets.
- 6:163 "I am the first of the submitters (muslimīn)": the ṢALĀH is the act of the FIRST submitter. The ṢALĀH is the foundational act of Islam: the first submitter's first act is the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the BEGINNING of submission. The same as C7's "do not die except as submitters" (2:132) - the ṢALĀH is the lifelong act of the submitter.
- 6:159 "those who divided their religion and became sects - you have nothing to do with them": the ṢALĀH is the UNDIVIDED religion. The ṢALĀH unifies; sectarianism divides. The ṢALĀH is the anti-sectarian act.
- 6:165 "He made you successors on earth and raised some of you above others in degrees, to test you in what He has given you": the ṢALĀH is given to TEST. The ṢALĀH is a TEST: the same as B1's "the birds know their ṣalāh" (the ṢALĀH is innate) and C1's "establish the ṢALĀH" (the ṢALĀH is commanded). The ṢALĀH is both innate (B1) and commanded (C1) and a test (6:165).

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED (C1) and DEDICATED (C12). The command (C1) and the dedication (C12) are paired: "establish the ṢALĀH" (C1) = "my ṢALĀH is for God" (C12). The ṢALĀH is both commanded and dedicated.
- C8 (19:31 - "He enjoined on me the ṢALĀH and the ZAKAH as long as I live"): C8 showed the LIFETIME enjoinment (mā dumtu ḥayyan); C12 shows the LIFETIME dedication (maḥyāy wa mamātī). The ṢALĀH is for as long as one lives (C8) AND beyond (death is also for God, C12).
- C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site): the SITE where the ṢALĀH is dedicated. The MUSSALLA (standing-site) is where the four-fold dedication (ṢALĀH + nusk + maḥyā + mamāt) takes place.
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (dedicated) IS. The ṢALĀH is the DEDICATED form of fa-ṣallā: "my ṢALĀH is for God" = "I stand-toward God."
- B4 (33:56 - ṢALLŪ ʿALAYHI + SALLIMŪ TASLĪMAN): the s-l-w + s-l-m pairing (standing-toward + surrendering) appears in C12's "my ṢALĀH and my sacrifice and my life and my death are for God" - the ṢALĀH is the standing-dedication, and the submission (s-l-m) is the surrender-dedication. Both are FOR God.
- 6:161 "the religion of Abraham, upright (ḥanīf)": the ṢALĀH is the upright religion of Abraham. The same as 2:135 (C5) and 98:5 (C1). The ṢALĀH is the ABRAHAMIC standing.

FINDING: C12 gives the ṢALĀH as a FOUR-FOLD DEDICATION: "my ṢALĀH and my sacrifice and my life and my death are for God." The ṢALĀH is the FIRST of four dedications: standing (ṢALĀH) + offering (nusk) + living (maḥyā) + dying (mamāt). All four are FOR God. The ṢALĀH spans the entire life and extends to death. The ṢALĀH is the LIFETIME dedication: "as long as I live" (C8) AND "my death is for God" (C12). The ṢALĀH is not a momentary act; it is a LIFETIME state that extends beyond death. And the ṢALĀH is the UNDIVIDED religion (6:159: "those who divided their religion... you have nothing to do with them") - the ṢALĀH unifies; sectarianism divides. The ṢALĀH is the anti-sectarian act. And the ṢALĀH is a TEST (6:165: "to test you in what He has given you") - the same as B1 (innate) and C1 (commanded). The ṢALĀH is both innate, commanded, and a test.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The FOUR-FOLD DEDICATION (6:162) - ṢALĀH + nusk + maḥyā + mamāt, all for God. HOLD for the final doc's dedication-table. (ii) The ṢALĀH + SACRIFICE pair (ṢALĀH + nusk) - the standing-dedication and the offering-dedication. HOLD for the final doc's act-table. (iii) The LIFETIME-AND-BEYOND dedication (maḥyā + mamāt) - the ṢALĀH spans life and death. HOLD for the final doc's time-table. (iv) The UNDIVIDED religion (6:159) - the ṢALĀH unifies; sectarianism divides. HOLD for the final doc's unity-table. (v) The TEST (6:165) - the ṢALĀH is a test. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check).

VERDICT: studied - see C13 (3:39 - angels call Zakariyya while he is in the mihrab) next.
