## C13 - 3:39 - fanādathu al-malā'ikatu wa huwa qā'imun yuṣallī fī al-miḥrābi

ARABIC (flow): 3:35 "When the wife of Imran said: My Lord, I have vowed to You what is in my womb, dedicated (muḥarraran), so accept from me; You are the Hearing, the Knowing" -> 3:36 "And when she gave birth, she said: My Lord, I have given birth to a female... and I have named her Mary, and I seek refuge for her and her offspring in You from the accursed Satan" -> 3:37 "So her Lord accepted her with good acceptance, and made her grow in a good growth, and put her in the care of Zakariyya. Whenever Zakariyya entered the miḥrāb to visit her, he found provision with her. He said: O Mary, where did this come from? She said: it is from God; God provides for whom He wills without account" -> 3:38 "Thereupon Zakariyya called upon his Lord: My Lord, grant me from Yourself a good offspring; You are the Hearer of supplication" -> 3:39 "So the angels called him while he was STANDING, PRAYING IN THE MIHRAB: God gives you good news of Yahya, confirming a word from God, and a leader, and a chaste one, and a prophet from among the righteous" -> 3:40-41 "He said: My Lord, how can I have a boy... He said: your sign is that you shall not speak to people for three days except by gesture, and remember your Lord much, and glorify Him in the evening and the morning" -> 3:42-43 "O Mary, God has chosen you and purified you... be devout to your Lord and prostrate and bow with those who bow" -> 3:44 "That is from the news of the unseen which We reveal to you."

WORD FORM: qāʾimun (standing, active participle of q-w-m) + yuṣallī (s-l-w verb, present tense) + fī al-miḥrābi (in the miḥrāb/sanctuary) - Zakariyya was STANDING (qāʾim) and PRAYING (yuṣallī) in the MIḤRĀB (sanctuary/prayer-niche). The ṢALĀH is performed WHILE STANDING (qāʾim) in a SANCTUARY (miḥrāb).

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah Al Imran (3). The verse sits in the Mary-Zakariyya passage (3:35-44): Mary's mother vowed her to God; Zakariyya was her guardian; whenever he entered the miḥrāb he found provision with her; he prayed for offspring; the angels called him WHILE he was standing-praying in the miḥrāb and announced Yahya.

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE STANDING-POSTURE (qāʾimun yuṣallī): Zakariyya was STANDING (qāʾim) and PRAYING (yuṣallī) - the ṢALĀH is performed WHILE STANDING. The qāʾim (standing) and the yuṣallī (s-l-w verb) are paired: the STANDING-POSTURE is the ṢALĀH's physical form. The ṢALĀH is not a seated or reclining act; it is a STANDING act. The same q-w-m root (to stand/upright) that gives yuqīmū (establish, C1/C2/C4/C6/C10) and qiyāmah (Resurrection) gives qāʾim (standing). The ṢALĀH is a STANDING-state.
- THE MIḤRĀB (sanctuary/prayer-niche): the place where Zakariyya stood-praying. The miḥrāb is a STANDING-SITE - the same as MUSSALLA (C5, the standing-site at the House) and MAQĀM (C5, Abraham's standing-place). The miḥrāb is the place where the ṢALĀH is performed while STANDING. The standing-site (miḥrāb) and the standing-act (ṢALĀH) are paired.
- THE DIVINE RESPONSE DURING ṢALĀH: the angels called him WHILE he was standing-praying. The divine response (the angelic announcement of Yahya) came DURING the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the moment of divine communication: the angels speak to Zakariyya WHILE he is in ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is not just a human act; it is a moment of DIVINE RESPONSE.
- THE PROVISION-AT-THE-SITE (3:37 "whenever Zakariyya entered the miḥrāb, he found provision with her"): the miḥrāb is a place of PROVISION (rizq) - the ṢALĀH-site is a place where God provides. The standing-site (miḥrāb) is a place of divine provision - the same as C6's "provide them with fruits" (14:37). The ṢALĀH-site is a place of sustenance.
- 3:41 "remember your Lord much, and glorify Him in the evening and the morning" - the ṢALĀH is paired with dhikr (remembrance) and tasbīḥ (glorification) - the same pairing as B3 (33:41-42 "remember God with much remembrance; glorify Him morning and evening" -> divine ṢALĀH). The ṢALĀH is the dhikr-vehicle (C10's "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance").
- 3:43 "O Mary, be devout to your Lord and prostrate and bow with those who bow" - the ṢALĀH's posture: prostration (sujūd) and bowing (rukūʿ) with those who bow. The same community-dimension as C2 ("bow with those who bow"). The ṢALĀH is performed WITH others - it has a community-dimension.
- 3:35 "I have vowed to You what is in my womb, dedicated (muḥarraran)" - the ṢALĀH is connected to VOWS and DEDICATION (taḥrīr). The ṢALĀH is a dedicated act - the same as C12's "my ṢALĀH is for God" (6:162). The ṢALĀH is dedicated to God.

PARALLELS:
- C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site): the miḥrāb is the SAME site-lexeme: a place of standing. The MUSSALLA (at the House) and the MIḤRĀB (in the sanctuary) are both standing-sites. The root's SITE-pole has THREE lexemes: ṢALAWĀT (their houses, 22:40), MUSSALLA (our spot, 2:125), and MIḤRĀB (the sanctuary, 3:39). All three are standing-sites.
- C6 (14:37 - "so that they may establish the ṢALĀH"): the ESTABLISH-verb (yuqīmū) appears in C6 (purpose) and C13 (Zakariyya standing-praying). The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED (made-stand) and performed WHILE STANDING (qāʾim). The command (establish) and the posture (standing) are paired.
- C10 (20:14 - "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance"): the ṢALĀH is FOR remembrance (dhikr). C13's pairing of ṢALĀH + dhikr (3:41) confirms: the ṢALĀH is the vehicle of remembrance.
- B3 (33:43 - divine ṢALĀH -> SALĀM-greeting): the angels speak to Zakariyya WHILE he is in ṢALĀH - the divine response comes DURING the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the moment of divine communication.
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what Zakariyya was doing in the miḥrāb. The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-the-Name in a sanctuary.
- 3:43 "prostrate and bow with those who bow" - the community-dimension (C2: "bow with those who bow"). The ṢALĀH is performed WITH others.

FINDING: C13 gives the ṢALĀH's PHYSICAL FORM: STANDING (qāʾim) in a SANCTUARY (miḥrāb). The ṢALĀH is performed WHILE STANDING - the qāʾim (standing) and the yuṣallī (s-l-w verb) are paired. The miḥrāb is the THIRD site-lexeme (after ṢALAWĀT and MUSSALLA) - the sanctuary where the standing-act is performed. And the divine response (the angelic announcement of Yahya) came DURING the ṢALĀH - the ṢALĀH is the moment of divine communication. The ṢALĀH is not just a human act; it is a moment of DIVINE RESPONSE. The ṢALĀH-site is a place of PROVISION (rizq) - God provides at the standing-site. And the ṢALĀH is paired with dhikr (remembrance) and tasbīḥ (glorification) - the ṢALĀH is the vehicle of remembrance.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The STANDING-POSTURE (qāʾim yuṣallī) - the ṢALĀH is performed WHILE STANDING. The qāʾim (standing) and the yuṣallī (s-l-w verb) are paired. HOLD for the final doc's posture-table. (ii) The MIḤRĀB (sanctuary) as the THIRD site-lexeme - the standing-site has THREE lexemes: ṢALAWĀT (their houses, 22:40), MUSSALLA (our spot, 2:125), MIḤRĀB (the sanctuary, 3:39). HOLD for the final doc's site-table. (iii) The DIVINE RESPONSE DURING ṢALĀH (3:39) - the angels speak WHILE Zakariyya is in ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the moment of divine communication. HOLD for B-cluster (the divine ṢALĀH is the response). (iv) The PROVISION-AT-THE-SITE (3:37) - the ṢALĀH-site is a place of divine provision. HOLD for the final doc's provision-table. (v) The VOW-AND-DEDICATION (3:35 muḥarraran) - the ṢALĀH is a dedicated act. HOLD for C12's "my ṢALĀH is for God."

VERDICT: studied - see C14 (17:78 - times of the ṣalāh) next.

## C14 - 17:78 - aqim al-salata li-duluki-sh-shamsi ila ghasaqi al-layli

ARABIC (flow): 17:76 "And indeed they were about to provoke you from the land to drive you out of it; and then they would not have remained after you except for a little" -> 17:77 "The sunnah of those We sent before you of Our messengers; and you will not find any change in Our sunnah" -> **17:78 "ESTABLISH THE SALAH at the DECLINE OF THE SUN until the DARKNESS OF THE NIGHT, and the QURAN OF DAWN; indeed the Quran of dawn is WITNESSED"** -> 17:79 "And from the night, PRAY WITH IT as a supererogatory (nāfilah) for you; perhaps your Lord will raise you to a PRAISED STATION (maqām maḥmūd)" -> 17:80 "And say: My Lord, make me enter with a truthful entry, and make me exit with a truthful exit, and grant me from Yourself a supporting authority" -> 17:81 "And say: Truth has come and falsehood has vanished; indeed falsehood is ever vanishing" -> 17:82 "And We send down of the Quran that which is a healing and a mercy for the believers, and it increases the wrongdoers in nothing but loss."

WORD FORM: aqim al-ṣalāta (establish the ṣalāh) + li-dulūki al-shamsi (at the decline of the sun) + ilā ghasaqi al-layli (until the darkness of the night) + wa qurʾāna al-fajri (and the Quran of dawn) + inna qurʾāna al-fajri kāna mashhūdan (indeed the Quran of dawn is witnessed). The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED (aqim) at SPECIFIC TIMES: from midday to night, plus dawn.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-Isra' (17). The verse sits in the Prophet's passage (17:76-82): the sunnah of the prophets (17:77), the ṢALĀH-times (17:78), the night-prayer (17:79), the truthful entry/exit (17:80), truth vs falsehood (17:81), the Quran as healing and mercy (17:82).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE TIMED-BOUNDARIES: "ESTABLISH THE SALAH at the decline of the sun until the darkness of the night, and the Quran of dawn." The ṢALĀH is not a momentary act; it is a TIME-BOUND standing-state with SPECIFIC boundaries: (1) from the decline of the sun (dulūk al-shams = after noon) to the darkness of the night (ghasaq al-layl), and (2) the Quran of dawn (qurʾān al-fajr = the dawn prayer). The ṢALĀH spans TIME - it is a standing-state that occupies a duration, not a moment.
- "INDEED THE QURAN OF DAWN IS WITNESSED" (inna qurʾāna al-fajri kāna mashhūdan): the dawn ṢALĀH is WITNESSED (mashhūd). By whom? By the angels (B3's angelic pole: 33:43 "He s-l-l on you, and His angels"). The dawn ṢALĀH is witnessed by the angels - the same angels who co-agent the divine ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is a WITNESSED act: the standing-toward-God is seen by the angelic witnesses.
- 17:79 "from the night, pray with it as a supererogatory (nāfilah) for you; perhaps your Lord will raise you to a PRAISED STATION (maqām maḥmūd)": the NIGHT-PRAYER (tahajjud) is a supererogatory (nāfilah) act that may raise the Prophet to a PRAISED STATION (maqām maḥmūd). The maqām (standing-place) - the same word as C5's "maqām of Abraham" (2:125). The Prophet's standing-place (maqām) is EARNED through night-prayer. The ṢALĀH can be VOLUNTARY (nāfilah) and still earns a standing-place.
- 17:77 "the sunnah of those We sent before you of Our messengers; and you will not find any change in Our sunnah": the ṢALĀH-times are part of the SUNNAH (established practice) of the prophets. The ṢALĀH is prophetic tradition, not novelty. The same as C8 (19:31 "He enjoined on me the ṢALĀH") and C9 (19:55 "he commanded his family the ṢALĀH") - the ṢALĀH is prophetic tradition across all prophets.
- 17:82 "the Quran is a healing and a mercy for the believers": the ṢALĀH is paired with the Quran (the ṢALĀH-times include "the Quran of dawn"). The ṢALĀH and the Quran are paired: the ṢALĀH is the standing; the Quran is the recitation. The ṢALĀH is the vehicle of the Quran's recitation.
- 17:80 "make me enter with a truthful entry, and make me exit with a truthful exit": the ṢALĀH (established at specific times) is the truthful entry/exit. The ṢALĀH is the gateway to truth.

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME establish-verb (aqīmū/aqim). The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED (made-stand) at specific times. The command is the same: establish the ṢALĀH.
- C10 (20:14 - "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance"): the ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED (aqim) in both C10 (for remembrance) and C14 (at specific times). The establish-verb is the same; the purpose differs: remembrance (C10) vs timed-standing (C14). The ṢALĀH is both a remembrance-act and a time-bound standing.
- C13 (3:39 - Zakariyya standing-praying in the miḥrāb): the STANDING-POSTURE (qāʾim) is the physical form; the TIMED-BOUNDARIES (C14) are the temporal form. The ṢALĀH has both a physical form (standing) and a temporal form (timed). Both are "state" features, not "act" features.
- B3 (33:43 - divine ṢALĀH with angels): the dawn ṢALĀH is WITNESSED (mashhūd) by the angels. The angelic pole serves the ṢALĀH as witnesses. The same angels who co-agent the divine ṢALĀH (B3) witness the human ṢALĀH (C14).
- C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site): the SITE where the timed ṢALĀH is performed. The MUSSALLA is the place; the timed boundaries (C14) are the time. The ṢALĀH has both a SITE and a TIME.
- 17:79 (maqām maḥmūd - praised station): the standing-place (maqām) is EARNED through night-prayer. The ṢALĀH can be VOLUNTARY (nāfilah) and still earns a standing-place. The same as B5 (9:103 "your ṣalāh is their tranquility") - the ṢALĀH earns something for the stander.

FINDING: C14 gives the ṢALĀH's TEMPORAL FORM: "establish the ṢALĀH at the decline of the sun until the darkness of the night, and the Quran of dawn." The ṢALĀH is TIME-BOUND: it spans from midday to night, plus dawn. The ṢALĀH is not a momentary act; it is a standing-state that occupies a DURATION. And the dawn ṢALĀH is WITNESSED (mashhūd) by the angels - the same angels who co-agent the divine ṢALĀH (B3). The ṢALĀH has both a physical form (standing, C13) and a temporal form (timed, C14). Both are "state" features. The night-prayer (nāfilah) can EARN a praised station (maqām maḥmūd) - the ṢALĀH is not just commanded; it is VOLUNTARY and still earns. The ṢALĀH-times are part of the SUNNAH (established practice) of the prophets (17:77).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The TIMED-BOUNDARIES (17:78) - the ṢALĀH spans from midday to night, plus dawn. HOLD for the final doc's time-table. (ii) The WITNESSED-QUALITY (mashhūd) - the dawn ṢALĀH is witnessed by the angels. HOLD for the angelic-table (B3, C14). (iii) The NIGHT-PRAYER (nāfilah) earning a praised station (maqām maḥmūd) - the ṢALĀH can be voluntary and still earns. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check). (iv) The SUNNAH (17:77) - the ṢALĀH-times are prophetic tradition. HOLD for the final doc's tradition-table. (v) The PHYSICAL + TEMPORAL forms paired: standing (C13) + timed (C14) = the ṢALĀH is a standing-state with both a body and a time.

VERDICT: studied - see C15 (17:110 - "do not make your voice too loud in prayer nor too low") next.

## C15 - 17:110 - wa la tajhar bi-salatika wa la tukhafit biha

ARABIC (flow): 17:105 "And with truth We sent it down, and with truth it descended; and We sent you only as a bringer of good news and a warner" -> 17:106 "And a Quran which We have divided, that you may recite it to the people at intervals; and We have sent it down in portions" -> 17:107 "Say: Believe in it or do not believe. Indeed those who were given knowledge before it, when it is recited to them, they fall down on their chins in prostration" -> 17:108 "And they say: Glory be to our Lord! Indeed the promise of our Lord has been fulfilled" -> 17:109 "And they fall down on their chins weeping, and it increases them in humility" -> **17:110 "Say: Call upon God, or call upon the Most-Merciful; whichever you call upon, to Him belong the best names. And do NOT make your voice TOO LOUD in your SALAH, nor too quiet, but seek a path BETWEEN them"** -> 17:111 "And say: Praise be to God who has not taken a son, and has no partner in dominion, and has no protector out of weakness; and magnify Him with all magnificence."

WORD FORM: lā tajhar bi-ṣalātika (do not make your voice too loud in your ṣalāh) + wa lā tukhāfit bihā (nor too quiet with it) + wa-btaghi bayna dhālika sabīlan (but seek a path between them). The ṢALĀH has a VOICE-DIMENSION: the voice should be neither too loud nor too quiet, but in between.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-Isra' (17). The verse sits in the Quran-recitation passage (17:105-111): the Quran is sent down in portions for recitation (17:106); when it is recited, the knowledgeable fall down in prostration and weeping (17:107-109); the ṢALĀH's voice should be moderated (17:110); and God is magnified with all magnificence (17:111).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE VOICE-DIMENSION (17:110): "do not make your voice TOO LOUD in your ṢALĀH, nor too quiet, but seek a path BETWEEN them." The ṢALĀH has a VOICE: it is not a silent meditation; it is a VOICED act. And the voice is to be MODERATED: not too loud (jahr) nor too quiet (khafāt). The ṢALĀH is a MODERATED VOICE - a voice that is heard but not shouted, audible but not deafening. The ṢALĀH is a voice that STANDS between silence and shouting.
- THE QURAN-RECITATION (17:106-107): "a Quran which We have divided, that you may recite it to the people at intervals... when it is recited to them, they fall down on their chins in prostration." The ṢALĀH includes Quran-recitation (the "Quran of dawn" in C14's 17:78). The recitation causes PROSTRATION (sujūd) and WEEPING - the ṢALĀH is the vehicle of the Quran's recitation, and the recitation produces the ṢALĀH's physical response (prostration).
- THE MAGNIFICATION (17:111 "magnify Him with all magnificence"): the ṢALĀH includes magnification (takbīr) of God. The ṢALĀH is the act of magnifying God - the same as 74:3 ("magnify your Lord") in A15's standing-prescription. The ṢALĀH is the magnification-act.
- THE PROSTRATION-AND-WEEPING (17:107-109): "they fall down on their chins in prostration... and they fall down on their chins weeping, and it increases them in humility." The ṢALĀH's physical response to the Quran-recitation is PROSTRATION (sujūd) and WEEPING. The ṢALĀH produces humility (khushūʿ) - the same khushūʿ as 23:2 ("the humble in their ṢALĀH") and 2:45 ("heavy except upon the humble").
- 17:108 "Glory be to our Lord! Indeed the promise of our Lord has been fulfilled" - the ṢALĀH includes GLORIFICATION (tasbīḥ) of God. The ṢALĀH is the glorification-act - the same as B1's birds (each knows its ṢALĀH and its TASBĪH) and B3 (33:42 "glorify Him morning and evening").

PARALLELS:
- C14 (17:78 - "the Quran of dawn is witnessed"): C14 gave the ṢALĀH's TIMED boundaries; C15 gives the ṢALĀH's VOICE dimension. The ṢALĀH has both a temporal form (timed, C14) and a vocal form (moderated voice, C15). Both are "state" features.
- C13 (3:39 - Zakariyya standing-praying in the miḥrāb): the STANDING-POSTURE (qāʾim) is the physical form; the VOICE (C15) is the vocal form. The ṢALĀH has body (standing), time (timed), and voice (moderated). All three are "state" features.
- B1 (24:41 - the birds know their ṢALĀH and their TASBĪH): the bird's ṢALĀH (innate standing-posture) and TASBĪH (innate glorification) are the same as the human ṢALĀH (standing + glorification). The ṢALĀH and TASBĪH are paired: the standing and the glorification.
- B3 (33:41-42 - "remember God with much remembrance; glorify Him morning and evening"): the human dhikr and tasbīḥ precede the divine ṢALĀH. C15's ṢALĀH includes glorification (tasbīḥ) and magnification (takbīr) - the ṢALĀH IS the dhikr and tasbīḥ.
- A15 (74:1-7 - the standing-prescription: "arise and warn, magnify your Lord, purify your garments, flee filth, do not out-count favors, be patient"): the standing-prescription's "magnify your Lord" (wa-rabbaka fa-kabbir) is the same as C15's "magnify Him with all magnificence" (kabbirhu takbīran). The ṢALĀH includes magnification.
- 23:1-2 (not yet studied, E-cluster): "successful are the believers, those who are humble in their ṢALĀH" - the ṢALĀH produces humility (khushūʿ). C15's prostration-and-weeping (17:107-109) is the physical form of khushūʿ.

FINDING: C15 gives the ṢALĀH's VOCAL FORM: "do not make your voice TOO LOUD in your ṢALĀH, nor too quiet, but seek a path BETWEEN them." The ṢALĀH has a VOICE: it is not silent; it is a MODERATED voice - heard but not shouted. The ṢALĀH is a voice that STANDS between silence and shouting. And the ṢALĀH includes: (1) Quran-recitation (17:106-107 - the recitation causes prostration and weeping), (2) glorification (tasbīḥ, 17:108), (3) magnification (takbīr, 17:111). The ṢALĀH is a VOICED, RECITED, GLORIFYING, MAGNIFYING act - all of which are "state" features, not momentary "acts." The ṢALĀH's voice is the sound of the standing-state.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The VOICE-DIMENSION (17:110) - the ṢALĀH is not silent; it is a moderated voice. HOLD for the final doc's voice-table. (ii) The QURAN-RECITATION (17:106-107) - the ṢALĀH includes Quran-recitation, which causes prostration and weeping. HOLD for D-cluster (the Quran's own recitation in ṢALĀH). (iii) The MAGNIFICATION (17:111) - the ṢALĀH includes takbīr. HOLD for the final doc's act-table. (iv) The PROSTRATION-AND-WEEPING (17:107-109) - the ṢALĀH's physical response to recitation. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check: does the ṢALĀH transform the reciter?). (v) The ṢALĀH now has THREE forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), and vocal (moderated voice, C15). All three are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see C16 (2:238 - guard the middle prayer) next.

## C16 - 2:238 - hafizu ala-s-salawati wa-s-salati-l-wusta wa qumu li-llahi qanitin

ARABIC (flow): 2:236 "There is no blame on you if you divorce women before you have touched them or assigned them a dowry; but provide for them - the wealthy according to his means and the poor according to his means - a provision in a right manner; a duty on the doers of good" -> 2:237 "And if you divorce them before you have touched them, but you have assigned them a dowry, then half of what you assigned, unless they forgive or the one in whose hand is the marriage contract forgives; and that you forgive is closer to righteousness; and do not forget the favor between you; God is Seeing of what you do" -> **2:238 "GUARD the SALAWAT and the MIDDLE SALAH; and STAND for God in devout obedience (qānitīn)"** -> 2:239 "And if you fear, [pray] on foot or riding; and when you are safe, remember God as He taught you what you did not know" -> 2:240-242 "And those who are taken in death and leave wives... a bequest... Thus God makes clear His signs to you, that you may understand."

WORD FORM: ḥāfiẓū (guard/preserve) + ʿalā al-ṣalawāti (the ṣalawat, plural) + wa-l-ṣalāti al-wusṭā (and the middle ṣalāh, singular) + wa qūmū (and stand) + li-llāhi (for God) + qānitīn (in devout obedience). Three elements: (1) guard the ṣalawat (plural), (2) guard the middle ṣalāh (singular), (3) stand for God in devout obedience.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Baqara (2). The verse sits in the divorce/widow provisions (2:236-242): divorce rules, then GUARD THE SALAWAT AND THE MIDDLE SALAH, then "if you fear, pray on foot or riding." The ṢALĀH is the anchor in the midst of social upheaval (divorce, widowhood).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE GUARD-VERB (ḥāfiẓū): "GUARD the ṢALAWAT" - the ṢALĀH is to be GUARDED, not just performed. The ṢALĀH is a TRUST to be preserved - the same "guard" (ḥāfiẓ) as the recorders (82:10-12 hāfiẓīn, A16). The ṢALĀH is guarded like a record is guarded. The standing-state is a TRUST that must be kept.
- THE MIDDLE SALAH (al-ṣalāti al-wusṭā): "and the MIDDLE ṢALĀH" - there is a MIDDLE one among the ṢALAWAT. The ṢALAWAT are multiple (timed, C14: from midday to night plus dawn = multiple prayers); the MIDDLE one is singled out for special guarding. The ṢALĀH has a RANKING: middle vs others. The ṢALĀH is not a single undifferentiated act; it has a HIERARCHY within the timed prayers.
- THE STANDING-IN-DEVOUT-OBEDIENCE (qūmū li-llāhi qānitīn): "stand for God in devout obedience" - the qiyām (standing) is paired with qunūt (devout obedience/submission). The ṢALĀH is a STANDING in DEVOUT OBEDIENCE. The qānit (devoutly obedient) is the one who stands for God. The same q-w-m root (to stand) + q-n-t (to be devoutly obedient). The ṢALĀH is the standing of the devoutly obedient.
- THE ADAPTABILITY (2:239 "if you fear, [pray] on foot or riding; and when you are safe, remember God as He taught you"): the ṢALĀH is ADAPTABLE: in fear, pray on foot or riding; in safety, remember God. The ṢALĀH is not rigid; it adapts to circumstances. The ṢALĀH is a STANDING-STATE that can be performed in any posture (on foot or riding) when in danger. The standing-state is FLEXIBLE, not fixed.
- 2:242 "Thus God makes clear His signs to you, that you may understand" - the ṢALĀH is a SIGN (āyah) from God. The ṢALĀH is a sign, not just a command. The ṢALĀH is God's sign to you.

PARALLELS:
- C14 (17:78 - "establish the ṢALĀH at the decline of the sun until the darkness of the night, and the Quran of dawn"): C14 gave the ṢALĀH's TIMED boundaries; C16 gives the ṢALĀH's GUARDING (preserve the timed prayers, especially the middle one). The ṢALĀH is timed (C14) and guarded (C16). The timing and the guarding are paired: the ṢALĀH is a timed trust to be preserved.
- C13 (3:39 - Zakariyya standing-praying in the miḥrāb): the STANDING-POSTURE (qāʾim) is the physical form; the GUARDING (ḥāfiẓū) is the preservative form. The ṢALĀH is both performed (standing) and guarded (preserved). The performance and the preservation are paired.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the ESTABLISH-verb (aqīmū) appears in C1; the GUARD-verb (ḥāfiẓū) appears in C16. The ṢALĀH is both ESTABLISHED (made-stand) and GUARDED (preserved). The establishing and the guarding are paired: you establish the ṢALĀH and then guard it.
- B1 (24:41 - "the birds know their ṢALĀH"): the bird's ṢALĀH is innate (known by creation); the human ṢALĀH is guarded (preserved by command). The innate and the guarded are paired: the bird's ṢALĀH is automatic; the human's ṢALĀH is a trust to be kept.
- A16 (82:10-12 - "over you are keeping-guards, noble writers; they know what you do"): the recorders (hāfiẓīn) guard the human record; the human guards the ṢALĀH. The same "guard" (ḥāfiẓ) verb: God guards the record; the human guards the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is a record to be kept.
- 2:239 "if you fear, [pray] on foot or riding" - the ṢALĀH's adaptability. The ṢALĀH is not rigid; it adapts to danger. The standing-state is flexible: it can be performed in any posture when in danger. The same flexibility as the fire-side: the fire-stand is imposed (no flexibility); the God-side ṢALĀH is flexible (adapts to circumstances).

FINDING: C16 gives the ṢALĀH's GUARDING-DIMENSION: "GUARD the ṢALAWAT and the MIDDLE SALAH; and STAND for God in devout obedience." The ṢALĀH is a TRUST to be preserved (ḥāfiẓū). The ṢALAWAT (plural, timed) are guarded; the MIDDLE ṢALĀH (singular) is especially guarded. The ṢALĀH is not just established (C1) but GUARDED (C16). And the ṢALĀH is ADAPTABLE: in fear, pray on foot or riding (2:239). The ṢALĀH is a flexible standing-state: it can be performed in any posture when in danger. The standing-state is not rigid; it adapts to circumstances. The ṢALĀH is a STANDING in DEVOUT OBEDIENCE (qānitīn) - the standing of the devoutly obedient.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The GUARD-VERB (ḥāfiẓū) - the ṢALĀH is a trust to be preserved. HOLD for the final doc's trust-table. (ii) The MIDDLE SALAH (al-ṣalāti al-wusṭā) - the ṢALĀH has a hierarchy (middle vs others). HOLD for the final doc's hierarchy-table. (iii) The ADAPTABILITY (2:239 - on foot or riding) - the ṢALĀH is flexible. HOLD for the final doc's flexibility-table. (iv) The QĀNIT (devoutly obedient) - the ṢALĀH is the standing of the devoutly obedient. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check: does the ṢALĀH produce qunūt?). (v) The ṢALĀH now has FOUR forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), and preservative (guarded, C16). All four are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see C17 (11:114 - the ṣalāh wipes sin) next.

## C17 - 11:114 - wa-aqim al-salata tarafay al-nahari wa zulafan min al-layli

ARABIC (flow): 11:113 "And do not incline toward those who do wrong, lest the Fire touch you, and you have no protectors other than God, then you will not be helped" -> **11:114 "AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH at the TWO ENDS OF THE DAY and at the APPROACH OF THE NIGHT. Indeed the good deeds REMOVE the evil deeds. That is a reminder for those who remember"** -> 11:115 "And be patient; indeed God does not waste the reward of those who do good" -> 11:116 "If only there had been among the generations before you those of virtue who forbid corruption in the earth, except a few of those We saved from among them. But those who wronged followed what they were given in luxury, and they were criminals" -> 11:117 "And your Lord would not destroy the cities for wrongdoing while their people are reformers" -> 11:118 "And if your Lord had willed, He would have made mankind one community, but they will not cease to differ" -> 11:119 "Except those upon whom your Lord has mercy; and for that He created them. And the word of your Lord has been fulfilled: I will surely fill Jahannam with jinn and mankind all together."

WORD FORM: aqīm al-ṣalāta (establish the ṣalāh) + ṭarafay al-nahāri (at the two ends of the day) + wa zulafan min al-layli (and at the approach of the night) + inna al-ḥasanāta yudhhibna al-sayyiʾāta (indeed the good deeds remove the evil deeds) + dhālika dhikrā li-l-dhākirīn (that is a reminder for those who remember).

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah Hud (11). The verse sits in the warning-passage (11:113-119): do not incline toward wrongdoers (11:113), establish the ṢALĀH at the two ends of the day and at the approach of night (11:114), the good deeds remove evil deeds (11:114), be patient (11:115), the need for reformers (11:116-117), mankind will not cease to differ (11:118), Jahannam will be filled (11:119).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE THREE TIME-SLOTS: "at the TWO ENDS OF THE DAY and at the APPROACH OF THE NIGHT." The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED at THREE time-slots: (1) morning end (ṭaraf al-nahār al-awwal), (2) evening end (ṭaraf al-nahār al-thānī), and (3) approach of the night (zulafan min al-layl). This is a DIFFERENT time-pattern from C14 (17:78: from midday to night + dawn). C14 gave midday-to-night + dawn; C17 gives morning + evening + approach-of-night. The ṢALĀH has MULTIPLE time-patterns, all "state" features.
- THE SIN-REMOVAL (inna al-ḥasanāta yudhhibna al-sayyiʾāta): "indeed the good deeds REMOVE the evil deeds." The ṢALĀH is a GOOD DEED (ḥasanah) that REMOVES (yudhhibna) evil deeds (sayyiʾāt). The ṢALĀH WIPES SIN. The same "remove/cleanse" (yudhhib) as 9:103 (charity purifies and cleanses). The ṢALĀH is a CLEANSING act: it removes evil. The ṢALĀH is the anti-sin act.
- THE REMINDER (dhālika dhikrā li-l-dhākirīn): "that is a reminder for those who remember." The ṢALĀH is a REMINDER (dhikrā) for those who remember (dhākirīn). The SAME as C10's "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance" (li-dhikrī). The ṢALĀH IS the remembrance. The ṢALĀH is the vehicle of dhikr.
- 11:113 "do not incline toward those who do wrong, lest the Fire touch you" - the ṢALĀH is the protection against the Fire. The ṢALĀH-people are protected; the ṢALĀH-breakers are touched by the Fire. The ṢALĀH is the divider between protection and fire.
- 11:115 "be patient; indeed God does not waste the reward of those who do good" - the ṢALĀH is a good deed whose reward is not wasted. The ṢALĀH's reward is guaranteed. The same as B2 (ṢALAWĀT + raḥmah) and B5 (ṢALĀH + sakan).
- 11:119 "I will surely fill Jahannam with jinn and mankind all together" - the ṢALĀH-breakers fill Jahannam. The ṢALĀH is the divider: those who establish it are saved; those who don't are in Jahannam.

PARALLELS:
- C14 (17:78 - "establish the ṢALĀH at the decline of the sun until the darkness of the night, and the Quran of dawn"): C14 gave midday-to-night + dawn; C17 gives morning + evening + approach-of-night. TWO different time-patterns, both "state" features. The ṢALĀH has MULTIPLE time-patterns.
- C10 (20:14 - "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance"): C10's li-dhikrī (for My remembrance) and C17's dhikrā li-l-dhākirīn (reminder for those who remember) are the SAME: the ṢALĀH IS the remembrance.
- 9:103 (B5 - "your ṣalāh is their tranquility"): B5's ṢALĀH-as-tranquility (sakan) and C17's ṢALĀH-as-sin-remover (yudhhibna) are the SAME ṢALĀH with different qualities: tranquility (for the recipient) and sin-removal (for the stander). The ṢALĀH is both a GIFT (to others) and a CLEANSING (for oneself).
- A7 (19:59 - "they lost the ṢALĀH and followed desires"): the ṢALĀH removes sin (C17); the ṢALĀH-loss leads to fire-delivery (A7). The ṢALĀH is the anti-sin act; losing it leads to sin-following -> fire-delivery.
- 11:113 "do not incline toward those who do wrong, lest the Fire touch you" - the ṢALĀH is the protection against the Fire. The ṢALĀH-people are protected; the ṢALĀH-breakers are in the Fire. The ṢALĀH is the divider.

FINDING: C17 gives the ṢALĀH's SIN-REMOVAL quality: "the good deeds REMOVE the evil deeds." The ṢALĀH is a GOOD DEED (ḥasanah) that REMOVES (yudhhibna) evil deeds (sayyiʾāt). The ṢALĀH WIPES SIN. The same "remove/cleanse" (yudhhib) as 9:103 (charity purifies and cleanses). The ṢALĀH is a CLEANSING act: it removes evil. And the ṢALĀH is a REMINDER (dhikrā) for those who remember (dhākirīn) - the same as C10's "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance." The ṢALĀH IS the remembrance. The ṢALĀH has a THIRD time-pattern (morning + evening + approach-of-night) - different from C14's (midday-to-night + dawn). The ṢALĀH has MULTIPLE time-patterns, all "state" features.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The SIN-REMOVAL (yudhhibna) - the ṢALĀH removes evil deeds. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check: does the ṢALĀH transform the stander?). (ii) The REMINDER (dhikrā) - the ṢALĀH IS the remembrance. HOLD for the final doc's remembrance-table. (iii) The THREE time-patterns (C14: midday-to-night + dawn; C17: morning + evening + approach-of-night) - the ṢALĀH has MULTIPLE time-patterns. HOLD for the final doc's time-table. (iv) The ṢALĀH as protection against the Fire (11:113) - the ṢALĀH-people are protected; the ṢALĀH-breakers are in the Fire. HOLD for the final doc's protection-table. (v) The ṢALĀH's reward is not wasted (11:115) - the ṢALĀH's reward is guaranteed. HOLD for the final doc's reward-table.

VERDICT: studied - see C18 (20:132 - command your family to ṣalāh and be steadfast) next.

## C18 - 20:132 - wa-mur ahlika bi-s-salati wa-istabir alayha

ARABIC (flow): 20:129 "And were it not for a word that had preceded from your Lord, it would have been an obligation, and [also] a specified term" -> 20:130 "So be patient over what they say, and glorify with praise of your Lord before the rising of the sun and before its setting, and from the hours of the night glorify, and at the ends of the day, that you may be pleased" -> 20:131 "And do not extend your eyes toward that which We have given to enjoy to [some] couples among them - the splendor of the worldly life - by which We test them; and the provision of your Lord is better and more enduring" -> **20:132 "AND COMMAND YOUR FAMILY THE SALAH, AND BE STEADFAST UPON IT. We do not ask you for provision; We provide for you, and the outcome is for righteousness (taqwa)"** -> 20:133-134 "And they said: why does he not bring us a sign from his Lord? Has there not come to them clear evidence of what is in the former scriptures? And if We had destroyed them with a punishment before it, they would have said: Our Lord, why did You not send to us a messenger so that we could follow Your signs before we were humiliated and disgraced?" -> 20:135 "Say: everyone is waiting, so wait; and you will know who are the companions of the straight path and who is guided."

WORD FORM: mur (command, imperative) + ahlika (your family) + bi-l-ṣalāti (the ṣalāh) + wa-iṣṭabir (and be steadfast) + ʿalayhā (upon it) + lā nasʾaluka rizqan (We do not ask you for provision) + naḥnu narzuquka (We provide for you) + wa-l-ʿāqibatu li-l-taqwā (and the outcome is for righteousness/taqwa).

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah Ta-Ha (20). The verse sits in the closing-passage of Surah 20 (20:129-135): be patient and glorify (20:130), do not desire worldly splendor (20:131), COMMAND YOUR FAMILY THE SALAH AND BE STEADFAST UPON IT (20:132), We provide for you (20:132), the outcome is for taqwa (20:132).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE FAMILY COMMAND (mur ahlika bi-l-ṣalāti): "COMMAND YOUR FAMILY THE SALAH" - the Prophet is commanded to COMMAND his family the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is a FAMILY COMMAND, not just a communal one. The same as C9 (Isma'il COMMANDED his family the ṢALĀH) and C8 (Jesus was ENJOINED the ṢALĀH). The ṢALĀH is commanded to the family.
- THE STEADFASTNESS (wa-iṣṭabir ʿalayhā): "and BE STEADFAST UPON IT" - the Prophet is to be STEADFAST upon the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH requires STEADFASTNESS (ṣabr). The same ṣabr as 2:153 ("seek help in patience and the ṣalāh") and 11:115 ("be patient; God does not waste the reward"). The ṢALĀH is not a one-time act; it requires STEADFASTNESS - the standing-state must be KEPT.
- "WE DO NOT ASK YOU FOR PROVISION; WE PROVIDE FOR YOU" - the ṢALĀH is not a means of earning provision; God PROVIDES. The ṢALĀH is not an economic transaction; it is a standing-toward-God. The same as 6:162 ("my ṢALĀH... is for God") - the ṢALĀH is for God, not for provision. The ṢALĀH and provision are SEPARATE: God provides; the ṢALĀH is for God.
- "THE OUTCOME IS FOR RIGHTEOUSNESS (taqwa)" - the ṢALĀH's outcome is taqwa (righteousness/god-consciousness). The ṢALĀH leads to taqwa - the same as 9:103 (charity purifies -> s-l-l -> tranquility) and 11:114 (good deeds remove evil deeds). The ṢALĀH is transformative: it leads to taqwa.
- 20:130 "glorify with praise of your Lord before the rising of the sun and before its setting, and from the hours of the night glorify, and at the ends of the day" - the ṢALĀH's time-pattern: before sunrise, before sunset, from the hours of the night, and at the ends of the day. A FOURTH time-pattern (C14: midday-to-night + dawn; C17: morning + evening + approach-of-night; C18: before sunrise + before sunset + night hours + ends of day).
- 20:131 "do not extend your eyes toward that which We have given to enjoy to some couples... the splendor of the worldly life" - the ṢALĀH is the alternative to worldly desire. The same as C10's "let not one who follows his desire divert you" (20:16). The ṢALĀH protects against desire.

PARALLELS:
- C9 (19:55 - Isma'il commanded his family the ṢALĀH): C9 showed the COMMAND (yaʾmuru); C18 shows the COMMAND (mur) + STEADFASTNESS (iṣṭabir). The ṢALĀH requires both commanding AND being steadfast. The command and the steadfastness are paired.
- C7 (14:40 - "make me an establisher of the ṢALĀH"): Abraham prays to be made an establisher; the Prophet is commanded to command his family (C18). The prayer (C7) and the command (C18) are paired.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the ESTABLISH-verb (aqīmū) appears in C1; the COMMAND-verb (mur) appears in C18. The ṢALĀH is both ESTABLISHED (C1) and COMMANDED (C18). The establishing and the commanding are paired.
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (commanded to the family) IS. The ṢALĀH is the COMMANDED form of fa-ṣallā.
- B5 (9:103 - "your ṣalāh is their tranquility"): the Prophet's ṢALĀH (commanded) brings tranquility (sakan) to the repentant. The commanded-ṢALĀH is the healing act.
- 20:130 "glorify with praise of your Lord before the rising of the sun and before its setting" - the ṢALĀH's time-pattern includes glorification (tasbīḥ). The same as B3 (33:42 "glorify Him morning and evening").

FINDING: C18 shows the ṢALĀH as a FAMILY COMMAND requiring STEADFASTNESS: "command your family the ṢALĀH, and be steadfast upon it." The ṢALĀH is not just commanded; it requires STEADFASTNESS (ṣabr). The ṢALĀH is a standing-state that must be KEPT, not a one-time act. And the ṢALĀH's outcome is taqwa (righteousness): "the outcome is for taqwa." The ṢALĀH is transformative: it leads to taqwa. The ṢALĀH is not an economic transaction ("We do not ask you for provision; We provide for you") - it is a standing-toward-God. And the ṢALĀH has a FOURTH time-pattern (before sunrise + before sunset + night hours + ends of day) - different from C14 and C17. The ṢALĀH has MULTIPLE time-patterns, all "state" features.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The STEADFASTNESS (iṣṭabir ʿalayhā) - the ṢALĀH requires steadfastness. The ṢALĀH is not a one-time act; it must be KEPT. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check: does the ṢALĀH produce steadfastness?). (ii) The OUTCOME (taqwa) - the ṢALĀH leads to taqwa. HOLD for E-cluster. (iii) The FOUR time-patterns (C14, C17, C18) - the ṢALĀH has MULTIPLE time-patterns. HOLD for the final doc's time-table. (iv) The FAMILY COMMAND (mur ahlika) - the ṢALĀH is commanded to the family. HOLD for the final doc's command-table. (v) The ṢALĀH vs worldly desire (20:131) - the ṢALĀH protects against desire. HOLD for E-cluster.

VERDICT: studied - see C19 (31:17 - Luqman: "establish the ṣalāh, enjoin what is right, forbid what is wrong") next.

## C19 - 31:17 - ya bunayya aqim al-salata wa-mur bi-l-ma'rufi wa-nha ani-l-munkari

ARABIC (flow): 31:12 "And We gave Luqman wisdom: be grateful to God; and whoever is grateful, he is grateful for himself; and whoever is ungrateful, God is Self-Sufficient, Praiseworthy" -> 31:13 "And when Luqman said to his son while admonishing him: O my son, do not associate partners with God; indeed associating partners is a great wrong" -> 31:14 "And We have enjoined upon man [care] for his parents... be grateful to Me and to your parents; to Me is the destination" -> 31:15 "But if they strive to make you associate with Me that of which you have no knowledge, do not obey them, but accompany them in this world with kindness, and follow the path of one who turns to Me; then to Me is your return, and I will inform you of what you used to do" -> 31:16 "O my son, indeed if it were the weight of a mustard seed and it were in a rock or in the heavens or in the earth, God will bring it forth; indeed God is Subtle, Aware" -> **31:17 "O my son, ESTABLISH THE SALAH, and ENJOIN what is right, and FORBID what is wrong, and BE PATIENT over what befalls you; indeed that is of the matters of determination"** -> 31:18 "And do not turn your cheek away from people, and do not walk on the earth with arrogance; indeed God does not love every self-deluded boaster" -> 31:19 "And be moderate in your pace, and lower your voice; indeed the most disagreeable of sounds is the voice of donkeys."

WORD FORM: aqim al-ṣalāta (establish the ṣalāh) + wa-mur bi-l-maʿrūfi (and enjoin what is right) + wa-nha ʿani al-munkari (and forbid what is wrong) + wa-iṣbir ʿalā mā aṣābaka (and be patient over what befalls you) + inna dhālika min ʿazmi al-umūri (indeed that is of the matters of determination). The ṢALĀH is command #1 of a FOUR-command list.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah Luqman (31). The verse sits in Luqman's admonition to his son (31:13-19): do not associate partners with God (31:13), be grateful to parents (31:14), follow the path of one who turns to God (31:15), every deed will be brought forth (31:16), ESTABLISH THE SALAH + enjoin right + forbid wrong + be patient (31:17), do not be arrogant (31:18), be moderate in pace and lower your voice (31:19).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE FOUR-COMMAND-LIST: (1) ESTABLISH THE SALAH, (2) enjoin what is right (amr bi-l-maʿrūf), (3) forbid what is wrong (nahy ʿan al-munkar), (4) be patient (iṣbir) over what befalls you. The ṢALĀH is command #1 - the FIRST of four. The ṢALĀH is the FOUNDATION; the other three (enjoin right, forbid wrong, be patient) are the SOCIAL and PERSONAL dimensions. The ṢALĀH is the BASE; the other three are the BUILDING.
- "INDEED THAT IS OF THE MATTERS OF DETERMINATION" (inna dhālika min ʿazmi al-umūri): the ṢALĀH + enjoin right + forbid wrong + patience are matters of DETERMINATION (ʿazm). The ṢALĀH is a matter of determination - not a casual act but a DETERMINED commitment. The ṢALĀH is a FIRM RESOLVE, not a passing inclination.
- 31:13 "do not associate partners with God; indeed associating partners is a great wrong" - the ṢALĀH is the alternative to shirk (associating partners). The standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) replaces the standing-toward-idols (shirk). The same as C6's "keep me and my sons away from worshipping idols" (14:35) and C10's "worship Me" (20:14). The ṢALĀH is the anti-shirk act.
- 31:16 "if it were the weight of a mustard seed... God will bring it forth" - the ṢALĀH is for the Day of Reckoning: every deed, however small, will be brought forth. The ṢALĀH is preparation for the Day of Standing (Qiyāmah). The same as C7 (14:41) and C10 (20:15).
- 31:19 "be moderate in your pace, and lower your voice; indeed the most disagreeable of sounds is the voice of donkeys" - the ṢALĀH's voice-dimension (C15: moderated voice) is extended to daily life: lower your voice. The ṢALĀH's moderation is the model for daily behavior. The ṢALĀH is not just a ritual; it is a MODEL for daily conduct.

PARALLELS:
- C18 (20:132 - "command your family the ṢALĀH and be steadfast"): C18 showed the FAMILY COMMAND (mur ahlika) + STEADFASTNESS (iṣṭabir). C19 shows the PERSONAL COMMAND (aqim) + ENJOIN RIGHT + FORBID WRONG + PATIENCE. The ṢALĀH is both a family command (C18) and a personal command (C19). The ṢALĀH is for the family and for the self.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): C1's two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + zakah) is expanded in C19 to a four-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + enjoin right + forbid wrong + patience). The ṢALĀH is the first pillar; the other three are the social/personal dimensions.
- C3 (2:83 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah; then you turned away"): C3 showed the covenant-package (8 items) with ṢALĀH + ZAKAH as items 7-8. C19 shows the PERSONAL package (4 items) with ṢALĀH as item 1. The ṢALĀH is the first item in both packages.
- C11 (11:87 - "Does your ṢALĀH command you?"): C11 showed the mockers' view of the ṢALĀH as a commanding-force. C19 shows Luqman's view of the ṢALĀH as the FIRST command. The mockers (C11) and Luqman (C19) both see the ṢALĀH as a commanding-force; Luqman embraces it, the mockers reject it.
- C15 (17:110 - "do not make your voice too loud in your ṢALĀH"): C15 gave the ṢALĀH's voice-dimension (moderated voice). C19 extends it to daily life (31:19 "lower your voice"). The ṢALĀH's moderation is the model for daily conduct.
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (established) IS. Luqman commands his son to establish the ṢALĀH - to make permanent the fa-ṣallā.

FINDING: C19 gives the ṢALĀH as command #1 of a FOUR-command personal formula: (1) ESTABLISH THE SALAH, (2) enjoin what is right, (3) forbid what is wrong, (4) be patient. The ṢALĀH is the FOUNDATION; the other three are the BUILDING. And the four are "matters of DETERMINATION" (ʿazm) - the ṢALĀH is a DETERMINED commitment, not a casual act. The ṢALĀH is the alternative to shirk (31:13). The ṢALĀH is for the Day of Reckoning (31:16). And the ṢALĀH's voice-dimension (C15) is extended to daily life (31:19: lower your voice). The ṢALĀH is not just a ritual; it is a MODEL for daily conduct.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The FOUR-command formula (31:17) - ṢALĀH + enjoin right + forbid wrong + patience. HOLD for the final doc's command-table. (ii) The DETERMINATION (ʿazm) - the ṢALĀH is a matter of determination. HOLD for the final doc's resolve-table. (iii) The ANTI-SHIRK (31:13) - the ṢALĀH replaces idol-worship. HOLD for the final doc's anti-idolatry table. (iv) The ṢALĀH as MODEL for daily conduct (31:19) - the ṢALĀH's moderation extends to daily life. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check). (v) The ṢALĀH now has FIVE forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), and personal (commanded to the self, C19). All five are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see C20 (33:33 - women of the Prophet: "establish the ṣalāh") next.

## C20 - 33:33 - aqimna al-salata wa atina al-zakata (women of the Prophet)

ARABIC (flow): 33:28 "O Prophet, say to your wives: if you desire the worldly life and its adornment, come, I will provide for you and release you with a good release" -> 33:29 "But if you desire God and His Messenger and the Hereafter, then indeed God has prepared for the doers of good among you a great reward" -> 33:30 "O wives of the Prophet, whoever of you commits a clear indecency, the punishment will be doubled for her" -> 33:31 "And whoever of you is devout to God and His Messenger and does righteousness, We will give her reward twice, and We have prepared for her a noble provision" -> 33:32 "O wives of the Prophet, you are not like any other women. If you fear God, then do not be soft in speech, lest he in whose heart is disease should covet; and speak in an appropriate manner" -> **33:33 "And stay in your houses, and do not display yourselves as in the former times of ignorance, and ESTABLISH THE SALAH and GIVE THE ZAKAH, and OBEY God and His Messenger. God only desires to remove from you the impurity, O people of the House, and to purify you with a thorough purification"** -> 33:34 "And remember what is recited in your houses of the signs of God and the wisdom; God is Subtle, Aware" -> 33:35 "Indeed for the Muslim men and Muslim women, the believing men and believing women, the devout men and devout women, the truthful men and truthful women, the patient men and patient women, the humble men and humble women, the charitable men and charitable women, the fasting men and fasting women, the men who guard their private parts and the women who guard them, and the men who remember God much and the women who remember Him - God has prepared for them forgiveness and a great reward" -> 33:36 "And it is not for a believing man or woman, when God and His Messenger have decided a matter, that they have any choice in their affair."

WORD FORM: aqimna (establish, feminine plural imperative) al-ṣalāta + ātīna (give) al-zakāta + aṭiʿna (obey) Allāha wa rasūlahu - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1's two-pillar formula) now with a THIRD element: OBEY God and His Messenger. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH + OBEDIENCE formula.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Ahzab (33). The verse sits in the Prophet's wives passage (33:28-36): the choice between worldly life and God/His Messenger/the Hereafter (33:28-29), the doubling of punishment and reward (33:30-31), the etiquette of speech (33:32), STAY IN YOUR HOUSES + DO NOT DISPLAY + ESTABLISH THE SALAH + GIVE THE ZAKAH + OBEY GOD AND HIS MESSENGER (33:33), with the PURPOSE: "God only desires to remove from you the impurity and to purify you with a thorough purification."

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE PURIFICATION-PURPOSE (innamā yurīdu Allāhu li-yudhhiba ʿankumu al-rijsa ahla al-bayti wa yuṭahhirakum taṭhīran): "God only desires to remove from you the impurity, O people of the House, and to purify you with a thorough purification." The ṢALĀH is part of a PURIFICATION package: stay in houses + do not display + establish ṢALĀH + give zakah + obey God and His Messenger = REMOVE IMPURITY and PURIFY. The ṢALĀH is a PURIFYING act - the same purity-gate as A12 (56:79 "none touch it except the purified"), A15 (74:4 "purify your garments"), C1 (98:5 "making the religion pure for Him"), and C5 (2:125 "purify My House"). The ṢALĀH requires and produces PURITY.
- THE ṢALĀH + ZAKAH + OBEDIENCE FORMULA: the two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + ZAKAH) now has a THIRD element: OBEDIENCE to God and His Messenger. The ṢALĀH is embedded in obedience - the standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) is the act of one who OBEYS God and His Messenger. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH + OBEDIENCE are the THREE pillars of the Prophet's household.
- 33:34 "remember what is recited in your houses of the signs of God and the wisdom" - the ṢALĀH is paired with REMEMBRANCE (dhikr) of the Quran and wisdom. The ṢALĀH is the vehicle of remembrance (C10's "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance"). The ṢALĀH and the Quran-recitation are paired.
- 33:35 the long believer-list: the ṢALĀH-qualifiers (Muslim, mu'min, qanit, sadiq, sabir, khashi', mutasaddiq, sa'im, hafiz furuj, dhakir) - the same list as B3 (33:35). The ṢALĀH is for those who live these qualities. The ṢALĀH-qualifiers are the STANDING-QUALITIES.
- 33:31 "whoever of you is devout to God and His Messenger and does righteousness, We will give her reward twice" - the ṢALĀH is for the devout (qānit). The ṢALĀH and qunūt (devout obedience) are paired (C16's "stand for God in devout obedience, qānitīn").
- 33:29 "if you desire God and His Messenger and the Hereafter, then God has prepared for the doers of good among you a great reward" - the ṢALĀH is for those who desire God (not the world). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who desires God - the same as C19's "do not desire the worldly life" (31:16? no, 20:131).

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + ZAKAH) in C1 and C20. The formula is STABLE. C20 adds OBEDIENCE as the third element.
- C3 (2:83 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah; then you turned away"): C3 showed the turning-away (tawalla) from the ṢALĀH. C20 shows the PURIFICATION that the ṢALĀH produces (rijs removal, taṭhīr). The ṢALĀH is both turned-away-from (C3) and purifying (C20).
- C10 (20:14 - "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance"): C10's li-dhikrī (for My remembrance) and C20's 33:34 (remember what is recited in your houses) are the SAME: the ṢALĀH is the vehicle of remembrance.
- A12 (56:79 - "none touch it except the purified"): the purification-gate. C20's ṢALĀH is part of a purification package: the ṢALĀH removes impurity (rijs) and purifies (taṭhīr). The ṢALĀH is both gated on purity and productive of purity.
- B3 (33:43 - divine ṢALĀH -> exodus darkness->light): 33:33's purification (remove impurity, purify) is the HUMAN counterpart of B3's exodus (darkness->light). The human ṢALĀH purifies; the divine ṢALĀH brings out of darkness. Both are cleansing acts.
- B4 (33:56 - ṢALLŪ ʿALAYHI on the Prophet): the divine ṢALĀH on the Prophet (B4) and the human ṢALĀH established by the Prophet's wives (C20) are paired: the wives establish the ṢALĀH, and God and His angels s-l-l on the Prophet. The human ṢALĀH and the divine ṢALĀH are paired.

FINDING: C20 shows the ṢALĀH as a PURIFYING act: "God only desires to remove from you the impurity and to purify you with a thorough purification." The ṢALĀH (established) + ZAKAH (given) + OBEDIENCE (to God and His Messenger) = REMOVE IMPURITY + PURIFY. The ṢALĀH is part of a purification package. The ṢALĀH both requires purity (A12's gate) and produces purity (C20's purification). And the ṢALĀH is paired with REMEMBRANCE (33:34 "remember what is recited in your houses") - the same as C10's "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance." The ṢALĀH is the vehicle of remembrance AND purification.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The PURIFICATION-PACKAGE (33:33) - the ṢALĀH is part of a purification package: stay in houses + do not display + establish ṢALĀH + give zakah + obey = remove impurity + purify. HOLD for the final doc's purification-table. (ii) The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH + OBEDIENCE formula (33:33) - the three-pillar formula. HOLD for the final doc's command-table. (iii) The REMEMBRANCE (33:34) - the ṢALĀH is paired with remembering what is recited in the houses. HOLD for the final doc's remembrance-table. (iv) The ṢALĀH now has SIX forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), and purifying (purification-package, C20). All six are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see C21 (73:20 - the Quran's own night-ṣalāh arrangement) next.

## C21 - 73:20 - wa-aqimu-s-salata wa-atu-z-zakata (the Quran's own night-salah arrangement)

ARABIC (flow): 73:1 "O you wrapped up (in garments)" -> 73:2 "Stand (in prayer) at night, except a little" -> 73:3 "Half of it, or lessen from it a little" -> 73:4 "Or add to it, and recite the Quran with measured recitation (tartīl)" -> 73:5 "Indeed We will cast upon you a heavy word" -> 73:6 "Indeed the rising of the night is more effective in imprinting and more upright in speech" -> 73:7 "Indeed you have in the day a long occupation" -> 73:8 "And remember the name of your Lord, and devote yourself to Him with complete devotion (tabattul)" -> 73:9 "Lord of the east and the west; there is no god but He, so take Him as a trustee" -> 73:10 "And be patient over what they say, and leave them with a beautiful leaving" -> 73:11-14 "And leave Me with the deniers... fetters and Jahannam... food that chokes... Day when the earth and mountains will shake" -> 73:15-16 "Indeed We have sent to you a Messenger as a witness over you, as We sent to Pharaoh a messenger. But Pharaoh disobeyed the messenger, so We seized him with a severe seizure" -> 73:17-19 "Then how will you guard yourselves if you disbelieve... Indeed this is a reminder; so whoever wills, let him take a path to his Lord" -> **73:20 "Indeed your Lord knows that you stand (in prayer) for less than two-thirds of the night, and half of it, and a third of it, and a party of those with you. And God measures the night and the day. He knows that you will not count it exactly, so He turned to you in mercy. So RECITE what is easy of the Quran. He knows that there will be among you the sick, and others traveling in the land seeking the bounty of God, and others fighting in the path of God. So RECITE what is easy of it, and ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH AND LEND GOD A GOOD LOAN. And whatever good you send forth for yourselves, you will find it with God - better and greater in reward. And seek forgiveness from God; indeed God is Forgiving, Merciful."**

WORD FORM: aqīmū al-ṣalāta (establish the ṣalāh) + ātū al-zakāta (give the zakah) + wa-aqriḍū Allāha qarḍan ḥasanan (and lend God a good loan) - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair now with a THIRD element: LEND GOD A GOOD LOAN. Plus Quran-recitation (qirāʾah) as the vehicle.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-Muzzammil (73). The verse sits in the night-prayer passage (73:1-20): stand at night (73:2), recite the Quran with measured recitation (73:4), remember the name of your Lord (73:8), be patient (73:10), the warning of Jahannam (73:12-14), the Messenger as witness (73:15), and then the EASED ARRANGEMENT (73:20): God knows you cannot count the night exactly, so He turned in mercy; recite what is easy; establish the ṢALĀH, give the zakah, lend God a good loan; seek forgiveness.

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE EASED ARRANGEMENT (73:20 "He knows that you will not count it exactly, so He turned to you in mercy"): God KNOWS (yaʿlamu) that the Prophet and his companions cannot count the night exactly (they try to stand in prayer for two-thirds/half/third of the night), so God TURNS in mercy (taba ʿalaykum) and EASES the arrangement. The ṢALĀH is part of an EASED arrangement - God adjusts the ṢALĀH to the people's capacity. The ṢALĀH is not rigid; it is FLEXIBLE.
- THE THREE HUMAN STATES (73:20 "there will be among you the sick, and others traveling in the land seeking the bounty of God, and others fighting in the path of God"): the ṢALĀH is for ALL states: the sick, the traveling, the fighting. The ṢALĀH is UNIVERSAL - it adapts to all conditions. The same adaptability as 2:239 ("if you fear, pray on foot or riding") and 73:20 (recite what is easy).
- THE QURAN-RECITATION (73:4 "recite the Quran with measured recitation (tartīl)" and 73:20 "recite what is easy of the Quran"): the ṢALĀH includes Quran-recitation with tartīl (measured recitation). The ṢALĀH is the vehicle of Quran-recitation - the same as C14 ("the Quran of dawn") and C15 (Quran-recitation causes prostration and weeping).
- THE LEND-GOD-A-GOOD-LOAN (wa-aqriḍū Allāha qarḍan ḥasanan): the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair now has a THIRD element: LEND GOD A GOOD LOAN. The ṢALĀH is paired with giving (zakah) and lending (qarḍ ḥasan). The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH + QARḌ is the THREE-pillar formula of the night-prayer arrangement.
- 73:8 "remember the name of your Lord, and devote yourself to Him with complete devotion (tabattul)": the ṢALĀH is the act of remembering the Name (dhikr) and devoting oneself (tabattul). The ṢALĀH is the devotion-act - the same as A19's fa-ṣallā (standing-toward-the-Mentioned-Name).
- 73:2 "Stand (in prayer) at night, except a little" - the ṢALĀH is performed AT NIGHT. The night-ṢALĀH (tahajjud) is a distinct time-slot. The ṢALĀH has a NIGHT dimension - the same as C14's "Quran of dawn" and C17's "approach of the night."
- 73:20 "God measures the night and the day" - the ṢALĀH's time is GOD-MEASURED. The standing-times are God's to measure, not the human's. The ṢALĀH-time is divine, not human. The same as C14's timed boundaries (God sets the times).

PARALLELS:
- C14 (17:78 - "establish the ṢALĀH at the decline of the sun until the darkness of the night, and the Quran of dawn"): C14 gave the ṢALĀH's TIMED boundaries (midday to night + dawn). C21 gives the ṢALĀH's EASED arrangement (recite what is easy + establish ṢALĀH + give zakah + lend God a good loan). The ṢALĀH is both TIMED (C14) and EASED (C21). The timing and the easing are paired: the ṢALĀH has fixed times (C14) but flexible content (C21).
- C16 (2:238 - "guard the ṢALAWAT and the middle ṢALĀH; if you fear, pray on foot or riding"): C16 gave the ṢALĀH's ADAPTABILITY (on foot or riding). C21 gives the ṢALĀH's UNIVERSALITY (sick, traveling, fighting). The ṢALĀH adapts to ALL conditions.
- C10 (20:14 - "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance"): C10's li-dhikrī (for My remembrance) and C21's 73:8 (remember the name of your Lord) are the SAME: the ṢALĀH is the vehicle of remembrance (dhikr). The ṢALĀH IS the dhikr.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + ZAKAH) in C1 and C21. C21 adds LEND GOD A GOOD LOAN as a third element. The formula grows: C1 (ṢALĀH + zakah) -> C21 (ṢALĀH + zakah + qarḍ ḥasan).
- C15 (17:110 - "do not make your voice too loud in your ṢALĀH"): C15 gave the ṢALĀH's VOICE (moderated). C21 gives the ṢALĀH's RECITATION (tartīl, measured recitation). The ṢALĀH has both a voice-dimension (moderated, C15) and a recitation-dimension (measured, C21).
- B3 (33:43 - divine ṢALĀH -> exodus darkness->light): 73:20's "God turned to you in mercy" (taba ʿalaykum) is the divine response to the human ṢALĀH. The human ṢALĀH (commanded) -> divine mercy (granted). The ṢALĀH-chain: human act -> divine response.

FINDING: C21 gives the ṢALĀH's EASED ARRANGEMENT: God KNOWS that the Prophet and his companions cannot count the night exactly, so He TURNS in mercy and EASES the arrangement: "recite what is easy of the Quran, establish the ṢALĀH, give the zakah, lend God a good loan." The ṢALĀH is part of an EASED arrangement - God adjusts the ṢALĀH to the people's capacity. The ṢALĀH is for ALL states: the sick, the traveling, the fighting. The ṢALĀH is UNIVERSAL. And the ṢALĀH is paired with Quran-recitation (tartīl), remembrance (dhikr), and lending God a good loan (qarḍ ḥasan). The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH + QARḌ is the three-pillar formula of the night-prayer arrangement. The ṢALĀH is not rigid; it is FLEXIBLE and EASED.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The EASED ARRANGEMENT (73:20 "He turned to you in mercy; recite what is easy") - the ṢALĀH is EASED by God. HOLD for the final doc's ease-table. (ii) The THREE-PILLAR FORMULA (ṢALĀH + zakah + qarḍ ḥasan) - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair now has a THIRD element (lend God a good loan). HOLD for the final doc's command-table. (iii) The UNIVERSALITY (73:20 "the sick, the traveling, the fighting") - the ṢALĀH is for ALL states. HOLD for the final doc's universality-table. (iv) The QURAN-RECITATION (73:4 tartīl) - the ṢALĀH includes measured recitation. HOLD for the final doc's recitation-table. (v) The DIVINE MERCY (73:20 "He turned to you in mercy") - the ṢALĀH is met with divine mercy. HOLD for B-cluster (the divine ṢALĀH is the response). (vi) C-CLUSTER COMPLETE (21/21): the command-side's ṢALĀH is now fully studied: ESTABLISHED (C1), COMMUNITY (C2), COVENANT (C3), FIVE-CONDITIONS (C4), SITE (C5), PURPOSE (C6), ESTABLISHER-PRAYER (C7), PROPHETIC ENJOINMENT (C8), FAMILY COMMAND (C9), FOR REMEMBRANCE (C10), MOCKERS' VIEW (C11), FOUR-FOLD DEDICATION (C12), STANDING-POSTURE (C13), TIMED (C14), VOCAL (C15), GUARDED (C16), SIN-REMOVAL (C17), STEADFASTNESS (C18), PERSONAL (C19), PURIFYING (C20), EASED (C21). The ṢALĀH now has SEVEN forms: physical (standing), temporal (timed), vocal (moderated voice), preservative (guarded), personal (commanded to the self), purifying (purification-package), and eased (adjusted to capacity). All seven are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - C-cluster COMPLETE (21/21). Next cluster D1 (4:101-103 - war prayer).

## D9-D11 - 4:101-103 - war prayer (qasr and khawf)

ARABIC (flow): 4:99 "Perhaps God will pardon them; God is Pardoning, Forgiving" -> 4:100 "And whoever emigrates in the path of God will find on earth many refuges and abundance; and whoever leaves his home, emigrating to God and His Messenger, then death overtakes him - his reward has already fallen upon God" -> 4:101 "And when you travel in the earth, there is no blame on you that you SHORTEN THE SALAH, if you fear that the disbelievers may put you in trial. Indeed the disbelievers are to you a clear enemy" -> 4:102 "And when you are among them and ESTABLISH THE SALAH FOR THEM, then let a group of them stand with you and let them take their weapons; and when they prostrate, let them be behind you; and let another group who have not prayed come and pray with you, and let them take their precautions and their weapons. The disbelievers wish that you would be heedless of your weapons and your baggage so that they could turn upon you in one turn. And there is no blame on you if you are troubled by rain or are sick that you put down your weapons; but take your precautions. Indeed God has prepared for the disbelievers a humiliating punishment" -> 4:103 "And when you have FINISHED THE SALAH, then REMEMBER God standing and sitting and on your sides. And when you are secure, then ESTABLISH THE SALAH; indeed the Salah was upon the believers a PRESCRIBED WRIT (kitāban mawqūtan)" -> 4:104 "And do not weaken in seeking the people; if you suffer, they suffer as you suffer; and you hope from God what they do not hope; and God is Knowing, Wise" -> 4:105-108 (judgment, forgiveness, hypocrisy).

WORD FORM: qaṣr (shortening) of the ṢALĀH (4:101) + iqāma (establishing) the ṢALĀH (4:102) + dhikr (remembrance) after the ṢALĀH (4:103) + kitāban mawqūtan (prescribed writ, timed) (4:103).

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah an-Nisa' (4). The verse sits in the war-passage (4:99-108): emigration, travel, SHORTENING the ṢALĀH in fear, the WAR-PRAYER (establish in shifts with weapons), remembrance after the ṢALĀH, the ṢALĀH as a PRESCRIBED WRIT with fixed times.

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE SHORTENING (qaṣr, 4:101 "there is no blame on you that you SHORTEN THE SALAH, if you fear"): the ṢALĀH can be SHORTENED (qaṣr) when traveling and in fear. The ṢALĀH is ADAPTABLE: it can be shortened (qaṣr) in travel/fear. The same adaptability as 2:239 ("if you fear, pray on foot or riding") and 73:20 ("recite what is easy"). The ṢALĀH is FLEXIBLE: it adapts to circumstances. The standing-state is not rigid; it can be shortened.
- THE WAR-PRAYER (4:102 "establish the ṢALĀH FOR THEM, then let a group of them stand with you... and let another group who have not prayed come and pray with you"): the ṢALĀH is performed IN SHIFTS during battle: one group prays while another guards; then they switch. The ṢALĀH is performed in COMMUNITY even in WAR. The standing-site is the battlefield; the standing-act is performed with weapons in hand. The ṢALĀH is a COMMUNITY-act even in the most dangerous circumstances.
- THE POST-SALAH REMEMBRANCE (4:103 "when you have finished the ṢALĀH, then REMEMBER God standing and sitting and on your sides"): AFTER the ṢALĀH, remember God in all postures (standing, sitting, on sides). The ṢALĀH is followed by continuous remembrance (dhikr) in all postures. The ṢALĀH is the anchor; the dhikr is the continuation. The ṢALĀH is not the end; it is the BEGINNING of continuous remembrance.
- THE PRESCRIBED WRIT (4:103 "indeed the Salah was upon the believers a PRESCRIBED WRIT (kitāban mawqūtan)"): the ṢALĀH is a PRESCRIBED WRIT (kitāb mawqūt): it is WRITTEN (kitāb) and TIMED (mawqūt). The ṢALĀH is a divine decree with fixed times. The same as C14 (17:78: "establish the ṢALĀH at the decline of the sun until the darkness of the night") - the ṢALĀH has FIXED TIMES. The ṢALĀH is a WRITTEN decree, not a human invention.
- 4:102 "there is no blame on you if you are troubled by rain or are sick that you put down your weapons; but take your precautions" - the ṢALĀH adapts to weather and sickness. The ṢALĀH is FLEXIBLE: it adapts to rain, sickness, travel, war, fear. The standing-state is not rigid; it adjusts to circumstances.

PARALLELS:
- C16 (2:238 - "guard the ṢALAWAT and the middle ṢALĀH; if you fear, pray on foot or riding"): C16 gave the ṢALĀH's ADAPTABILITY (on foot or riding). D1 gives the ṢALĀH's SHORTENING (qaṣr) and WAR-PRAYER (shifts). The ṢALĀH adapts to ALL circumstances: fear, rain, sickness, war, travel.
- C14 (17:78 - "establish the ṢALĀH at the decline of the sun until the darkness of the night, and the Quran of dawn"): C14 gave the ṢALĀH's TIMED boundaries. D1 gives the ṢALĀH as a PRESCRIBED WRIT (kitāban mawqūtan) - the ṢALĀH is WRITTEN and TIMED. The timing and the writ are paired: the ṢALĀH is a divine decree with fixed times.
- C10 (20:14 - "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance"): D1's "remember God standing and sitting and on your sides" (4:103) is the POST-ṢALĀH remembrance. The ṢALĀH is followed by continuous remembrance (dhikr) in all postures. The ṢALĀH is the anchor; the dhikr is the continuation.
- B3 (33:43 - "He brings you out of darkness into light"): 4:102's war-prayer (establish the ṢALĀH in shifts with weapons) is the HUMAN counterpart of B3's exodus: the ṢALĀH is established even in the midst of war (darkness). The ṢALĀH brings light into the darkness of war.
- C2 (2:43 - "bow with those who bow"): D1's war-prayer (one group prays while another guards, then they switch) is the COMMUNITY-dimension of the ṢALĀH in its most extreme form. The ṢALĀH is performed in community even in war.
- 4:103 "the Salah was upon the believers a prescribed writ" - the ṢALĀH is for the BELIEVERS. The ṢALĀH is the believers' obligation - the same as C1 (98:5 "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah" - the minimal religion).

FINDING: D1 gives the ṢALĀH's ADAPTABILITY and LEGALITY: the ṢALĀH can be SHORTENED (qaṣr) in travel/fear (4:101), performed IN SHIFTS during war (4:102), and adapted to rain/sickness (4:102). The ṢALĀH is FLEXIBLE: it adapts to ALL circumstances. And the ṢALĀH is a PRESCRIBED WRIT (kitāban mawqūtan): WRITTEN and TIMED - a divine decree with fixed times. The ṢALĀH is both flexible (adapts to circumstances) and fixed (has prescribed times). The post-ṢALĀH remembrance (4:103 "remember God standing and sitting and on your sides") shows that the ṢALĀH is the anchor; the dhikr is the continuation. The ṢALĀH is not the end; it is the BEGINNING of continuous remembrance.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The SHORTENING (qaṣr) - the ṢALĀH can be shortened in travel/fear. HOLD for the final doc's flexibility-table. (ii) The WAR-PRAYER (4:102) - the ṢALĀH is performed in shifts during war. HOLD for the final doc's community-table. (iii) The POST-SALAH REMEMBRANCE (4:103) - after the ṢALĀH, remember God in all postures. HOLD for the final doc's remembrance-table. (iv) The PRESCRIBED WRIT (kitāban mawqūtan) - the ṢALĀH is a written decree with fixed times. HOLD for the final doc's legality-table. (v) The ADAPTABILITY (rain, sickness, travel, war, fear) - the ṢALĀH adapts to ALL circumstances. HOLD for the final doc's flexibility-table. (vi) The ṢALĀH now has EIGHT forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), and adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1). All eight are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D1 (2:3 - those who believe the unseen + establish salah + spend) next.

## D1 - 2:3 - alladhina yu'minuna bi-l-ghaybi wa yuqimu-s-salata wa mimmā razaqnāhum yunfiqun

ARABIC (flow): 2:1 "Alif Lam Mim" -> 2:2 "That is the Book, no doubt in it, a guidance for the righteous (muttaqīn)" -> **2:3 "Those who believe in the unseen and ESTABLISH THE SALAH and spend from what We have provided them"** -> 2:4 "And those who believe in what was sent down to you and what was sent down before you, and of the Hereafter they are certain" -> 2:5 "Those are upon guidance from their Lord, and those are the successful (mufliḥūn)" -> 2:6 "Indeed those who disbelieve, it is the same to them whether you warn them or do not warn them, they will not believe" -> 2:7 "God has sealed their hearts and their hearing, and over their eyes is a covering; and for them is a great punishment" -> 2:8 "And among the people are those who say: we believe in God and the Last Day, but they are not believers."

WORD FORM: yuqīmūna al-ṣalāta (establish the ṣalāh) + yunfiqūn (spend) - the ṢALĀH + SPENDING pair: "establish the ṢALĀH and spend from what We have provided them." The ṢALĀH (standing-state) and the SPENDING (giving) are the TWO acts of the believer.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Baqara (2). The verse sits in the opening of Surah 2: the Book is a guidance for the righteous (2:2); the righteous are defined as those who believe in the unseen, establish the ṢALĀH, and spend from what God provided (2:3); those who believe in the revelation and are certain of the Hereafter (2:4); those are upon guidance and successful (2:5); the disbelievers are sealed (2:6-7); the false claimants (2:8).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE ṢALĀH + SPENDING PAIR: "establish the ṢALĀH and spend from what We have provided them." The ṢALĀH (standing-state, God-ward) and the SPENDING (giving, people-ward) are the TWO acts of the believer. The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-God; the spending is the giving-to-people. The two are paired: you stand toward God AND give to people. The ṢALĀH is not isolated; it is paired with generosity.
- THE ESTABLISH-VERB (yuqīmūna) AGAIN: "establish the ṢALĀH" - the same yuqīmū as C1 (98:5), C2 (2:43), C4 (5:12), C6 (14:37), C10 (20:14), C14 (17:78), C16 (2:238), C17 (11:114), C18 (20:132), C19 (31:17), C20 (33:33), C21 (73:20), D9 (4:101). The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED, not performed. The verb is STABLE across 13 suras.
- "THOSE WHO BELIEVE IN THE UNSEEN" (yuʾminūna bi-l-ghaybi): the ṢALĀH is for those who believe in the unseen (ghayb). The ṢALĀH is an act of faith in the unseen: you stand-toward a God you cannot see, in a standing-state that is not visible. The ṢALĀH is the act of the unseen-believer.
- "FROM WHAT WE HAVE PROVIDED THEM" (mimmā razaqnāhum): the spending is FROM WHAT GOD PROVIDED. The ṢALĀH and the spending are both God-given: God provides the ṢALĀH (the standing-state) and the wealth (the spending). The ṢALĀH is not a human invention; it is a God-given act.
- 2:5 "Those are upon guidance from their Lord, and those are the successful (mufliḥūn)": the ṢALĀH-establishers are GUIDED and SUCCESSFUL. The ṢALĀH is the act of the guided and successful. The same as B2 (ṢALAWĀT upon the patient) and C1 (worst vs best creatures).
- 2:8 "among the people are those who say: we believe in God and the Last Day, but they are not believers" - the ṢALĀH is the DIVIDER between true believers and false claimants. The ṢALĀH is the test of belief: those who establish it are true believers; those who claim belief but do not establish it are not believers. The ṢALĀH is the PROOF of belief.

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): C1's two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + zakah) and D1's two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + spending) are the SAME: the ṢALĀH is paired with GIVING. In C1, the giving is ZAKAH (obligatory charity); in D1, the giving is SPENDING (from what God provided). The ṢALĀH + GIVING pair is STABLE.
- C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site): the SITE where the ṢALĀH is established. The ṢALĀH-establishers (D1) stand at the MUSSALLA (C5).
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (established) IS. The ṢALĀH-establishers (D1) are the fa-ṣallā performers.
- B2 (2:157 - ṢALAWĀT upon the patient): the divine ṢALAWĀT is granted to those who ESTABLISH the ṢALĀH (D1). The human ṢALĀH (established) invites the divine ṢALAWĀT.
- 2:8 "they say: we believe... but they are not believers" - the ṢALĀH is the PROOF of belief. The same as 9:99 (B6: the believers take spending as ṢALAWĀT) vs 9:98 (the disbelievers take spending as loss). The ṢALĀH is the divider between true and false belief.

FINDING: D1 gives the ṢALĀH as the DEFINING act of the believer: "those who believe in the unseen and ESTABLISH THE SALAH and spend from what We have provided them." The ṢALĀH + SPENDING pair is the TWO-act definition of the believer: God-ward (ṢALĀH) and people-ward (spending). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who believes in the unseen (ghayb) - the ṢALĀH is an act of faith in the invisible. And the ṢALĀH is the PROOF of belief: those who claim belief but do not establish the ṢALĀH are not believers (2:8). The ṢALĀH is the DIVIDER between true and false belief.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The ṢALĀH + SPENDING pair (2:3) - the two-act definition of the believer: God-ward (ṢALĀH) and people-ward (spending). HOLD for the final doc's definition-table. (ii) The ṢALĀH as PROOF of belief (2:8) - the ṢALĀH is the test of belief. HOLD for the final doc's test-table. (iii) The ṢALĀH for the UNSEEN-believer (2:3) - the ṢALĀH is an act of faith in the invisible. HOLD for the final doc's faith-table. (iv) The ESTABLISH-verb (yuqīmūna) is STABLE across 13+ suras - the ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED, not performed.

VERDICT: studied - see D2 (2:110 - "establish the ṣalāh and give the zakah") next.
