## D2 - 2:110 - wa-aqimu-s-salata wa-atu-z-zakata

ARABIC (flow): 2:108 "Or do you wish to ask your Messenger as Moses was asked before? And whoever exchanges faith for disbelief has strayed from the right path" -> 2:109 "Many of the People of the Book wish they could turn you back to disbelief after you have believed, out of envy from themselves, after the truth has become clear to them. So pardon and overlook until God brings His command. Indeed God has power over all things" -> **2:110 "AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH. And whatever good you send forth for yourselves, you will find it with God. Indeed God is Seeing of what you do"** -> 2:111 "And they said: no one will enter the Garden except one who is a Jew or a Christian. That is their wishful thinking. Say: bring your proof if you are truthful" -> 2:112 "Yes, whoever submits his face to God and is a doer of good, for him is his reward with his Lord; and no fear shall be on them, nor shall they grieve" -> 2:113 "And the Jews said: the Christians are not on anything; and the Christians said: the Jews are not on anything, while they recite the Book. Thus those who do not know speak the like of their speech. So God will judge between them on the Day of Resurrection concerning that in which they used to differ."

WORD FORM: aqīmū al-ṣalāta (establish the ṣalāh) + ātū al-zakāta (give the zakah) + wa mā tuqaddimū li-anfusikum min khayrin tajidūhu ʿinda Allāhi (whatever good you send forth for yourselves, you will find it with God) + inna Allāha bimā taʿmalūna baṣīr (God is Seeing of what you do).

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Baqara (2). The verse sits in the People-of-the-Book passage (2:108-113): the People of the Book wish to turn you back to disbelief (2:109), ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH (2:110), whatever good you send forth you will find with God (2:110), the People of the Book dispute about who enters the Garden (2:111-113).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE ṢALĀH + ZAKAH PAIR (AGAIN): "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah" - the SAME two-pillar formula as C1 (98:5), C2 (2:43), C3 (2:83), C4 (5:12), C9 (19:55), C10 (20:14), C12 (6:162), C18 (20:132), C19 (31:17), C20 (33:33), C21 (73:20), D1 (2:3). The formula is STABLE across 12+ suras. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is the UNIVERSAL formula of the religion.
- THE SEND-FORTH-AND-FIND (wa mā tuqaddimū li-anfusikum min khayrin tajidūhu ʿinda Allāhi): "whatever good you send forth for yourselves, you will find it with God." The ṢALĀH is a GOOD (khayr) that you SEND FORTH (tuqaddimū) for yourselves, and you will FIND it with God. The ṢALĀH is an INVESTMENT: you send it forth, and you will find it with God. The ṢALĀH is not lost; it is STORED with God. The same as 6:162 ("my ṢALĀH... is for God") - the ṢALĀH is FOR God, and it is FOUND with God.
- "GOD IS SEEING OF WHAT YOU DO" (inna Allāha bimā taʿmalūna baṣīr): God SEES the ṢALĀH. The standing is seen by God. The same as A17's "his Lord was EVER SEEING of him" (84:15) and C13's "You know what we conceal and what we reveal" (14:38). The ṢALĀH is a VISIBLE standing, seen by God.
- 2:112 "whoever submits his face to God (aslama wajhahu li-llāh) and is a doer of good": the ṢALĀH is the act of one who SUBMITS his face to God. The standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) is the submission-of-face. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who faces God. The same s-l-m (surrender) + ṣ-l-w (standing) cousin-stems (B4's ṣallū ʿalayhi wa sallimū taslīman).
- 2:109 "Many of the People of the Book wish they could turn you back to disbelief" - the ṢALĀH is established in the context of HOSTILITY from the People of the Book. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is opposed. The same as C3's covenant-breakers (2:83 "then you turned away") - the ṢALĀH is established in the face of opposition.
- 2:113 "the Jews said: the Christians are not on anything; and the Christians said: the Jews are not on anything" - the ṢALĀH is the UNIFYING act: the ṢALĀH is what unifies; sectarianism divides. The same as 6:159 ("those who divided their religion... you have nothing to do with them"). The ṢALĀH is the anti-sectarian act.

PARALLELS:
- D1 (2:3 - "believe in the unseen and establish the ṢALĀH and spend"): D1's "establish the ṢALĀH and spend" and D2's "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah" are the SAME: the ṢALĀH is paired with GIVING. In D1, the giving is SPENDING (from what God provided); in D2, the giving is ZAKAH (obligatory charity). The ṢALĀH + GIVING pair is STABLE.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula. D2 repeats C1's formula in the People-of-the-Book context.
- C12 (6:162 - "my ṢALĀH... is for God"): D2's "whatever good you send forth, you will find it with God" - the ṢALĀH is FOR God (C12) and FOUND with God (D2). The ṢALĀH is both dedicated (C12) and stored (D2).
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (established) IS. The ṢALĀH is the SEND-FORTH of the standing: you send forth your standing-toward-God, and you find it with God.
- B2 (2:157 - ṢALAWĀT upon the patient): the divine ṢALAWĀT is granted to those who ESTABLISH the ṢALĀH (D2). The human ṢALĀH (established) invites the divine ṢALAWĀT.
- 2:112 "submit his face to God" - the ṢALĀH is the submission-of-face. The same as B4's ṣallū ʿalayhi wa sallimū taslīman (standing-toward + surrendering). The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-God with submission.

FINDING: D2 confirms the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair in the People-of-the-Book context: "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah" - the SAME two-pillar formula. And the ṢALĀH is a GOOD that you SEND FORTH for yourselves and FIND with God. The ṢALĀH is an INVESTMENT: you send it forth, and you will find it with God. The ṢALĀH is not lost; it is STORED with God. And God SEES the ṢALĀH (baṣīr). The ṢALĀH is a visible standing, seen by God. And the ṢALĀH is the act of one who SUBMITS his face to God (2:112) - the standing-toward-God is the submission-of-face.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The SEND-FORTH-AND-FIND (2:110) - the ṢALĀH is a good you send forth and find with God. HOLD for the final doc's investment-table. (ii) The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is STABLE across 13+ suras - the UNIVERSAL formula. HOLD for the final doc's formula-table. (iii) The SUBMISSION-OF-FACE (2:112) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who submits his face to God. HOLD for the final doc's submission-table. (iv) God SEES the ṢALĀH (2:110) - the ṢALĀH is a visible standing. HOLD for the final doc's visibility-table. (v) The ṢALĀH as UNIFYING act (2:113) - the ṢALĀH unifies; sectarianism divides. HOLD for the final doc's unity-table.

VERDICT: studied - see D3 (2:177 - definition of birr: ṣalāh inside a whole character list) next.

## D3 - 2:177 - definition of birr: salah inside a whole character list

ARABIC: 2:177 "Righteousness (birr) is not that you turn your faces toward the east or the west, but righteousness is [in] one who believes in God and the Last Day and the angels and the Book and the prophets, and gives wealth, despite love for it, to relatives, orphans, the needy, the traveler, those who ask, and for freeing slaves; AND ESTABLISHES THE SALAH AND GIVES THE ZAKAH; and those who fulfill their promise when they promise, and those who are patient in poverty and hardship and during battle. Those are the ones who have been truthful, and those are the righteous (muttaqūn)."

WORD FORM: aqāma (established) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + ātā (gave) al-zakāta (the zakah) - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is item 7-8 of a 10-item birr (righteousness) list. The ṢALĀH is embedded in a COMPREHENSIVE CHARACTER LIST.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Baqara (2). The verse defines RIGHTEOUSNESS (birr) as a WHOLE CHARACTER, not just the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is one element among ten: (1) belief in God, (2) Last Day, (3) angels, (4) Book, (5) prophets, (6) giving wealth to relatives/orphans/needy/traveler/askers/slaves, (7) ESTABLISH THE SALAH, (8) GIVE THE ZAKAH, (9) fulfill promises, (10) patience in poverty/hardship/battle.

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE COMPREHENSIVE CHARACTER LIST: the ṢALĀH is item 7-8 of a 10-item list. The ṢALĀH is NOT the whole of righteousness; it is ONE element among ten. But it is a DEFINING element: "those are the ones who have been truthful, and those are the righteous (muttaqūn)." The ṢALĀH is one of the markers of the truthful and the righteous. The ṢALĀH is embedded in belief, giving, promise-keeping, and patience.
- THE ESTABLISH-VERB (aqāma) AGAIN: "establishes the ṢALĀH" - the same aqāma as C1 (98:5), C2 (2:43), etc. The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED, not performed. The verb is STABLE across suras.
- "RIGHTEOUSNESS (birr) IS NOT THAT YOU TURN YOUR FACES TOWARD THE EAST OR THE WEST": the ṢALĀH is NOT just a direction (qibla). Righteousness is not a direction; it is a CHARACTER. The ṢALĀH is part of a character, not a compass-point. The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-God, not the turning-toward-a-direction.
- THE ṢALĀH + ZAKAH PAIR (AGAIN): items 7-8 of the 10-item list. The formula is STABLE. The ṢALĀH and the ZAKAH are the TWO religious pillars within the broader character-list.
- THE PATIENCE (sabirīna) IN THREE CONTEXTS: (1) poverty (al-baʾsāʾ), (2) hardship (al-ḍarrāʾ), (3) battle (ḥīna al-baʾs). The ṢALĀH is paired with patience in ALL THREE contexts. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is patient in poverty, hardship, and battle.
- 2:177 "those are the ones who have been truthful (ṣadaqū), and those are the righteous (muttaqūn)" - the ṢALĀH is the marker of truthfulness and righteousness. The ṢALĀH-establisher is the truthful one and the righteous one. The ṢALĀH is the PROOF of righteousness.

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): C1's two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + ZAKAH) is part of a 10-item list in D3. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is embedded in a broader character. The two-pillar formula is the CORE; the 10-item list is the FULL character.
- C3 (2:83 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah; then you turned away"): C3 showed the turning-away from the ṢALĀH. D3 shows what the ṢALĀH looks like when KEPT: it is part of a 10-item righteous character. The ṢALĀH is the marker of the righteous.
- C19 (31:17 - "establish the ṢALĀH, enjoin right, forbid wrong, be patient"): C19's four-command formula (ṢALĀH + enjoin + forbid + patience) is a subset of D3's 10-item list. The ṢALĀH + patience appear in both. The ṢALĀH is the first command; patience is the last.
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (established) IS. The ṢALĀH is one element of the righteous character.
- B2 (2:157 - ṢALAWĀT upon the patient): the divine ṢALAWĀT is granted to the patient. D3 shows that the ṢALĀH-establisher is also patient (in poverty, hardship, battle). The human patience and the divine ṢALAWĀT are paired.
- 2:177 "those are the ones who have been truthful" - the ṢALĀH is the marker of truthfulness. The same as A19's fa-ṣallā (standing-toward-the-Name is the truthful act).

FINDING: D3 gives the ṢALĀH as ONE ELEMENT of a 10-item RIGHTEOUSNESS-CHARACTER: belief + giving + ESTABLISH SALAH + GIVE ZAKAH + fulfill promises + patience (in poverty, hardship, battle). The ṢALĀH is not the whole of righteousness; it is one element among ten. But it is a DEFINING element: "those are the ones who have been truthful, and those are the righteous." The ṢALĀH is the marker of truthfulness and righteousness. And righteousness is NOT a direction (qibla) but a CHARACTER: belief, giving, ṢALĀH, zakah, promise-keeping, patience. The ṢALĀH is embedded in a whole character - it is not isolated.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The 10-item birr list (2:177) - the ṢALĀH is item 7 of 10. HOLD for the final doc's character-table. (ii) The ṢALĀH as MARKER of truthfulness and righteousness (2:177) - the ṢALĀH-establisher is the truthful and righteous one. HOLD for the final doc's marker-table. (iii) The ṢALĀH is NOT a direction (qibla) but a CHARACTER - the ṢALĀH is embedded in belief, giving, promise-keeping, and patience. HOLD for the final doc's character-table. (iv) The patience (sabirīna) in three contexts (poverty, hardship, battle) - the ṢALĀH is paired with patience in all three. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check).

VERDICT: studied - see D4 (2:277 - those who believe + establish salah + give zakah + no fear nor grief) next.

## D4 - 2:277 - inna alladhina amanu wa 'amilu-s-salihati wa aqamu-s-salata wa atu-z-zakata

ARABIC (flow): 2:275 "Those who consume usury will not stand except as one stands whom Satan has touched by [his] touch - that is because they say: trade is like usury, but God has permitted trade and forbidden usury. So whoever has received an admonition from his Lord and desists may have what is past, and his affair is with God. But whoever returns - those are the companions of the Fire; they abide therein" -> 2:276 "God destroys usury and gives increase to charities; and God does not love every ungrateful sinner" -> **2:277 "Indeed those who believe and do righteous deeds and ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH - for them is their reward with their Lord, and NO FEAR shall be on them, NOR SHALL THEY GRIEVE"** -> 2:278 "O you who believe, fear God and abandon what remains of usury, if you are believers" -> 2:279 "And if you do not, then be informed of a war from God and His Messenger; and if you repent, then for you is the principal of your wealth - you will not be wronged, nor will you wrong" -> 2:280 "And if someone is in hardship, then [let there be] postponement until ease; and if you give as charity, it is better for you, if you know."

WORD FORM: aqāmū (established) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + ātū (gave) al-zakāta (the zakah) - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1's two-pillar formula) with a PROMISE: "for them is their reward with their Lord, and no fear shall be on them, nor shall they grieve."

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Baqara (2). The verse sits in the usury-passage (2:275-280): those who consume usury are like Satan-touched (2:275); God destroys usury and increases charities (2:276); those who believe and do righteous deeds and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah are rewarded, fearless, and griefless (2:277); abandon usury (2:278); if you don't, war from God and His Messenger (2:279); if someone is in hardship, postpone (2:280).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE PROMISE (2:277 "for them is their reward with their Lord, and no fear shall be on them, nor shall they grieve"): the ṢALĀH-establisher is REWARDED (ajr), FEARLESS (lā khawf), and GRIEFLESS (lā ḥuzn). The ṢALĀH is the act that brings reward, fearlessness, and grieflessness. The ṢALĀH is the anti-fear act: the ṢALĀH-establisher has no fear and no grief. The same as B2 (ṢALAWĀT + raḥmah) and B5 (ṢALĀH + sakan) - the ṢALĀH brings a soft-quality (raḥmah/sakan/no-fear/no-grief).
- THE ṢALĀH + ZAKAH PAIR (AGAIN): "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah" - the SAME two-pillar formula as C1 (98:5), C2 (2:43), C3 (2:83), C4 (5:12), C9 (19:55), C10 (20:14), C12 (6:162), C18 (20:132), C19 (31:17), C20 (33:33), C21 (73:20), D1 (2:3), D2 (2:110), D3 (2:177). The formula is STABLE across 14+ suras. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is the UNIVERSAL formula of the religion.
- THE ANTI-USURY PAIR (2:275-277): those who consume usury are like Satan-touched (2:275); those who establish the ṢALĀH and give zakah are rewarded and fearless (2:277). The ṢALĀH is the ALTERNATIVE to usury: standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) vs consuming-riba (usury). The ṢALĀH is the anti-usury act.
- 2:276 "God destroys usury and gives increase to charities" - the ṢALĀH + zakah are the anti-usury pair: usury is destroyed; charities (and the ṢALĀH) are increased. The ṢALĀH is part of the ANTI-USURY package.
- 2:279 "if you do not [abandon usury], then be informed of a war from God and His Messenger" - the ṢALĀH-establisher is protected from the war; the usury-consumer is at war with God and His Messenger. The ṢALĀH is the peace-act; usury is the war-act. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is at peace with God.
- 2:280 "if someone is in hardship, then [let there be] postponement until ease; and if you give as charity, it is better for you" - the ṢALĀH + zakah are paired with MERCY (postponement for the hardship). The ṢALĀH is part of a MERCY-package: the ṢALĀH-establisher is merciful (postpones for the hardship), and God is merciful to him (no fear, no grief).

PARALLELS:
- D1 (2:3 - "believe in the unseen and establish the ṢALĀH and spend"): D1's "establish the ṢALĀH and spend" and D4's "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah" are the SAME: the ṢALĀH is paired with GIVING. In D1, the giving is SPENDING; in D4, the giving is ZAKAH. The ṢALĀH + GIVING pair is STABLE.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula. D4 repeats C1's formula in the usury-context.
- B2 (2:157 - ṢALAWĀT upon the patient + raḥmah): D4's "no fear and no grief" is the God-side's soft-quality for the ṢALĀH-establisher. The ṢALĀH brings fearlessness and grieflessness - the same as B2's raḥmah (mercy) and B5's sakan (tranquility). The ṢALĀH always brings a soft-quality.
- A6 (17:18 - "whoever wants the immediate"): the usury-consumer (2:275) wants the immediate (riba = quick gain); the ṢALĀH-establisher (2:277) is patient and fearless. The ṢALĀH is the anti-immediate act: standing-toward-God vs consuming-riba.
- 2:279 "war from God and His Messenger" - the ṢALĀH-establisher is protected from the war; the usury-consumer is at war. The ṢALĀH is the peace-act; usury is the war-act. The same as B3's exodus (darkness -> light): the ṢALĀH brings peace; usury brings war.

FINDING: D4 gives the ṢALĀH as the ANTI-USURY act: those who consume usury are like Satan-touched (2:275); those who establish the ṢALĀH and give zakah are rewarded, fearless, and griefless (2:277). The ṢALĀH is the ALTERNATIVE to usury: standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) vs consuming-riba (usury). The ṢALĀH is the peace-act; usury is the war-act. And the ṢALĀH brings a SOFT-QUALITY: no fear (lā khawf) and no grief (lā ḥuzn). The same as B2's raḥmah, B5's sakan, C17's sin-removal. The ṢALĀH always brings a soft-quality to the stander. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is the UNIVERSAL formula: stable across 14+ suras.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The ANTI-USURY PAIR (2:275-277) - the ṢALĀH is the alternative to usury. HOLD for the final doc's economic-table. (ii) The SOFT-QUALITY (no fear, no grief) - the ṢALĀH brings fearlessness and grieflessness. HOLD for the final doc's quality-table (raḥmah/sakan/no-fear/no-grief/sin-removal). (iii) The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is STABLE across 14+ suras - the UNIVERSAL formula. HOLD for the final doc's formula-table. (iv) The WAR (2:279) - the ṢALĀH-establisher is protected from the war; the usury-consumer is at war. HOLD for the final doc's war-peace table.

VERDICT: studied - see D5 (2:45 - seek help in patience and salah; heavy except on the humble) next.

## D5 - 2:45 - wa-sta'inu bi-s-sabri wa-s-salati wa-innaha la-kabiratun illa ala-l-khashi'in

ARABIC (flow): 2:44 "Do you command people to righteousness and forget yourselves while you recite the Book? Do you not reason?" -> **2:45 "And seek help in PATIENCE and the SALAH; and it is indeed HEAVY except upon the HUMBLE (khāshiʿīn)"** -> 2:46 "who think that they will MEET their Lord and that they will RETURN to Him."

WORD FORM: staʿīnū (seek help) + bi-l-ṣabri (in patience) + wa-l-ṣalāti (and the ṣalāh) + wa-innahā (and it) + la-kabīratun (is indeed heavy) + illā ʿalā al-khāshiʿīn (except upon the humble). The ṢALĀH is HEAVY (kabīra) except for the humble (khāshiʿ).

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Baqara (2). The verse sits in the Bani-Israel passage (2:44-46): the hypocrisy-charge (2:44), seek help in patience and the ṢALĀH (2:45), the ṢALĀH is heavy except for the humble (2:45), who think they will meet their Lord and return to Him (2:46).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE HEAVY-QUALITY (kabīra): "it is indeed HEAVY except upon the humble." The ṢALĀH is HEAVY (kabīra) - the same heaviness as the fire-stand (A-cluster's imposed standing was a burden). But the God-side ṢALĀH's heaviness is BEARABLE by the humble (khāshiʿ). The ṢALĀH is heavy for the proud and light for the humble - the same act, different weight depending on the STANDER's posture. The ṢALĀH is heavy not because it is difficult but because it is SERIOUS: it is a standing-toward-God, and that weight is felt by those who are humble (they feel the weight of standing before God) and not felt by those who are proud (they do not feel the weight because they do not humble themselves).
- THE PATIENCE-PAIRING (ṣabr + ṢALĀH): "seek help in patience and the ṢALĀH" - the ṢALĀH and patience are paired. The ṢALĀH requires patience; patience makes the ṢALĀH bearable. The same as C18 (20:132 "command your family the ṢALĀH and be steadfast upon it") and 11:115 ("be patient; God does not waste the reward"). The ṢALĀH and patience are the TWO tools of the believer: patience bears the weight; the ṢALĀH is the weight.
- THE HUMBLE (khāshiʿ): the ṢALĀH is light for the humble (khāshiʿ) - those who are humble before God. The same khushūʿ (humility) as 23:2 ("successful are those who are humble in their ṢALĀH") and 17:109 ("it increases them in humility"). The ṢALĀH produces humility; the humble find the ṢALĀH light.
- THE MEET-AND-RETURN ORIENTATION (2:46 "who think that they will MEET their Lord and that they will RETURN to Him"): the ṢALĀH is for those who ORIENT toward MEETING (mulāqāh) and RETURNING (rajaʿa). The same as C2 (2:46, repeated here) and A17 (84:6 "you will MEET Him"). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who faces toward MEETING and RETURNING.
- 2:44 "do you command people to righteousness and forget yourselves while you recite the Book?" - the ṢALĀH is for THEMSELVES, not just for commanding others. The hypocrite commands others to do good but forgets his own ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is a PERSONAL standing, not a public performance.

PARALLELS:
- C2 (2:43 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah and bow with those who bow"): C2 added the community-dimension (bow with those who bow). D5 adds the WEIGHT-dimension (heavy except for the humble). The ṢALĀH has both a community-dimension and a weight-dimension.
- C18 (20:132 - "command your family the ṢALĀH and be steadfast upon it"): C18 showed the STEADFASTNESS (iṣṭabir). D5 shows the PATIENCE (ṣabr) that makes the ṢALĀH bearable. The steadfastness and the patience are paired: you are steadfast UPON the ṢALĀH, and patience makes it bearable.
- A17 (84:6 - "toil toward your Lord and you will MEET Him"): the meet-word (mulāqāh) appears in D5's "who think that they will MEET their Lord." The ṢALĀH is for those who orient toward MEETING. The same meet-word that A17 used for the positive anchor.
- B2 (2:157 - "we belong to God and to Him we return"): the return-word (rajaʿa) appears in D5's "they will RETURN to Him." The ṢALĀH is for those who orient toward RETURNING.
- 23:1-2 (E-cluster - "successful are the believers, those who are humble in their ṢALĀH"): the ṢALĀH produces humility (khushūʿ). D5 shows that the humble find the ṢALĀH light. The ṢALĀH and humility are paired: the ṢALĀH produces humility, and the humble find the ṢALĀH light.

FINDING: D5 gives the ṢALĀH's WEIGHT-DIMENSION: "it is indeed HEAVY except upon the HUMBLE." The ṢALĀH is HEAVY (kabīra) - the same heaviness as the fire-stand (A-cluster's imposed standing was a burden). But the God-side ṢALĀH's heaviness is BEARABLE by the humble (khāshiʿ). The ṢALĀH is heavy for the proud and light for the humble - the same act, different weight depending on the STANDER's posture. The ṢALĀH is heavy not because it is difficult but because it is SERIOUS: it is a standing-toward-God, and that weight is felt by those who are humble. The ṢALĀH is paired with PATIENCE (ṣabr): patience makes the ṢALĀH bearable. And the ṢALĀH is for those who ORIENT toward MEETING and RETURNING (2:46) - the same meet/return grammar as A17 and B2.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The HEAVY-QUALITY (kabīra) - the ṢALĀH is heavy except for the humble. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check: does the ṢALĀH produce humility, which makes it light?). (ii) The PATIENCE-PAIRING (ṣabr + ṢALĀH) - patience makes the ṢALĀH bearable. HOLD for E-cluster. (iii) The HUMBLE (khāshiʿ) - the ṢALĀH is light for the humble. HOLD for E-cluster (23:1-2 "humble in their ṢALĀH"). (iv) The MEET-AND-RETURN orientation (2:46) - the ṢALĀH is for those who face toward MEETING and RETURNING. HOLD as the positive anchor.

VERDICT: studied - see D6 (2:153 - seek help in patience and salah; God with the patient) next.

## D6 - 2:153 - ya ayyuha alladhina amanu sta'inu bi-s-sabri wa-s-salati

ARABIC (flow): **2:153 "O you who believe, seek help in PATIENCE and the SALAH; God is with the PATIENT"** -> 2:154 "And do not say of those killed in the path of God: dead; rather they are alive, but you do not perceive."

WORD FORM: staʿīnū (seek help) + bi-l-ṣabri (in patience) + wa-l-ṣalāti (and the ṣalāh) + inna Allāha maʿa al-ṣābirīn (God is with the patient). The ṢALĀH + PATIENCE pair (same as D5's 2:45) with a NEW element: "God is with the PATIENT."

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Baqara (2). The verse sits in the patience-testing passage (2:153-157): seek help in patience and the ṢALĀH (2:153), God is with the patient (2:153), do not say the killed are dead (2:154), We will test you with fear, hunger, loss (2:155), the patient say "we belong to God and to Him we return" (2:156), upon them are ṢALAWĀT from their Lord and mercy (2:157).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE ṢALĀH + PATIENCE PAIR (AGAIN): "seek help in patience and the ṢALĀH" - the SAME pair as D5 (2:45). The ṢALĀH and patience are the TWO tools of the believer. But D6 adds: "God is with the PATIENT" - the divine presence is with the patient. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is patient; God is with the patient.
- THE DIVINE PRESENCE (maʿa al-ṣābirīn): "God is with the patient" - the ṢALĀH is heavy (D5: kabīra), but God is WITH those who bear it patiently. The ṢALĀH's weight is borne with God's presence. The ṢALĀH is not a solitary burden; it is a shared weight - God is with the stander.
- 2:154 "do not say of those killed in the path of God: dead; rather they are alive" - the ṢALĀH is for those who are willing to be killed in the path of God. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is willing to die for God. The ṢALĀH is not just a ritual; it is a commitment to die for God. The same as 2:156-157: the patient who confess "we belong to God and to Him we return" receive God's ṢALAWĀT and mercy.
- D5 (2:45) said the ṢALĀH is HEAVY except for the humble; D6 says God is WITH the patient. The ṢALĀH is heavy (D5) but God is with those who bear it patiently (D6). The ṢALĀH's weight is borne with God's presence.

PARALLELS:
- D5 (2:45 - "seek help in patience and the ṢALĀH; heavy except on the humble"): D5 gave the WEIGHT-dimension (heavy except for the humble). D6 gives the DIVINE-PRESENCE-dimension (God is with the patient). The ṢALĀH is heavy (D5) but God is with the patient (D6). The weight and the presence are paired.
- B2 (2:157 - ṢALAWĀT upon the patient + raḥmah): D6's "God is with the patient" is the foundation for B2's ṢALAWĀT: God is with the patient (D6) -> God grants ṢALAWĀT to the patient (B2). The divine presence precedes the divine ṢALĀH.
- C18 (20:132 - "command your family the ṢALĀH and be steadfast upon it"): C18 showed the STEADFASTNESS (iṣṭabir). D6 shows the PATIENCE (ṣabr) that makes the ṢALĀH bearable. The steadfastness and the patience are paired.
- A17 (84:6 - "toil toward your Lord and you will MEET Him"): the meet-word (mulāqāh) appears in D5's "who think that they will MEET their Lord." The ṢALĀH is for those who orient toward MEETING.
- 2:154 "do not say of those killed in the path of God: dead" - the ṢALĀH is for those who are willing to die for God. The same as A17's "he deemed he would never return" (84:14) - the fire-people deny return; the ṢALĀH-people are willing to die (return) for God.

FINDING: D6 gives the DIVINE-PRESENCE dimension of the ṢALĀH: "God is with the PATIENT." The ṢALĀH is heavy (D5: kabīra), but God is WITH those who bear it patiently. The ṢALĀH is not a solitary burden; it is a shared weight - God is with the stander. The ṢALĀH is for those who are willing to be killed in the path of God (2:154) - the ṢALĀH is a commitment to die for God. And the ṢALĀH + patience are the TWO tools of the believer: patience bears the weight; the ṢALĀH is the weight; and God is with the patient.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The DIVINE PRESENCE (maʿa al-ṣābirīn) - God is with the patient. HOLD for the final doc's presence-table. (ii) The WILLINGNESS TO DIE (2:154) - the ṢALĀH is for those willing to die for God. HOLD for the final doc's martyrdom-table. (iii) The ṢALĀH + PATIENCE pair (D5, D6) - patience makes the ṢALĀH bearable. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check). (iv) The ṢALĀH now has NINE forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), and patient (borne with divine presence, D6). All nine are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D7 (4:43 - tayammum: deed of states) next.

## D7 - 4:43 - ya ayyuha alladhina amanu la taqarrabu-s-salata wa antum sukarā

ARABIC (flow): 4:42 "On that Day, those who disbelieved and disobeyed the Messenger will wish that the earth were leveled with them; and they will not be able to conceal from God a [single] statement" -> **4:43 "O you who believe, do NOT APPROACH THE SALAH while you are intoxicated until you know what you are saying, nor while you are in a state of major impurity (janābah) except when passing through, until you have washed. And if you are ill or on a journey or one of you comes from the toilet or you have touched women and you do not find water, then seek clean earth and wipe your faces and your hands. Indeed God is Pardoning, Forgiving"** -> 4:44 "Have you not seen those who were given a portion of the Book? They purchase misguidance and wish that you would stray from the path."

WORD FORM: lā taqarrabū (do not approach) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + sukarā (intoxicated) + ḥattā taʿlamū mā taqūlūna (until you know what you are saying) + janāban (major impurity) + ʿābirī sabīlin (passing through) + taghtasilū (you have washed) + marḍā (ill) + safarin (on a journey) + ghāʾiṭ (toilet) + lāmastum al-nisāʾa (touched women) + fa-lam tajidū māʾan (did not find water) + fa-tayammamū (then seek dry ablution) + ṣaʿīdan ṭayyiban (clean earth) + fa-msaḥū bi-wujūhikum wa aydīkum (wipe faces and hands) + ʿafuwwan ghafūran (Pardoning, Forgiving).

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah an-Nisa' (4). The verse sits in the purification-passage (4:43): do not approach the ṢALĀH while intoxicated or in major impurity; if water is unavailable, use dry ablution (tayammum). God is Pardoning, Forgiving.

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE PURITY-GATE (lā taqarrabū al-ṣalāta wa-antum sukarā): "do not APPROACH THE SALAH while intoxicated." The ṢALĀH is NOT to be approached in a state of INTOXICATION (sukrā) or MAJOR IMPURITY (janābah). The ṢALĀH requires a STATE OF PURITY: you must know what you are saying (intoxication impairs knowledge) and you must be washed (janābah requires ghusl). The ṢALĀH is gated on PURITY - the same purity-gate as A12 (56:79 "none touch it except the purified"), C1 (98:5 "making the religion pure for Him"), C5 (2:125 "purify My House"), and C20 (33:33 "remove impurity and purify").
- THE TAYAMMUM (dry ablution): "if you are ill or on a journey or one of you comes from the toilet or you have touched women and you do not find water, then seek clean earth and wipe your faces and your hands." If water is unavailable, use clean earth (ṣaʿīd ṭayyib) and wipe faces and hands. The ṢALĀH is ACCESSIBLE even without water: the ṢALĀH can be performed with dry ablution when water is unavailable. The ṢALĀH is not RIGID; it is FLEXIBLE: it adapts to the availability of water.
- THE MERCY (ʿafuwwan ghafūran): "Indeed God is Pardoning, Forgiving." The ṢALĀH is given with MERCY: God pardons and forgives. The ṢALĀH is accessible even in states of impurity (through tayammum) because God is pardoning and forgiving. The ṢALĀH is a MERCIFUL act: God makes it accessible.
- THE BODY-DIMENSION: the ṢALĀH requires WASHING (ghusl) of the body. The ṢALĀH is a BODY-act: the body must be clean. The same as A3 (4:56 skin-roasting - the skin is the organ of exposure) and A20 (88:4-7 - the complete body-list: drink, food, hearing, furniture). The ṢALĀH is a FULL-BODY act: the body must be clean, and the body must be present (standing, C13). The ṢALĀH is both a BODY-state and a PURITY-state.

PARALLELS:
- A12 (56:79 - "none touch it except the purified"): the purity-gate. D7's tayammum is the MERCIFUL version of the purity-gate: when water is unavailable, clean earth suffices. The purity-gate is not rigid; it adapts.
- C20 (33:33 - "remove impurity and purify"): the purification-package. D7's tayammum is part of the purification-package: the ṢALĀH requires purification, and purification can be achieved with water or earth.
- C5 (2:125 - "purify My House"): the purification-covenant. D7's tayammum is the individual version of the purification-covenant: the House is purified; the individual is purified (by water or earth).
- A15 (74:4 - "purify your garments"): the standing-prescription's purity step. D7's tayammum is the purification step: the garments (body) must be pure before standing.
- D1 (4:101 - "shorten the ṢALĀH in travel/fear"): D1 gave the ṢALĀH's SHORTENING (qaṣr). D7 gives the ṢALĀH's PURIFICATION (tayammum). The ṢALĀH is both shortened (D1) and purified (D7). The ṢALĀH is flexible in BOTH time (shortened) and purity (tayammum).
- D5 (2:45 - "heavy except on the humble"): D5 gave the ṢALĀH's WEIGHT. D7 gives the ṢALĀH's PURITY. The ṢALĀH is both heavy (D5) and pure (D7). The weight and the purity are paired: the heavy act requires a pure body.

FINDING: D7 gives the ṢALĀH's PURITY-DIMENSION: "do not APPROACH THE SALAH while intoxicated or in major impurity." The ṢALĀH is gated on PURITY: you must know what you are saying (no intoxication) and you must be washed (no major impurity). But the gate is MERCIFUL: if water is unavailable, use clean earth (tayammum). The ṢALĀH is ACCESSIBLE: it can be performed with water or earth. The ṢALĀH is both RIGID (requires purity) and FLEXIBLE (adapts to water availability). The ṢALĀH is a FULL-BODY act: the body must be clean (water or earth), and the body must be present (standing, C13). The ṢALĀH is both a BODY-state and a PURITY-state. And God is PARDONING and FORGIVING (4:43) - the ṢALĀH is given with mercy.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The PURITY-GATE (4:43) - the ṢALĀH requires purity (water or earth). HOLD for the final doc's purity-table. (ii) The TAYAMMUM (dry ablution) - the ṢALĀH is accessible even without water. HOLD for the final doc's accessibility-table. (iii) The MERCY (ʿafuwwan ghafūran) - the ṢALĀH is given with mercy. HOLD for the final doc's mercy-table. (iv) The BODY-DIMENSION (4:43 - washing/wiping) - the ṢALĀH is a full-body act. HOLD for the final doc's body-table (A3's skin, A20's body-list, D7's washing). (v) The ṢALĀH now has TEN forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), and pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7). All ten are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D8 (4:77 - "then fighting terrified them") next.

## D8 - 4:77 - alam tara ila alladhina qila lahum kuffu aydiyakum wa aqimu-s-salata wa atu-z-zakata

ARABIC (flow): 4:75 "And what is [the matter] with you that you do not fight in the path of God and for the oppressed among men, women, and children who say: Our Lord, take us out of this city of wrongdoers and appoint for us from Yourself a protector and appoint for us from Yourself a helper" -> 4:76 "Those who believe fight in the path of God, and those who disbelieve fight in the path of the false deity; so fight the allies of Satan; indeed the plot of Satan has always been weak" -> **4:77 "Have you not seen those to whom it was said: RESTRAIN YOUR HANDS, and ESTABLISH THE SALAH and GIVE THE ZAKAH? Then when FIGHTING WAS PRESCRIBED FOR THEM, behold, a group of them fear people as they should fear God, or with greater fear, and they said: Our Lord, why have You prescribed fighting for us? If only You had delayed us for a short while. Say: the enjoyment of this world is little, and the Hereafter is better for one who fears God; and you will not be wronged even as much as a thread"** -> 4:78 "Wherever you are, death will overtake you, even if you are in fortified towers. And if a good comes to them, they say: this is from God; and if an evil afflicts them, they say: this is from you. Say: all is from God. So what is [the matter] with these people that they can hardly understand any speech?" -> 4:79 "What comes to you of good is from God, and what comes to you of evil is from yourself. And We have sent you to the people as a messenger, and sufficient is God as a witness" -> 4:80 "Whoever obeys the Messenger has obeyed God; and whoever turns away - We have not sent you over them as a guardian."

WORD FORM: kuffū (restrain) aydiyakum (your hands) + aqīmū (establish) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + ātū (give) al-zakāta (the zakah) - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1's two-pillar formula) given as a PEACE-TIME command: "restrain your hands and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah." The ṢALĀH is established in PEACE.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah an-Nisa' (4). The verse sits in the fighting-passage (4:75-80): the oppressed (4:75), believers fight in God's path vs disbelievers in the path of the false deity (4:76), RESTRAIN YOUR HANDS AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH, then when fighting is prescribed, they fear people (4:77), death overtakes wherever you are (4:78), good is from God, evil is from yourself (4:79), obey the Messenger (4:80).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE PEACE-TIME COMMAND: "restrain your hands and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah" - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is given as a PEACE-TIME command: RESTRAIN YOUR HANDS (do not fight) AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH. The ṢALĀH is established in PEACE, not in war. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is at PEACE: standing-toward-God instead of fighting people. The ṢALĀH is the anti-war act.
- THE FEAR-OF-PEOPLE vs FEAR-OF-GOD: "then when fighting was prescribed for them, behold, a group of them fear people as they should fear God, or with greater fear." The ṢALĀH-establishers are supposed to FEAR GOD, not people. But when fighting is prescribed, they fear PEOPLE more than God. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears God MORE than people. The ṢALĀH is the anti-fear act: standing-toward-God instead of fearing people.
- "Say: the enjoyment of this world is little, and the Hereafter is better for one who fears God" - the ṢALĀH is for one who FEARS God, not people. The ṢALĀH-establisher fears God; the fighter fears people. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears God MORE than people. The same fear-economy as B7 (9:84 "do not s-l-l on them" - the ṢALĀH is withheld from those who fear people instead of God).
- 4:76 "Those who believe fight in the path of God, and those who disbelieve fight in the path of the false deity" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who fights in the path of God, not in the path of the false deity. The ṢALĀH and fighting in God's path are paired: the ṢALĀH-establisher fights in God's path.
- 4:75 "what is [the matter] with you that you do not fight in the path of God and for the oppressed" - the ṢALĀH is for the oppressed: the ṢALĀH-establisher fights for the oppressed. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who fights for justice.
- 4:79 "What comes to you of good is from God, and what comes to you of evil is from yourself" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who attributes good to God and evil to himself. The ṢALĀH is the humility-act.

PARALLELS:
- D1 (4:101-103 - war prayer: shortening, shifts, prescribed writ): D1 gave the ṢALĀH's ADAPTABILITY (shortened in fear, performed in shifts during war). D8 gives the ṢALĀH's PEACE-TIME command (restrain your hands and establish the ṢALĀH). The ṢALĀH is both established in peace (D8) and adapted in war (D1). The ṢALĀH is the peace-act that adapts to war.
- D4 (2:277 - "those who believe and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah: no fear, no grief"): D4 gave the ṢALĀH's PROMISE (no fear, no grief). D8 gives the ṢALĀH's FEAR-OF-GOD (fear God, not people). The ṢALĀH-establisher fears God (D8) and has no fear (D4). The fear-of-God replaces the fear-of-people.
- D5 (2:45 - "heavy except on the humble"): D5 gave the ṢALĀH's WEIGHT. D8 gives the ṢALĀH's FEAR-DIMENSION: the ṢALĀH is for one who fears God. The ṢALĀH is heavy (D5) and fearful (D8) - the same act, different dimensions.
- B7 (9:84 - "do not s-l-l on them"): B7's withholding of ṢALĀH from the disbelievers matches D8's ṢALĀH for the believers. The ṢALĀH is granted to believers and withheld from disbelievers.
- A6 (17:18 - "whoever wants the immediate"): D8's "the enjoyment of this world is little" matches A6's immediate-preference. The ṢALĀH-establisher prefers the Hereafter; the usury-consumer (D4) prefers the immediate.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula. D8 repeats C1's formula in the fighting-context.

FINDING: D8 gives the ṢALĀH as the PEACE-ACT: "restrain your hands and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah." The ṢALĀH is established in PEACE, not in war. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is at peace: standing-toward-God instead of fighting people. And the ṢALĀH is for one who FEARS GOD, not people: "the Hereafter is better for one who fears God." The ṢALĀH is the anti-fear act: the ṢALĀH-establisher fears God; the fighter fears people. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is the UNIVERSAL formula, even in the fighting-context.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The PEACE-ACT (4:77) - the ṢALĀH is established in peace, not in war. HOLD for the final doc's peace-table. (ii) The FEAR-OF-GOD vs FEAR-OF-PEOPLE (4:77) - the ṢALĀH is for one who fears God, not people. HOLD for the final doc's fear-table. (iii) The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is STABLE across 15+ suras - the UNIVERSAL formula. HOLD for the final doc's formula-table. (iv) The ṢALĀH as protection against the Fire (11:113) and the ṢALĀH as peace-act (4:77) - the ṢALĀH is both protection and peace. HOLD for the final doc's protection-table.

VERDICT: studied - see D9 (already studied ahead: 4:101-103 - war prayer) - next canonical is D10? No, D9-D11 are done. Next unstudied is D12 (4:162).

## D12 - 4:162 - wal-muqimina-s-salata wa-l-mu'tina-z-zakata

ARABIC (flow): 4:160 "For wrongdoing on the part of the Jews, We made unlawful for them [certain] good foods which had been lawful for them, and for their averting from the way of God many [people]" -> 4:161 "And [for] their taking of usury while they had been forbidden from it, and their consuming the people's wealth unjustly. And We have prepared for the disbelievers among them a painful punishment" -> **4:162 "But the FIRM (rāsikhūn) in knowledge among them, and the believers, believe in what has been sent down to you and what was sent down before you, AND THE ESTABLISHERS OF THE SALAH AND THE GIVERS OF THE ZAKAH, and the believers in God and the Last Day - to those We will give a great reward"** -> 4:163-164 "Indeed We have revealed to you as We revealed to Noah and the prophets after him; and We revealed to Abraham, Isma'il, Isaac, Jacob, and the Tribes, and Jesus, Job, Jonah, Aaron, and Solomon; and We gave David the Psalms... And [We sent] messengers about whom We have related to you before and messengers about whom We have not related to you" -> 4:165 "Messengers as bringers of good news and warners, so that mankind will have no argument against God after the messengers. And God is Exalted in Might and Wise."

WORD FORM: al-muqīmīna (establishers, active participle plural) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + al-muʾtīna (givers) al-zakāta (the zakah) - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1's two-pillar formula) now with FOUR categories of believers: (1) the firm in knowledge (rāsikhūn), (2) the believers, (3) THE ESTABLISHERS OF THE SALAH AND THE GIVERS OF THE ZAKAH, (4) the believers in God and the Last Day.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah an-Nisa' (4). The verse sits in the People-of-the-Book passage (4:160-165): the Jews' wrongdoing (4:160), usury-taking (4:161), the firm in knowledge and the believers who believe in the revelation and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah (4:162), the prophetic chain (4:163-164), the messengers as bringers of good news and warners (4:165).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE FOUR CATEGORIES: (1) rāsikhūn fī al-ʿilmi (firm in knowledge), (2) muʾminūn (believers), (3) muqīmū al-ṣalāti wa muʾtū al-zakāta (establishers of the ṢALĀH and givers of the zakah), (4) muʾminūna bi-llāhi wa-l-yawmi al-ākhiri (believers in God and the Last Day). The ṢALĀH-establishers are a DISTINCT category among the believers - they are specifically named as "establishers of the ṢALĀH and givers of the zakah."
- THE KNOWLEDGE-PAIRING (rāsikhūna fī al-ʿilmi): the ṢALĀH-establishers are paired with KNOWLEDGE (ʿilm). The ṢALĀH is established by those who are FIRM in knowledge. The ṢALĀH requires knowledge - the same as C1 (98:5 "making the religion pure for Him" - purity requires knowledge). The ṢALĀH is not blind; it is knowledgeable.
- "TO THOSE WE WILL GIVE A GREAT REWARD" (sanuʾtīhim ajran ʿaẓīman): the ṢALĀH-establishers receive a GREAT REWARD. The ṢALĀH is rewarded by God. The same as D4 (2:277 "for them is their reward with their Lord") and C1 (98:7 "those are the best of creatures").
- 4:163-165 "Indeed We have revealed to you as We revealed to Noah and the prophets after him... messengers as bringers of good news and warners" - the ṢALĀH-covenant is given through the PROPHETIC CHAIN. The ṢALĀH is part of the prophetic tradition (C8, C9, C10, C12). The ṢALĀH is not a new invention; it is the prophetic tradition.
- 4:161 "they took usury" - the ṢALĀH-establishers are contrasted with the usury-takers (4:161). The ṢALĀH is the alternative to usury - the same as D4 (2:275-277). The ṢALĀH-establisher does not consume usury; he establishes the ṢALĀH.
- 4:160 "for wrongdoing on the part of the Jews, We made unlawful for them [certain] good foods" - the ṢALĀH-establishers are contrasted with the wrongdoers. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who does not wrong.

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + ZAKAH) in C1 and D12. The formula is STABLE across suras. D12 adds the KNOWLEDGE-dimension (rāsikhūna fī al-ʿilmi).
- D4 (2:277 - "those who believe and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah: no fear, no grief"): D4 gave the PROMISE (no fear, no grief). D12 gives the REWARD (great reward). The ṢALĀH-establisher is both fearless/griefless (D4) and greatly rewarded (D12).
- C3 (2:83 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah; then you turned away"): C3 showed the turning-away from the ṢALĀH. D12 shows the ṢALĀH-establishers who did NOT turn away - they are the firm in knowledge and the believers. The ṢALĀH is the act of those who did not turn away.
- D2 (2:110 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah; whatever good you send forth you will find with God"): D2 gave the SEND-FORTH (tuqaddimū). D12 gives the REWARD (ajran ʿaẓīman). The ṢALĀH is sent forth (D2) and rewarded (D12).
- 4:163-165 (prophetic chain): the ṢALĀH-covenant is given through the prophetic chain. The ṢALĀH is part of the prophetic tradition (C8, C9, C10, C12, D12).

FINDING: D12 shows the ṢALĀH-establishers as a DISTINCT CATEGORY among the believers: "the firm in knowledge, and the believers, AND THE ESTABLISHERS OF THE SALAH AND THE GIVERS OF THE ZAKAH, and the believers in God and the Last Day." The ṢALĀH-establishers are SPECIFICALLY NAMED - they are not just "believers" but "establishers of the ṢALĀH." And they are paired with KNOWLEDGE (rāsikhūna fī al-ʿilmi): the ṢALĀH requires knowledge. The ṢALĀH is not blind; it is knowledgeable. And the ṢALĀH-establishers receive a GREAT REWARD. The ṢALĀH is rewarded by God.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The FOUR CATEGORIES (4:162) - the ṢALĀH-establishers are a distinct category among the believers. HOLD for the final doc's category-table. (ii) The KNOWLEDGE-PAIRING (rāsikhūna fī al-ʿilmi) - the ṢALĀH requires knowledge. HOLD for the final doc's knowledge-table. (iii) The REWARD (ajran ʿaẓīman) - the ṢALĀH-establishers receive a great reward. HOLD for the final doc's reward-table. (iv) The PROPHETIC CHAIN (4:163-165) - the ṢALĀH-covenant is given through the prophetic chain. HOLD for C-cluster's covenant-tradition.

VERDICT: studied - see D13 (5:6 - ablution for salah) next.

## D13 - 5:6 - idha qumtum ila-s-salati fa-ghsilu wujuhakum

ARABIC (flow): **5:6 "O you who believe, when you RISE TO THE SALAH, then WASH your faces and your hands up to the elbows, and WIPE your heads and your feet up to the ankles. And if you are in a state of major impurity (janābah), then PURIFY yourselves. And if you are ill or on a journey or one of you comes from the toilet or you have touched women and you do not find water, then seek clean earth and wipe your faces and your hands from it. God does not intend to make difficulty for you, but He intends to PURIFY you and to COMPLETE His favor upon you, that you may be grateful"** -> 5:7 "And remember the favor of God upon you and His covenant which He bound you with when you said: we hear and we obey. And fear God; indeed God is Knowing of what is in the breasts."

WORD FORM: idhā qumtum (when you rise) ilā al-ṣalāti (to the ṣalāh) + fa-ghsilū (then wash) wujūhakum (your faces) + wa aydiyakum (and your hands) + ilā al-marāfiqi (up to the elbows) + wa-msaḥū (and wipe) bi-ruʾūsikum (your heads) + wa arjulakum (and your feet) + ilā al-kaʿbayni (up to the ankles) + janāban (major impurity) + ṭahharū (purify) + tayammamū (seek dry ablution) + ṣaʿīdan ṭayyiban (clean earth) + ṭahhirakum (to purify you) + niʿmatahu (His favor).

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Ma'ida (5). The verse sits in the purification-passage (5:6-7): wash before the ṢALĀH (5:6), if major impurity, purify (5:6), if no water, use clean earth (5:6), God intends to purify you, not to make difficulty (5:6), remember the favor and the covenant (5:7).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE WASHING (wuḍūʾ): "when you RISE TO THE SALAH, then WASH your faces and your hands up to the elbows, and WIPE your heads and your feet up to the ankles." The ṢALĀH requires WASHING (wuḍūʾ): faces, hands (to elbows), heads (wipe), feet (to ankles). The ṢALĀH is a BODY-act: the body must be washed before standing. The ṢALĀH is preceded by a PURIFICATION ritual. The same body-dimension as A3 (4:56 skin-roasting - the skin is the organ of exposure) and A20 (88:4-7 - the complete body-list).
- THE MAJOR IMPURITY (janābah): "if you are in a state of major impurity, then PURIFY yourselves." Major impurity (janābah) requires full purification (ghusl). The ṢALĀH requires full purification for major impurity. The ṢALĀH is gated on PURITY - the same purity-gate as A12 (56:79 "none touch it except the purified"), C1 (98:5 "making the religion pure for Him"), C5 (2:125 "purify My House"), and C20 (33:33 "remove impurity and purify").
- THE TAYAMMUM (dry ablution): "if you are ill or on a journey or one of you comes from the toilet or you have touched women and you do not find water, then seek clean earth and wipe your faces and your hands from it." If water is unavailable, use clean earth (ṣaʿīd ṭayyib) and wipe faces and hands. The ṢALĀH is ACCESSIBLE even without water: the ṢALĀH can be performed with dry ablution when water is unavailable. The ṢALĀH is not RIGID; it is FLEXIBLE: it adapts to the availability of water. The same adaptability as D1 (qaṣr in travel/fear), D7 (tayammum in 4:43), and 2:239 (on foot or riding).
- THE MERCY ("God does not intend to make difficulty for you, but He intends to PURIFY you and to COMPLETE His favor upon you"): the ṢALĀH's purification is a FAVOR from God, not a burden. God intends to PURIFY you, not to make difficulty. The ṢALĀH is given with MERCY - the same as D7 (4:43 "Indeed God is Pardoning, Forgiving"). The ṢALĀH is a MERCIFUL act: God makes it accessible.
- 5:7 "remember the favor of God upon you and His covenant which He bound you with when you said: we hear and we obey" - the ṢALĀH's washing is part of the COVENANT: "we hear and we obey." The ṢALĀH is a COVENANT-act. The same as C5 (2:125 "We covenanted to Abraham and Isma'il: purify My House") and C3 (2:83 "We took a covenant from the Children of Israel").

PARALLELS:
- D7 (4:43 - "do not approach the ṢALĀH while intoxicated or in major impurity; if no water, use clean earth"): D7 gave the ṢALĀH's PURITY-GATE (lā taqarrabū). D13 gives the ṢALĀH's WASHING (wuḍūʾ) in detail: wash faces, hands, wipe heads, feet. D7 and D13 are the SAME purification-passage: 4:43 (do not approach while impure) and 5:6 (wash before rising to the ṢALĀH). The ṢALĀH requires purity, and purity is achieved by washing (or tayammum).
- C20 (33:33 - "remove impurity and purify"): D13's washing (wuḍūʾ) is the individual version of C20's purification-package. The ṢALĀH removes impurity (C20) by washing (D13).
- C5 (2:125 - "purify My House"): the House is purified; the individual is purified (by water or earth). The standing-site (MUSSALLA) and the standing-act (ṢALĀH) are both gated on purity.
- A12 (56:79 - "none touch it except the purified"): the purity-gate. D13's washing is the MERCIFUL version: when water is unavailable, clean earth suffices. The purity-gate is not rigid; it adapts.
- D1 (4:101 - "shorten the ṢALĀH in travel/fear"): D1 gave the ṢALĀH's SHORTENING (qaṣr). D13 gives the ṢALĀH's WASHING (wuḍūʾ). The ṢALĀH is both shortened (D1) and washed (D13). The ṢALĀH is flexible in BOTH time (shortened) and purity (washing/tayammum).
- A3 (4:56 - "skin-roasting detail"): the skin is the organ of exposure. D13's washing (of faces, hands, heads, feet) is the preparation of the body for the standing-exposure. The body is washed before standing before God.

FINDING: D13 gives the ṢALĀH's WASHING-DIMENSION (wuḍūʾ): "when you RISE TO THE SALAH, then WASH your faces and your hands up to the elbows, and WIPE your heads and your feet up to the ankles." The ṢALĀH requires WASHING: the body must be clean before standing. The ṢALĀH is a FULL-BODY act: the body is washed (faces, hands, heads, feet) before standing. And if water is unavailable, clean earth suffices (tayammum). The ṢALĀH is both RIGID (requires washing) and FLEXIBLE (adapts to water availability). And God is PARDONING and FORGIVING (5:6) - the ṢALĀH's washing is a FAVOR, not a burden. The ṢALĀH is given with MERCY.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The WASHING (wuḍūʾ) - the ṢALĀH requires washing of faces, hands, heads, feet. HOLD for the final doc's body-table (A3's skin + A20's body-list + D13's washing). (ii) The TAYAMMUM (dry ablution) - the ṢALĀH is accessible even without water. HOLD for the final doc's accessibility-table. (iii) The MERCY (ʿafuwwan ghafūran) - the ṢALĀH is given with mercy. HOLD for the final doc's mercy-table. (iv) The COVENANT ("we hear and we obey", 5:7) - the ṢALĀH's washing is part of the covenant. HOLD for C-cluster's covenant-tradition. (v) The ṢALĀH now has ELEVEN forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), and washed (wuḍūʾ, D13). All eleven are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D14 (5:55 - ???) next.

## D14 - 5:55 - alladhina yuqimu-s-salata wa yu'tuna-z-zakata wa hum raki'un

ARABIC (flow): 5:54 "O you who believe, whoever of you turns back from his religion - God will bring a people whom He loves and who love Him, humble toward the believers, powerful against the disbelievers; they strive in the path of God and do not fear the blame of a blamer. That is the favor of God; He gives it to whom He wills. And God is All-Encompassing, Knowing" -> **5:55 "YOUR ALLY IS ONLY GOD AND HIS MESSENGER AND THOSE WHO BELIEVE - THOSE WHO ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH WHILE THEY BOW (rākiʿūn)"** -> 5:56 "And whoever takes God and His Messenger and those who believe as allies - indeed the party of God - they are the victorious" -> 5:57 "O you who believe, do not take as allies those who take your religion as ridicule and amusement from among those who were given the Book before you and the disbelievers; and fear God if you are believers."

WORD FORM: yuqīmūna (establish) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + yuʾtūna (give) al-zakāta (the zakah) + wa hum rākiʿūn (while they bow) - the ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1's two-pillar formula) now with a POSTURE: "while they BOW (rākiʿūn)." The ṢALĀH is established WHILE BOWING.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Ma'ida (5). The verse sits in the ally-passage (5:54-57): God will bring a people whom He loves (5:54), YOUR ALLY IS ONLY GOD AND HIS MESSENGER AND THOSE WHO BELIEVE - THOSE WHO ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH WHILE THEY BOW (5:55), the party of God is victorious (5:56), do not take as allies those who mock your religion (5:57).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE BOWING-POSTURE (rākiʿūn): "while they BOW (rākiʿūn)" - the ṢALĀH is established WHILE BOWING. The ṢALĀH includes rukūʿ (bowing) as part of its physical form. The ṢALĀH is not just standing (qiyām); it includes bowing (rukūʿ). The same q-w-m root (to stand) + r-k-ʿ (to bow). The ṢALĀH is a standing-AND-bowing act.
- THE ALLY-MARKER: "your ally is only God and His Messenger and those who believe - those who establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah while they bow." The ṢALĀH-establishers are the ALLIES (awliyāʾ) of God and His Messenger. The ṢALĀH is the marker of ALLIANCE with God. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is allied with God.
- 5:54 "God will bring a people whom He loves and who love Him" - the ṢALĀH-establishers are the people whom God loves. The ṢALĀH is the act of those whom God loves. The same love-economy as B2 (ṢALAWĀT upon the patient) and B4 (ṢALĀH on the Prophet).
- 5:56 "the party of God - they are the victorious" - the ṢALĀH-establishers are the party of God (ḥizb Allāh) and they are VICTORIOUS. The ṢALĀH is the act of the victorious party. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is on God's side.
- 5:57 "do not take as allies those who take your religion as ridicule and amusement" - the ṢALĀH is the marker of true alliance: those who establish the ṢALĀH are true allies; those who mock the religion are false allies. The ṢALĀH is the divider between true and false allies.
- THE ṢALĀH + ZAKAH PAIR (AGAIN): "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah" - the SAME two-pillar formula as C1 (98:5), C2 (2:43), etc. The formula is STABLE. D14 adds the BOWING-posture (rākiʿūn) as a qualifier.

PARALLELS:
- C13 (3:39 - "Zakariyya standing-praying in the miḥrāb"): the STANDING-POSTURE (qāʾim) is the physical form; D14's BOWING-POSTURE (rākiʿ) is the OTHER physical form. The ṢALĀH has BOTH standing (qiyām) and bowing (rukūʿ). The ṢALĀH is a standing-AND-bowing act. The same as C2's "bow with those who bow" (rukūʿ maʿa al-rākiʿīn).
- C2 (2:43 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah and bow with those who bow"): C2 added the community-dimension (bow with those who bow). D14 adds the ALLY-dimension (those who establish the ṢALĀH while bowing are God's allies). The ṢALĀH is both a community-act and an ally-marker.
- D8 (4:77 - "restrain your hands and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): D8 gave the ṢALĀH as a PEACE-ACT. D14 gives the ṢALĀH as an ALLY-MARKER. The ṢALĀH is both a peace-act and an ally-marker.
- B4 (33:56 - "ṢALLŪ ʿALAYHI and SALLIMŪ TASLĪMAN"): the s-l-w + s-l-m pairing (standing-toward + surrendering). D14's ṢALĀH + ZAKAH + BOWING (rukūʿ) is a three-part physical formula: standing + giving + bowing. The ṢALĀH is a multi-posture act.
- 5:54 "God will bring a people whom He loves" - the ṢALĀH-establishers are the people whom God loves. The same as 9:99 (B6 - "God will admit them to His mercy") and 9:103 (B5 - "your ṣalāh is their tranquility"). The ṢALĀH is the act of those whom God loves.

FINDING: D14 gives the ṢALĀH's BOWING-DIMENSION: "those who establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah WHILE THEY BOW (rākiʿūn)." The ṢALĀH includes rukūʿ (bowing) as part of its physical form. The ṢALĀH is not just standing (qiyām); it is standing-AND-bowing. And the ṢALĀH-establishers are God's ALLIES (awliyāʾ): "your ally is only God and His Messenger and those who believe - those who establish the ṢALĀH." The ṢALĀH is the marker of ALLIANCE with God. The ṢALĀH-establishers are the party of God (ḥizb Allāh) and they are VICTORIOUS. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is on God's side.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The BOWING-POSTURE (rākiʿūn) - the ṢALĀH includes rukūʿ (bowing). The ṢALĀH is standing-AND-bowing. HOLD for the final doc's posture-table (C13 standing + D14 bowing). (ii) The ALLY-MARKER (awliyāʾ) - the ṢALĀH-establishers are God's allies. HOLD for the final doc's alliance-table. (iii) The PARTY-OF-GOD (ḥizb Allāh) - the ṢALĀH-establishers are the party of God and they are victorious. HOLD for the final doc's victory-table. (iv) The ṢALĀH now has TWELVE forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), and bowing (rukūʿ, D14). All twelve are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D15 (5:58 - the call as identity marker) next.

## D15 - 5:58 - wa-idha nadaytum ila-s-salati attakhadh-uha huzwan wa la'ban

ARABIC (flow): 5:57 "O you who believe, do not take as allies those who take your religion as ridicule and amusement from among those who were given the Book before you and the disbelievers; and fear God if you are believers" -> **5:58 "And when you CALL TO THE SALAH, they take it as ridicule and amusement. That is because they are a people who do not reason"** -> 5:59 "Say, O People of the Book, do you resent us except that we believe in God and what has been revealed to us and what was revealed before, and because most of you are transgressors?"

WORD FORM: nādaytum (you call) ilā al-ṣalāti (to the ṣalāh) + attakhadhūhā (they take it) huzwan (as ridicule) wa laʿban (and amusement) + dhālika (that) bi-annahum (because they are) qawmun (a people) lā yaʿqilūn (who do not reason).

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Ma'ida (5). The verse sits in the ally-passage (5:54-59): God will bring a people whom He loves (5:54), your ally is God and His Messenger and those who establish the ṢALĀH (5:55), the party of God is victorious (5:56), do not take as allies those who mock your religion (5:57), when you CALL TO THE SALAH they take it as ridicule and amusement (5:58), say: do you resent us except that we believe? (5:59).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE CALL (nidāʾ ilā al-ṣalāh): "when you CALL TO THE SALAH" - the ṢALĀH has a CALL (nidāʾ). The ṢALĀH is ANNOUNCED: there is a public call to the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is not a private act; it is a PUBLIC act, announced to the community. The call (nidāʾ) is the adhān (the call to prayer). The ṢALĀH is proclaimed.
- THE MOCKERY (huzw + laʿb): "they take it as ridicule and amusement" - the disbelievers MOCK the call to the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH-call is mocked by the disbelievers. The ṢALĀH is the IDENTITY MARKER of the believers: those who respond to the call are believers; those who mock it are disbelievers. The ṢALĀH is the divider between believers and mockers.
- "THAT IS BECAUSE THEY ARE A PEOPLE WHO DO NOT REASON" (dhālika bi-annahum qawmun lā yaʿqilūn): the mockers do not REASON (yaʿqilūn). The ṢALĀH is the act of the REASONING ones. The ṢALĀH is the act of those who reason: the ṢALĀH is a reasonable act, and those who mock it are unreasonable.
- 5:57 "do not take as allies those who take your religion as ridicule and amusement" - the ṢALĀH-call is the IDENTITY MARKER: those who mock the call are NOT allies. The ṢALĀH is the divider between allies and non-allies. The same as D14 (5:55 - the ṢALĀH-establishers are God's allies).
- 5:59 "Say, O People of the Book, do you resent us except that we believe in God..." - the ṢALĀH-call is the marker of BELIEF: the mockers resent the believers because they believe. The ṢALĀH is the act of belief.

PARALLELS:
- D14 (5:55 - "those who establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah while they bow"): D14 gave the ṢALĀH as the ALLY-MARKER (awliyāʾ). D15 gives the ṢALĀH-CALL as the IDENTITY-MARKER: those who mock the call are not allies. The ṢALĀH is both an ally-marker (D14) and an identity-marker (D15).
- D13 (5:6 - "when you rise to the ṢALĀH, wash..."): D13 gave the ṢALĀH's WASHING (wuḍūʾ). D15 gives the ṢALĀH's CALL (nidāʾ). The ṢALĀH is both a washed act (D13) and a called act (D15). The washing and the calling are paired: you wash before the ṢALĀH, and the ṢALĀH is called publicly.
- 5:57 "do not take as allies those who take your religion as ridicule" - the ṢALĀH-call is the identity marker. The same as 9:99 (B6 - "those who believe... take what they spend as ṢALAWĀT of the Messenger") vs 9:98 (those who take spending as loss). The ṢALĀH is the divider between believers and mockers.
- C11 (11:87 - "Does your ṢALĀH command you?"): the mockers' view of the ṢALĀH as a commanding-force. D15's mockers take the CALL as ridicule - the mockers see the ṢALĀH as something to be mocked, not obeyed. The ṢALĀH is both a commanding-force (C11) and a mocked-call (D15) - the same ṢALĀH, two views.
- A7 (19:59 - "they lost the ṢALĀH and followed desires"): the ṢALĀH-loss is the opposite of the ṢALĀH-call: the mockers mock the call; the ṢALĀH-loser loses the ṢALĀH. Both are forms of REJECTION of the ṢALĀH.

FINDING: D15 gives the ṢALĀH's CALL-DIMENSION (nidāʾ): "when you CALL TO THE SALAH, they take it as ridicule and amusement." The ṢALĀH is ANNOUNCED: there is a public call (nidāʾ/adhān) to the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is not a private act; it is a PUBLIC act, announced to the community. And the CALL is the IDENTITY MARKER: those who respond to the call are believers; those who mock it are disbelievers. The ṢALĀH is the divider between believers and mockers. And the mockers "do not reason" (lā yaʿqilūn) - the ṢALĀH is the act of the REASONING ones.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The CALL (nidāʾ) - the ṢALĀH is announced publicly. HOLD for the final doc's call-table. (ii) The MOCKERY (huzw + laʿb) - the disbelievers mock the ṢALĀH-call. HOLD for the final doc's rejection-table. (iii) The IDENTITY-MARKER - the ṢALĀH-call divides believers from mockers. HOLD for the final doc's identity-table. (iv) The REASONING (yaʿqilūn) - the ṢALĀH is the act of the reasoning ones. HOLD for the final doc's reason-table. (v) The ṢALĀH now has THIRTEEN forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), and called (publicly announced, D15). All thirteen are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D16 (5:91 - ???) next.

## D16 - 5:91 - yaṣuddakum ʿan dhikri Allāhi wa ʿani al-ṣalāti

ARABIC (flow): 5:90 "O you who believe, intoxicants and gambling and stone altars and divining arrows are only an abomination from the work of Satan, so avoid them, that you may be successful" -> **5:91 "Satan only wants to cause between you animosity and hatred through intoxicants and gambling and to AVERT YOU FROM THE REMEMBRANCE OF GOD AND FROM THE SALAH. So will you not desist?"** -> 5:92 "And obey God and obey the Messenger, and beware. And if you turn away, then know that upon Our Messenger is only the clear conveyance."

WORD FORM: yaṣuddakum (he averts you) ʿan (from) dhikri Allāhi (the remembrance of God) wa ʿani (and from) al-ṣalāti (the ṣalāh) + fa-hal antum muntahūn (so will you not desist?).

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Ma'ida (5). The verse sits in the intoxicants-passage (5:90-92): intoxicants and gambling are an abomination from Satan (5:90), Satan wants to cause animosity and hatred through them and to avert you from the remembrance of God and from the ṢALĀH (5:91), obey God and His Messenger (5:92).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE AVERTING (yaṣuddakum ʿan): "Satan wants to AVERT you FROM the remembrance of God AND FROM THE SALAH." The ṢALĀH is what Satan wants to AVERT you from. The ṢALĀH is the TARGET of Satan's aversion. Satan uses intoxicants and gambling as the MEANS; the ṢALĀH is the TARGET. The ṢALĀH is the act that Satan opposes.
- THE DHIKR + SALAH PAIR (ʿan dhikri Allāhi wa ʿani al-ṣalāti): "avert you from the remembrance of God and from the ṢALĀH." The ṢALĀH IS the remembrance (C10's "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance"). Satan wants to avert you from BOTH: from dhikr AND from ṢALĀH - but they are the SAME thing. Satan wants to avert you from the ṢALĀH because the ṢALĀH IS the remembrance.
- 5:90 "intoxicants and gambling... are an abomination from the work of Satan, so avoid them, that you may be successful" - the ṢALĀH is the alternative to intoxicants and gambling. The ṢALĀH is the SUCCESS-act; intoxicants and gambling are the ABOMINATION-act. The ṢALĀH is the path to success (tuflīḥūn).
- 5:92 "obey God and obey the Messenger" - the ṢALĀH is the act of obedience. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who obeys God and His Messenger.
- The ṢALĀH is the act that Satan opposes: Satan uses intoxicants and gambling to avert you from the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the anti-Satan act: standing-toward-God vs following Satan's abomination. The same opposition as C10 (the ṢALĀH vs desire/shahwa): the ṢALĀH is the God-ward act; intoxicants/gambling/desire are the self-ward acts.

PARALLELS:
- C10 (20:14 - "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance"): C10's li-dhikrī (for My remembrance) and D16's ʿan dhikri Allāhi (from the remembrance of God) are the SAME: the ṢALĀH IS the remembrance. Satan wants to avert you from the ṢALĀH because the ṢALĀH IS the remembrance. The ṢALĀH is the vehicle of dhikr; Satan attacks the vehicle.
- D7 (4:43 - "do not approach the ṢALĀH while intoxicated"): D7 gave the ṢALĀH's PURITY-GATE (no intoxication before the ṢALĀH). D16 gives the ṢALĀH's ENEMY (Satan uses intoxicants to avert you from the ṢALĀH). The ṢALĀH is both gated on purity (D7) and targeted by Satan (D16). The purity-gate (D7) is the DEFENSE against Satan's aversion (D16).
- C19 (31:17 - "establish the ṢALĀH, enjoin right, forbid wrong, be patient"): C19's ṢALĀH is the anti-desire act. D16's ṢALĀH is the anti-Satan act. The ṢALĀH is the act that opposes both desire (shahwa) and Satan (shayṭān). The ṢALĀH is the God-ward act against all self-ward forces.
- A7 (19:59 - "they lost the ṢALĀH and followed desires"): the ṢALĀH-loss is the opposite of the ṢALĀH-establishment: the ṢALĀH-loser follows desires (shahwa); the ṢALĀH-establisher follows God. D16's Satan-aversion is the MECHANISM of the ṢALĀH-loss: Satan uses intoxicants and gambling to avert you from the ṢALĀH, and the ṢALĀH-loser follows desires -> fire-delivery.
- 5:90 "avoid them, that you may be successful" - the ṢALĀH is the path to success (tuflīḥūn). The ṢALĀH-establisher is successful; the intoxicant-consumer is not.

FINDING: D16 gives the ṢALĀH's ENEMY: Satan wants to AVERT you from the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the TARGET of Satan's aversion: Satan uses intoxicants and gambling as the MEANS; the ṢALĀH is the TARGET. And the ṢALĀH IS the remembrance (dhikr): Satan wants to avert you from BOTH (dhikr + ṢALĀH), but they are the SAME thing. The ṢALĀH is the act that Satan opposes - the anti-Satan act. The ṢALĀH is the path to success (tuflīḥūn); intoxicants and gambling are the path to ruin. The ṢALĀH is the God-ward act against all self-ward forces (desire, Satan, intoxicants, gambling).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The AVERTING (yaṣuddakum ʿan) - Satan wants to avert you from the ṢALĀH. HOLD for the final doc's enemy-table. (ii) The DHIKR + SALAH PAIR (ʿan dhikri Allāhi wa ʿani al-ṣalāti) - the ṢALĀH IS the remembrance. Satan attacks the vehicle. HOLD for the final doc's remembrance-table. (iii) The ANTI-SATAN (5:91) - the ṢALĀH is the anti-Satan act. HOLD for the final doc's opposition-table. (iv) The SUCCESS-PATH (tuflīḥūn, 5:90) - the ṢALĀH is the path to success. HOLD for the final doc's success-table. (v) The ṢALĀH now has FOURTEEN forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), and enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16). All fourteen are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D17 (5:106 - witnesses retained after salah: will-bequest law) next.
