## D17 - 5:106 - shuhada baynakum idha hadara ahadukum al-mawtu... tahbisunahuma min ba'di-s-salati

ARABIC (flow): 5:105 "O you who believe, upon you is [responsibility for] yourselves. Those who go astray will not harm you if you are guided. To God is your return, all together, and He will inform you of what you used to do" -> **5:106 "O you who believe, testimony [for bequest] between you when death approaches one of you at the time of bequest: two just men from among you, or two from other than you if you are traveling in the land and death strikes you. DETAIN THEM AFTER THE SALAH, then if you doubt [their testimony], they will swear by God: we will not exchange it for any price, even if it be for a near relative, and we will not conceal the testimony of God, for then indeed we would be among the sinners"** -> 5:107 "But if it is found that they were guilty of sin, then two others shall stand in their place from those who have a right to the claim, and they shall swear by God: our testimony is truer than their testimony, and we have not transgressed, for then indeed we would be among the wrongdoers" -> 5:108 "That is more likely that they will give the testimony in its true form, or that they will fear that oaths will be taken after their oaths. And fear God and listen; and God does not guide the transgressing people."

WORD FORM: tahbisūnahumā (detain them) min baʿdi al-ṣalāti (after the ṣalāh) + fa-yuqsimāni (then they will swear) bi-llāhi (by God). The witnesses are DETAINED AFTER THE SALAH, then they swear by God. The ṢALĀH is the temporal anchor for legal proceedings.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Ma'ida (5). The verse sits in the bequest-passage (5:105-108): responsibility for yourselves (5:105), testimony for bequest with witnesses detained after the ṢALĀH (5:106), replacement witnesses if the first are guilty of sin (5:107), truthfulness of testimony (5:108).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE TEMPORAL ANCHOR (tahbisūnahumā min baʿdi al-ṣalāti): "DETAIN THEM AFTER THE SALAH." The witnesses are detained AFTER THE SALAH. The ṢALĀH is the temporal anchor for legal proceedings: the witnesses are held until after the ṢALĀH, then they swear by God. The ṢALĀH is the CLOCK of legal proceedings - the community's legal life is anchored to the ṢALĀH.
- THE TRUTH-ANCHOR: "then if you doubt [their testimony], they will swear by God: we will not exchange it for any price, even if it be for a near relative, and we will not conceal the testimony of God, for then indeed we would be among the sinners." The ṢALĀH establishes the TRUTH-CONTEXT: after the ṢALĀH, the witnesses swear by God. The ṢALĀH is the act that creates the truth-context for legal testimony. The ṢALĀH is the TRUTH-ANCHOR.
- THE STANDING-OF-WITNESSES (5:107 "two others shall stand in their place" - qāma): the STANDING (qiyām) of the witnesses is paired with the ṢALĀH: the witnesses STAND (qāma) in place of the first witnesses. The same q-w-m root: qiyām (standing) in the ṢALĀH and qiyām (standing) of the witnesses. The ṢALĀH and the legal standing are paired.
- 5:105 "upon you is [responsibility for] yourselves... to God is your return, all together" - the ṢALĀH is the anchor in the midst of social/legal matters: the witnesses are detained after the ṢALĀH, showing that the ṢALĀH is the temporal frame for all community life.
- 5:108 "that is more likely that they will give the testimony in its true form" - the ṢALĀH establishes the truth-context: the witnesses are more likely to tell the truth after the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the TRUTH-PRODUCER.

PARALLELS:
- C16 (2:238 - "guard the ṢALAWAT and the middle ṢALĀH"): C16 gave the ṢALĀH's GUARDING (preserve the timed prayers). D17 gives the ṢALĀH's LEGAL-ANCHORING (detain witnesses after the ṢALĀH). The ṢALĀH is both guarded (C16) and legally anchoring (D17). The guarding and the anchoring are paired: the ṢALĀH is a guarded trust that anchors legal proceedings.
- C14 (17:78 - "establish the ṢALĀH at the decline of the sun until the darkness of the night, and the Quran of dawn"): C14 gave the ṢALĀH's TIMED boundaries. D17 gives the ṢALĀH's LEGAL timing: witnesses are detained AFTER the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH's time is the legal time.
- C15 (17:110 - "do not make your voice too loud in your ṢALĀH"): C15 gave the ṢALĀH's VOICE. D17 gives the ṢALĀH's TRUTH-VOICE: the witnesses swear by God after the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the act that produces truthful speech.
- D1 (4:101-103 - war prayer): D1 gave the ṢALĀH's ADAPTABILITY (shortened in war). D17 gives the ṢALĀH's LEGALITY (anchors legal proceedings). The ṢALĀH is both adaptable (D1) and legally anchoring (D17).
- 5:108 "fear God and listen" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears God and listens. The ṢALĀH is the act of the God-fearing listener.

FINDING: D17 gives the ṢALĀH's LEGAL-DIMENSION: witnesses are DETAINED AFTER THE SALAH. The ṢALĀH is the temporal anchor for legal proceedings: the community's legal life is anchored to the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the CLOCK of the community: legal proceedings happen AFTER the ṢALĀH. And the ṢALĀH is the TRUTH-ANCHOR: after the ṢALĀH, witnesses swear by God, ensuring truthfulness. The ṢALĀH is the act that creates the truth-context for legal testimony. The ṢALĀH is not just a ritual; it is the temporal and truth-anchor of the community's legal life.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The LEGAL-ANCHOR (5:106 - witnesses detained after the ṢALĀH) - the ṢALĀH is the temporal anchor for legal proceedings. HOLD for the final doc's legal-table. (ii) The TRUTH-ANCHOR (5:106-108) - the ṢALĀH establishes the truth-context for testimony. HOLD for the final doc's truth-table. (iii) The STANDING-OF-WITNESSES (5:107 - qāma) - the witnesses STAND in place of the first witnesses. The q-w-m root links the ṢALĀH (standing) and the legal standing. HOLD for the final doc's standing-table. (iv) The ṢALĀH now has FIFTEEN forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), and legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17). All fifteen are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D18 (6:72 - ???) next.

## D18 - 6:72 - wa-an aqimu-s-salata wa-ittaqūhu

ARABIC (flow): 6:70 "And leave those who take their religion as amusement and play, and whom the worldly life has deluded. But remind with it, lest a soul be given up to destruction for what it has earned; it will have besides God no protector and no intercessor; and if it should offer every compensation, it would not be taken from it. Those are the ones who are given up to destruction for what they have earned. For them will be a drink of scalding water and a painful punishment because they used to disbelieve" -> 6:71 "Say: Shall we invoke besides God that which neither benefits us nor harms us, and be turned back on our heels after God has guided us? [Like] one whom the devils have enticed [to wander] upon the earth, confused, [while] he has companions inviting him to guidance, [calling], 'Come to us.' Say: Indeed, the guidance of God is the [true] guidance, and we have been commanded to submit to the Lord of the worlds" -> **6:72 "AND TO ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND FEAR HIM. And He is the One to whom you will be gathered"** -> 6:73 "And He is the One who created the heavens and the earth in truth, and the Day He says: Be, and it is. His word is truth. And to Him belongs the dominion on the Day the Horn is blown. [He is] Knower of the unseen and the witnessed. And He is the Wise, the Acquainted" -> 6:74 "And when Abraham said to his father Azar: Do you take idols as gods? Indeed I see you and your people in clear error."

WORD FORM: aqīmū (establish) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + wa-ittaquhu (and fear Him) + wa-huwa alladhī ilayhi tuḥsharūn (and He is the One to whom you will be gathered). The ṢALĀH is paired with FEAR (taqwā) and oriented toward GATHERING (ḥashr).

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-An'am (6). The verse sits in the closing-passage of Surah 6: leave those who take their religion as amusement (6:70), we have been commanded to submit to the Lord of the worlds (6:71), ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND FEAR HIM, and He is the One to whom you will be gathered (6:72), God created the heavens and the earth (6:73), Abraham said to his father: do you take idols as gods? (6:74).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE FEAR-PAIRING (ṢALĀH + taqwā): "establish the ṢALĀH and FEAR HIM." The ṢALĀH is paired with FEAR (taqwā). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who FEARS God. The same as D5 (2:45 "heavy except on the humble") and D8 (4:77 "the Hereafter is better for one who fears God"). The ṢALĀH is the act of the God-fearing.
- THE GATHERING-ORIENTATION (wa-huwa alladhī ilayhi tuḥsharūn): "He is the One to whom you will be GATHERED." The ṢALĀH is for the Day of Gathering (yawm al-ḥashr). The standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) is preparation for the Day of Gathering (Ḥashr). The same as C7 (14:41 "on the Day the reckoning is established") and C10 (20:15 "the Hour is coming"). The ṢALĀH is for the Day of Gathering.
- 6:71 "we have been commanded to submit to the Lord of the worlds" - the ṢALĀH is the act of submission (islām). The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-God as submission. The same as C5 (2:128 "make us submitters") and C12 (6:163 "I am the first of the submitters").
- 6:73 "He is the Wise, the Acquainted" - the ṢALĀH is given by the Wise and Acquainted God. The ṢALĀH is a wise and knowing act - the same as D12 (4:162 "the firm in knowledge"). The ṢALĀH requires knowledge.
- 6:74 "Abraham said to his father Azar: Do you take idols as gods? Indeed I see you and your people in clear error" - the ṢALĀH is the alternative to idolatry. The same as C6 (14:35 "keep me and my sons away from worshipping idols") and C19 (31:13 "do not associate partners with God"). The ṢALĀH is the anti-idolatry act.

PARALLELS:
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): D18 gives the ṢALĀH + FEAR (ittaqu) pair. The ṢALĀH is paired with fear (taqwā) in D18, and with zakah in C1. The ṢALĀH is paired with BOTH fear and zakah: the standing-toward-God requires both fear (D18) and giving (C1).
- D5 (2:45 - "heavy except on the humble"): D5 gave the ṢALĀH's WEIGHT. D18 gives the ṢALĀH's FEAR-DIMENSION: the ṢALĀH is for one who fears God. The ṢALĀH is heavy (D5) and fearful (D18) - the same act, different dimensions.
- D8 (4:77 - "the Hereafter is better for one who fears God"): D8 gave the ṢALĀH as a PEACE-ACT for one who fears God. D18 gives the ṢALĀH + FEAR as a PAIR. The ṢALĀH is both a peace-act (D8) and a fear-act (D18).
- C7 (14:41 - "on the Day the reckoning is established"): D18's "He is the One to whom you will be gathered" (ḥashr) is the SAME as C7's "on the Day the reckoning is established" (qiyāmah). The ṢALĀH is for the Day of Gathering/Reckoning.
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (established) IS. The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-God as submission (6:71 "we have been commanded to submit").
- 6:74 "Abraham said to his father Azar: Do you take idols as gods?" - the ṢALĀH is the alternative to idolatry. The same as C6 (14:35) and C19 (31:13).

FINDING: D18 gives the ṢALĀH + FEAR pairing: "establish the ṢALĀH and FEAR HIM." The ṢALĀH is paired with FEAR (taqwā): the ṢALĀH is the act of one who FEARS God. And the ṢALĀH is for the Day of GATHERING (ḥashr): "He is the One to whom you will be gathered." The standing-toward-God (ṢALĀH) is preparation for the Day of Gathering (Ḥashr). The ṢALĀH is the anti-idolatry act (6:74: "do you take idols as gods?"). And the ṢALĀH is the act of submission (6:71: "we have been commanded to submit to the Lord of the worlds").

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The FEAR-PAIRING (ṢALĀH + taqwā) - the ṢALĀH is paired with fear of God. HOLD for the final doc's fear-table. (ii) The GATHERING-ORIENTATION (ḥashr) - the ṢALĀH is for the Day of Gathering. HOLD for the final doc's eschatology-table. (iii) The ANTI-IDOLATRY (6:74) - the ṢALĀH is the alternative to idolatry. HOLD for the final doc's anti-idolatry table. (iv) The SUBMISSION (6:71) - the ṢALĀH is the act of submission. HOLD for the final doc's submission-table. (v) The ṢALĀH now has SIXTEEN forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), and fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18). All sixteen are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D19 (6:92 - ???) next.

## D19 - 6:92 - wa hum ala salatihim yuhafizun

ARABIC (flow): 6:90 "Those are the ones whom God guided, so follow their guidance. Say: I do not ask you for a reward for it; it is only a reminder for the worlds" -> 6:91 "And they did not appraise God with true appraisal when they said: God has not sent down anything to a human being. Say: who sent down the Book which Moses brought, a light and guidance for the people? You make it into pages, disclosing some of it and concealing much. And you were taught that which you did not know - neither you nor your fathers. Say: God. Then leave them to plunge in their vain discourse" -> **6:92 "And this is a Book which We have sent down, blessed, confirming what was before it, that you may warn the Mother of Cities and those around it. And those who believe in the Hereafter believe in it, and THEY MAINTAIN THEIR SALAH (wa hum ʿalā ṣalātihim yuḥāfiẓūn)"** -> 6:93 "And who is more unjust than one who invents a lie about God or says: it has been revealed to me, while nothing has been revealed to him, and one who says: I will reveal the like of what God has revealed. If you could see when the wrongdoers are in the agonies of death and the angels are stretching out their hands: give up your souls; today you will be recompensed with the punishment of humiliation because you used to say about God other than the truth and you used to be arrogant toward His signs" -> 6:94 "And you have come to Us individually, as We created you the first time, and you have left behind what We bestowed upon you. And We do not see with you your intercessors whom you claimed were your partners. Indeed, it has been severed between you, and lost from you is that which you used to claim."

WORD FORM: yuḥāfiẓūn (they guard/preserve/maintain) + ʿalā (upon) + ṣalātihim (their ṣalāh) - the ṢALĀH is GUARDED/MAINTAINED by those who believe in the Hereafter. The ṢALĀH is a TRUST to be preserved - the same "guard" (ḥāfiẓ) as C16 (2:238 "guard the ṢALAWAT").

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-An'am (6). The verse sits in the Book-passage (6:90-94): those whom God guided (6:90), the Book of Moses (6:91), THIS BOOK is blessed, confirming what was before it; those who believe in the Hereafter believe in it and MAINTAIN THEIR SALAH (6:92), the wrongdoers die in agony (6:93), you have come to Us individually (6:94).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE GUARD-VERB (yuḥāfiẓūn): "they MAINTAIN/PRESERVE their ṢALĀH" - the ṢALĀH is GUARDED by those who believe in the Hereafter. The ṢALĀH is a TRUST to be preserved - the same "guard" (ḥāfiẓ) as C16 (2:238 "guard the ṢALAWAT and the middle ṢALĀH"). The ṢALĀH is guarded like a trust is guarded. The standing-state is a TRUST.
- THE HEREAFTER-BELIEF PAIRING (yuʾminūna bi-l-ākhirati ... wa hum ʿalā ṣalātihim yuḥāfiẓūn): "those who believe in the Hereafter believe in it, and they MAINTAIN THEIR SALAH." The ṢALĀH is the act of one who believes in the Hereafter. The ṢALĀH is the marker of Hereafter-belief: those who believe in the Hereafter maintain their ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is the proof of Hereafter-belief.
- 6:93 "when the wrongdoers are in the agonies of death and the angels are stretching out their hands: give up your souls" - the ṢALĀH-guardians (6:92) are contrasted with the wrongdoers who die in agony (6:93). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is PREPARED for death: the ṢALĀH-guardian is prepared; the ṢALĀH-loser dies in agony. The same as A17 (84:14 "he deemed he would never return" - the fire-people deny return; the ṢALĀH-guardian is prepared for return).
- 6:94 "you have come to Us individually, as We created you the first time" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who remembers that he will return to God INDIVIDUALLY. The ṢALĀH is the preparation for the individual return: you will come to God alone, as you were created alone. The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-God in preparation for the individual return.
- 6:91 "who sent down the Book which Moses brought, a light and guidance for the people?" - the ṢALĀH is part of the Book's guidance. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who follows the Book's guidance. The same as C8 (19:31 "He gave me the Book and made me a prophet; He enjoined on me the ṢALĀH").

PARALLELS:
- C16 (2:238 - "guard the ṢALAWAT and the middle ṢALĀH"): C16 gave the ṢALĀH's GUARDING (preserve the timed prayers). D19 gives the ṢALĀH's MAINTENANCE (guard/preserve the ṢALĀH). The ṢALĀH is both guarded (C16) and maintained (D19). The guarding and the maintaining are the same: the ṢALĀH is a trust to be kept.
- D1 (2:3 - "those who believe in the unseen and establish the ṢALĀH"): D1 gave the ṢALĀH as the DEFINING act of the believer. D19 gives the ṢALĀH as the act of one who believes in the Hereafter. The ṢALĀH is both the defining act (D1) and the Hereafter-believer's act (D19).
- D17 (5:106 - "detain them after the ṢALĀH"): D17 gave the ṢALĀH's LEGAL-ANCHORING. D19 gives the ṢALĀH's DEATH-PREPARATION: the ṢALĀH-guardian is prepared for death; the ṢALĀH-loser dies in agony (6:93). The ṢALĀH is both a legal anchor (D17) and a death-preparation (D19).
- A17 (84:14 - "he deemed he would never return"): the fire-people deny return; the ṢALĀH-guardian is prepared for return (6:94 "you have come to Us individually"). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is prepared for return.
- B2 (2:157 - ṢALAWĀT upon the patient): the divine ṢALAWĀT is granted to those who MAINTAIN the ṢALĀH (D19). The human ṢALĀH (maintained) invites the divine ṢALAWĀT.

FINDING: D19 gives the ṢALĀH as a MAINTAINED trust: "those who believe in the Hereafter believe in it, and they MAINTAIN THEIR SALAH." The ṢALĀH is GUARDED/PRESERVED by those who believe in the Hereafter. The ṢALĀH is the marker of Hereafter-belief: those who believe in the Hereafter maintain their ṢALĀH. And the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is PREPARED for death (6:93-94): the ṢALĀH-guardian is prepared; the ṢALĀH-loser dies in agony. The ṢALĀH is the preparation for the individual return to God.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The MAINTENANCE (yuḥāfiẓūn) - the ṢALĀH is a trust to be preserved. HOLD for the final doc's trust-table. (ii) The HEREAFTER-BELIEF (6:92) - the ṢALĀH is the marker of Hereafter-belief. HOLD for the final doc's belief-table. (iii) The DEATH-PREPARATION (6:93-94) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is prepared for death. HOLD for the final doc's preparation-table. (iv) The INDIVIDUAL RETURN (6:94) - the ṢALĀH is the preparation for the individual return to God. HOLD for the final doc's return-table.

VERDICT: studied - see D20 (7:170 - those who hold fast to the Book and establish salah) next.

## D20 - 7:170 - wa alladhina yumsiku-na bi-l-kitabi wa aqamu-s-salata

ARABIC (flow): 7:169 "And there succeeded after them a successor who inherited the Book, taking the goods of this lower [world] and saying: it will be forgiven for us. And if there comes to them goods like it, they will take it. Was not the covenant of the Book taken from them that they would not say about God except the truth, and they studied what is in it? And the Hereafter is better for those who fear God. Do you not reason?" -> **7:170 "AND THOSE WHO HOLD FAST TO THE BOOK AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH - INDEED WE DO NOT WASTE THE REWARD OF THE REFORMERS"** -> 7:171 "And when We raised the mountain above them as if it were a canopy, and they thought it would fall upon them: Take what We have given you with strength, and remember what is in it, that you may fear God."

WORD FORM: yumsikūna (hold fast) bi-l-kitābi (to the Book) + aqāmū (established) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + innā lā nuḍīʿu (indeed We do not waste) ajra (the reward) al-muṣliḥīn (of the reformers).

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-A'raf (7). The verse sits in the Bani-Israel passage (7:169-171): the successor who inherited the Book and took the goods of this world (7:169), THOSE WHO HOLD FAST TO THE BOOK AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH - their reward is not wasted (7:170), take what We have given you with strength and remember what is in it (7:171).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE BOOK + SALAH PAIR: "those who HOLD FAST TO THE BOOK AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH" - the ṢALĀH is paired with HOLDING FAST TO THE BOOK. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who holds fast to the Book (the scripture). The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-the-Book's God. The ṢALĀH and the Book are paired: the ṢALĀH is the standing-act; the Book is the standing-text. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who holds fast to the revelation.
- "INDEED WE DO NOT WASTE THE REWARD OF THE REFORMERS" (innā lā nuḍīʿu ajra al-muṣliḥīn): the ṢALĀH-establishers are the REFORMERS (muṣliḥūn). The ṢALĀH is the act of reform: those who establish the ṢALĀH are reformers, and their reward is not wasted. The ṢALĀH is the reform-act - the same as 11:115 ("God does not waste the reward of those who do good"). The ṢALĀH is a good deed whose reward is guaranteed.
- 7:169 "there succeeded after them a successor who inherited the Book, taking the goods of this lower [world]" - the ṢALĀH-loser is the one who takes the goods of this world. The ṢALĀH-establisher holds fast to the Book; the ṢALĀH-loser takes the goods of this world. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who holds fast to the Book, not the world. The same as D4 (2:277 - the ṢALĀH-establisher is rewarded; the usury-consumer is at war) and A6 (17:18 - "whoever wants the immediate").
- 7:171 "Take what We have given you with strength, and remember what is in it, that you may fear God" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who takes the Book with strength and remembers what is in it. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears God (taqwā). The same as D18 (6:72 - "establish the ṢALĀH and fear Him").

PARALLELS:
- D1 (2:3 - "those who believe in the unseen and establish the ṢALĀH and spend"): D1 gave the ṢALĀH + SPENDING pair. D20 gives the ṢALĀH + BOOK pair. The ṢALĀH is paired with both spending (D1) and the Book (D20). The ṢALĀH is the God-ward act; spending and holding the Book are the two complementary acts.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): C1's two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + zakah) and D20's two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + Book) are the SAME structure: the ṢALĀH is paired with a complementary act. The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH (C1) and the ṢALĀH + BOOK (D20) are the two forms of the ṢALĀH-pair.
- D19 (6:92 - "those who believe in the Hereafter believe in it, and they maintain their ṢALĀH"): D19 gave the ṢALĀH as the act of one who believes in the Hereafter. D20 gives the ṢALĀH as the act of one who holds fast to the Book. The ṢALĀH is both a Hereafter-belief-act (D19) and a Book-holding-act (D20).
- C13 (3:39 - Zakariyya standing-praying in the miḥrāb): the STANDING-POSTURE (qāʾim) is the physical form. D20 gives the ṢALĀH as the act of one who holds fast to the Book. The ṢALĀH is both a standing-posture (C13) and a Book-holding-act (D20).
- 7:169 "there succeeded after them a successor who inherited the Book, taking the goods of this lower [world]" - the ṢALĀH-loser takes the goods of this world. The ṢALĀH-establisher holds fast to the Book. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who holds fast to the Book, not the world. The same as A6 (17:18 "whoever wants the immediate") and D4 (2:277 "the usury-consumer is at war").

FINDING: D20 gives the ṢALĀH as a BOOK-HOLDING act: "those who hold fast to the Book AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH." The ṢALĀH is paired with HOLDING FAST TO THE BOOK. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who holds fast to the Book (the scripture). The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-the-Book's God. And the ṢALĀH-establishers are the REFORMERS (muṣliḥūn): "indeed We do not waste the reward of the reformers." The ṢALĀH is the act of reform. The ṢALĀH is not just a ritual; it is a REFORM-act: those who establish the ṢALĀH are reformers.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The BOOK + SALAH PAIR (7:170) - the ṢALĀH is paired with holding fast to the Book. HOLD for the final doc's book-table. (ii) The REFORMER (muṣliḥ) - the ṢALĀH-establisher is a reformer. HOLD for the final doc's reform-table. (iii) The ṢALĀH as ALTERNATIVE to worldly goods (7:169) - the ṢALĀH-establisher holds fast to the Book; the ṢALĀH-loser takes the goods of this world. HOLD for the wealth-thread (A1 A14 A17 A21 A22 D20). (iv) The ṢALĀH now has SEVENTEEN forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), and Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20). All seventeen are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D21 (8:3 - those who establish salah and spend from what We provided) next.

## D21 - 8:3 - alladhina yuqimu-s-salata wa mimmā razaqnāhum yunfiqun

ARABIC (flow): 8:1 "They ask you about the spoils of war. Say: the spoils of war are for God and the Messenger. So fear God and reconcile between yourselves, and obey God and His Messenger if you are believers" -> 8:2 "The believers are only those whose hearts tremble when God is mentioned, and when His signs are recited to them, it increases them in faith, and upon their Lord they rely" -> **8:3 "THOSE WHO ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND SPEND FROM WHAT WE HAVE PROVIDED THEM"** -> 8:4 "Those are the TRUE BELIEVERS. For them are ranks with their Lord and forgiveness and a noble provision" -> 8:5 "As your Lord brought you out of your house with truth, while indeed a party of the believers disliked it."

WORD FORM: yuqīmūna (establish) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + yunfiqūn (spend) + mimmā razaqnāhum (from what We provided them) - the ṢALĀH + SPENDING pair (D1's two-act definition of the believer) returns: "establish the ṢALĀH and spend from what We provided."

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah al-Anfal (8). The verse sits in the spoils-of-war passage (8:1-5): the spoils are for God and the Messenger (8:1), the believers are those whose hearts tremble when God is mentioned (8:2), THOSE WHO ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND SPEND (8:3), those are the TRUE BELIEVERS with ranks, forgiveness, and noble provision (8:4).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE ṢALĀH + SPENDING PAIR (RETURNS): "establish the ṢALĀH and spend from what We provided them." The SAME two-act definition as D1 (2:3): God-ward (ṢALĀH) + people-ward (spending). The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-God; spending is the giving-to-people. The two are paired: you stand toward God AND give to people. The ṢALĀH is not isolated; it is paired with generosity.
- THE TRUE BELIEVER (8:4 "Those are the TRUE BELIEVERS"): the ṢALĀH-establishers are the TRUE BELIEVERS. The ṢALĀH is the proof of true belief: those who establish the ṢALĀH and spend are the TRUE believers. The same as D1 (2:8 "they say: we believe... but they are not believers") and D19 (6:92 "those who believe in the Hereafter believe in it, and they maintain their ṢALĀH"). The ṢALĀH is the DIVIDER between true and false believers.
- THE HEART-TREMBLING (8:2 "whose hearts tremble when God is mentioned"): the ṢALĀH is the act of one whose heart TREMBLES when God is mentioned. The ṢALĀH is the act of the God-fearing: the heart trembles, faith increases, and reliance on God follows. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who FEARS God.
- THE FAITH-INCREASING (8:2 "when His signs are recited to them, it increases them in faith"): the ṢALĀH is the act of one whose faith INCREASES when God's signs are recited. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is receptive to revelation: the ṢALĀH increases faith.
- THE RELIANCE (8:2 "upon their Lord they rely"): the ṢALĀH is the act of one who RELIES on God. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who trusts in God. The same as B2 (2:157 "we belong to God and to Him we return") and C7 (14:40 "accept my supplication").
- 8:1 "fear God and reconcile between yourselves, and obey God and His Messenger if you are believers" - the ṢALĀH is established in the context of RECONCILIATION and OBEDIENCE. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who reconciles and obeys.

PARALLELS:
- D1 (2:3 - "those who believe in the unseen and establish the ṢALĀH and spend"): D1 gave the ṢALĀH + SPENDING pair. D21 repeats it. The ṢALĀH + SPENDING pair is STABLE across suras: 2:3 and 8:3. The two-act definition of the believer is UNIVERSAL.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): C1's two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + zakah) and D21's two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + spending) are the SAME: the ṢALĀH is paired with GIVING. In C1, the giving is ZAKAH; in D21, the giving is SPENDING. The ṢALĀH + GIVING pair is STABLE.
- D19 (6:92 - "those who believe in the Hereafter believe in it, and they maintain their ṢALĀH"): D19 gave the ṢALĀH as the act of one who believes in the Hereafter. D21 gives the ṢALĀH as the act of the TRUE BELIEVER. The ṢALĀH is both a Hereafter-belief-act (D19) and a true-belief-act (D21).
- 8:4 "Those are the TRUE BELIEVERS. For them are ranks with their Lord and forgiveness and a noble provision" - the ṢALĀH-establishers receive RANKS (darajāt), FORGIVENESS (maghfirah), and NOBLE PROVISION (rizq karīm). The ṢALĀH is rewarded with ranks, forgiveness, and provision.
- B2 (2:157 - ṢALAWĀT upon the patient + raḥmah): the divine ṢALAWĀT is granted to those who ESTABLISH the ṢALĀH (D21). The human ṢALĀH (established) invites the divine ṢALAWĀT.

FINDING: D21 confirms the ṢALĀH + SPENDING pair as the TWO-ACT DEFINITION of the TRUE BELIEVER: "those who establish the ṢALĀH and spend from what We provided them - those are the TRUE BELIEVERS." The ṢALĀH is the God-ward act; spending is the people-ward act. The ṢALĀH is the act of one whose heart TREMBLES when God is mentioned, whose faith INCREASES when God's signs are recited, and who RELIES on God (8:2). The ṢALĀH is the act of the TRUE believer, and the ṢALĀH-establisher receives RANKS, FORGIVENESS, and NOBLE PROVISION (8:4).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The ṢALĀH + SPENDING pair (8:3) - the two-act definition of the TRUE believer. HOLD for the final doc's definition-table. (ii) The TRUE BELIEVER (8:4) - the ṢALĀH is the proof of true belief. HOLD for the final doc's belief-table. (iii) The HEART-TREMBLING (8:2) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one whose heart trembles when God is mentioned. HOLD for the final doc's heart-table. (iv) The FAITH-INCREASING (8:2) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one whose faith increases when God's signs are recited. HOLD for the final doc's faith-table. (v) The RELIANCE (8:2) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who relies on God. HOLD for the final doc's reliance-table. (vi) The REWARD (8:4 - ranks, forgiveness, noble provision) - the ṢALĀH-establisher is rewarded. HOLD for the final doc's reward-table.

VERDICT: studied - see D22 (9:5 - ???) next.

## D22 - 9:5 - fa-in tabu wa-aqamu-s-salata wa-atu-z-zakata fa-khallu sabilahum

ARABIC (flow): 9:1 "A declaration of disavowal from God and His Messenger to those of the polytheists with whom you had made a treaty" -> 9:2 "So travel freely in the land for four months, and know that you cannot escape God, and that God will disgrace the disbelievers" -> 9:3 "And a proclamation from God and His Messenger to the people on the Day of the Greater Pilgrimage: God is disassociated from the polytheists, and so is His Messenger. So if you repent, it is better for you; and if you turn away, then know that you cannot escape God. And give good tidings to the disbelievers of a painful punishment" -> 9:4 "Except those of the polytheists with whom you made a treaty and then they have not failed you in anything nor supported anyone against you - so fulfill to them their treaty until their term. Indeed God loves the righteous" -> **9:5 "And when the sacred months have passed, then kill the polytheists wherever you find them and capture them and besiege them and sit in wait for them at every place of ambush. But if they REPENT and ESTABLISH THE SALAH and GIVE THE ZAKAH, then let them go on their way. Indeed God is Forgiving, Merciful"** -> 9:6 "And if any one of the polytheists seeks your protection, then grant him protection until he hears the words of God; then deliver him to his place of safety. That is because they are a people who do not know" -> 9:7-8 (treaty rules).

WORD FORM: fa-in (but if) tabū (they repent) wa-aqāmū (and they establish) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) wa-ātū (and they give) al-zakāta (the zakah) fa-khallū (then let go) sabīlahum (their way) inna Allāha (indeed God) ghafūrun raḥīmun (is Forgiving, Merciful). The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair (C1's two-pillar formula) is the CONDITION for peace: repent + establish ṢALĀH + give zakah = let them go.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah at-Tawba (9). The verse sits in the treaty-passage (9:1-8): disavowal from the polytheists (9:1), four months of travel (9:2), proclamation on the Day of the Greater Pilgrimage (9:3), except those with treaties (9:4), when the sacred months have passed, kill the polytheists; but if they repent and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah, then let them go (9:5), if any seeks protection, grant it until he hears the words of God (9:6).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE ṢALĀH AS CONDITION FOR PEACE: "if they REPENT and ESTABLISH THE SALAH and GIVE THE ZAKAH, then let them go on their way." The ṢALĀH is the CONDITION for peace: the polytheist who repents AND establishes the ṢALĀH AND gives the zakah is granted peace (let them go). The ṢALĀH is the GATE to peace: the ṢALĀH is the act that transforms an enemy into a protected person. The ṢALĀH is the PEACE-act.
- THE ṢALĀH + ZAKAH PAIR (AGAIN): "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah" - the SAME two-pillar formula as C1 (98:5), C2 (2:43), etc. The formula is STABLE. D22 adds REPENTANCE as the third element: repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace. The ṢALĀH is the PROOF of repentance: repentance is not just a verbal act; it is ESTABLISHING THE SALAH.
- "INDEED GOD IS FORGIVING, MERCIFUL" (ghafūrun raḥīmun): the ṢALĀH-establisher is forgiven and shown mercy. The ṢALĀH is met with FORGIVENESS and MERCY. The same as B2 (ṢALAWĀT + raḥmah) and B5 (ṢALĀH + sakan).
- 9:6 "if any one of the polytheists seeks your protection, then grant him protection until he hears the words of God" - the ṢALĀH is given after HEARING the words of God. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who has HEARD the words of God. The ṢALĀH is the response to hearing God's words.
- 9:4 "Indeed God loves the righteous" - the ṢALĀH-establisher is loved by God. The ṢALĀH is the act of one whom God loves. The same as 9:99 (B6 - "God will admit them to His mercy") and 9:103 (B5 - "your ṣalāh is their tranquility").

PARALLELS:
- D8 (4:77 - "restrain your hands and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): D8 gave the ṢALĀH as a PEACE-ACT (restrain your hands and establish the ṢALĀH). D22 gives the ṢALĀH as a PEACE-CONDITION (if they repent and establish the ṢALĀH, let them go). The ṢALĀH is both a peace-act (D8) and a peace-condition (D22).
- D4 (2:277 - "those who believe and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah: no fear, no grief"): D4 gave the ṢALĀH's PROMISE (no fear, no grief). D22 gives the ṢALĀH's PEACE (let them go). The ṢALĀH is both a promise (D4) and a peace (D22).
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula. D22 adds REPENTANCE as the third element: repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace.
- B6 (9:99 - "those who believe and take what they spend as ṢALAWĀT of the Messenger"): the ṢALĀH is the act of the believer (9:99). D22 shows the ṢALĀH as the act of the REPENTANT: the polytheist who repents and establishes the ṢALĀH is granted peace. The ṢALĀH is the act of both the believer and the repentant.
- 9:6 "grant him protection until he hears the words of God" - the ṢALĀH is given after hearing. The same as C10 (20:14 "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance") - the ṢALĀH is the response to hearing God's words.

FINDING: D22 gives the ṢALĀH as the CONDITION FOR PEACE: "if they repent and ESTABLISH THE SALAH and GIVE THE ZAKAH, then let them go on their way." The ṢALĀH is the GATE to peace: the polytheist who repents AND establishes the ṢALĀH AND gives the zakah is granted peace. The ṢALĀH is the PROOF of repentance: repentance is not just a verbal act; it is ESTABLISHING THE SALAH. The ṢALĀH is the PEACE-act: the act that transforms an enemy into a protected person. And the ṢALĀH is given after HEARING the words of God (9:6) - the ṢALĀH is the response to hearing God's words.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The PEACE-CONDITION (9:5) - the ṢALĀH is the condition for peace. HOLD for the final doc's peace-table. (ii) The REPENTANCE (9:5) - the ṢALĀH is the proof of repentance. HOLD for the final doc's repentance-table. (iii) The HEARING (9:6) - the ṢALĀH is given after hearing God's words. HOLD for the final doc's hearing-table. (iv) The FORGIVENESS+MERCY (9:5) - the ṢALĀH-establisher is forgiven and shown mercy. HOLD for the final doc's mercy-table. (v) The ṢALĀH now has EIGHTEEN forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20), and peace-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace, D22). All eighteen are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D23 (9:11 - ???) next.

## D23 - 9:11 - fa-in tabu wa-aqamu-s-salata wa-atu-z-zakata fa-ikhwanukum fi-d-dini

ARABIC (flow): 9:9 "They have sold the signs of God for a small price, and averted [people] from His way. Indeed, evil is that which they were doing" -> 9:10 "They do not observe toward a believer any pact of kinship or covenant of protection. And it is they who are the transgressors" -> **9:11 "BUT IF THEY REPENT AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH, THEN THEY ARE YOUR BROTHERS IN RELIGION. And We detail the signs for a people who know"** -> 9:12 "And if they break their oaths after their covenant and defame your religion, then fight the leaders of disbelief - indeed they have no oaths - that they might desist" -> 9:13 "Will you not fight a people who broke their oaths and determined to expel the Messenger, and they had begun [the attack upon] you the first time? Do you fear them? But God has more right that you should fear Him, if you are believers."

WORD FORM: fa-in (but if) tabū (they repent) wa-aqāmū (and they establish) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) wa-ātū (and they give) al-zakāta (the zakah) fa-ikhawānukum (then they are your brothers) fī al-dīni (in religion) wa nufaṣṣilu (and We detail) al-āyāti (the signs) li-qawmin (for a people) yaʿlamūn (who know).

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah at-Tawba (9). The verse sits in the treaty-passage (9:9-13): the polytheists sold God's signs for a small price (9:9), they do not observe pacts (9:10), if they repent and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah, they are your brothers in religion (9:11), if they break their oaths, fight them (9:12-13).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE ṢALĀH AS CONDITION FOR BROTHERHOOD: "if they repent and ESTABLISH THE SALAH and GIVE THE ZAKAH, then they are YOUR BROTHERS IN RELIGION." The ṢALĀH is the GATE to brotherhood: the polytheist who repents AND establishes the ṢALĀH AND gives the zakah becomes a BROTHER in religion. The ṢALĀH is the act that transforms an enemy into a brother. The same as D22 (9:5 - "if they repent and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah, then let them go") - D22 gives peace; D23 gives brotherhood. The ṢALĀH is both the peace-condition and the brotherhood-condition.
- THE ṢALĀH + ZAKAH PAIR (AGAIN): "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah" - the SAME two-pillar formula as C1 (98:5), C2 (2:43), etc. The formula is STABLE. D23 adds BROTHERHOOD as the outcome: repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers in religion.
- "THEN THEY ARE YOUR BROTHERS IN RELIGION" (fa-ikhawānukum fī al-dīni): the ṢALĀH is the marker of BROTHERHOOD: those who establish the ṢALĀH are your brothers in religion. The ṢALĀH is the divider between brothers and non-brothers. The same as D14 (5:55 - "your ally is only God and His Messenger and those who believe - those who establish the ṢALĀH"). The ṢALĀH is both an ally-marker (D14) and a brotherhood-marker (D23).
- "AND WE DETAIL THE SIGNS FOR A PEOPLE WHO KNOW" (wa nufaṣṣilu al-āyāti li-qawmin yaʿlamūn): the ṢALĀH is for those who KNOW. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who knows. The same as D12 (4:162 - "the firm in knowledge... establishers of the ṢALĀH"). The ṢALĀH requires knowledge.
- 9:12 "if they break their oaths after their covenant and defame your religion, then fight the leaders of disbelief" - the ṢALĀH-breakers are to be fought. The ṢALĀH is the marker of peace; the ṢALĀH-breaker is the enemy. The same as D22 (9:5 - "if they repent and establish the ṢALĀH, then let them go").
- 9:13 "Will you not fight a people who broke their oaths... Do you fear them? But God has more right that you should fear Him" - the ṢALĀH-establisher fears God, not people. The same as D8 (4:77 "the Hereafter is better for one who fears God").

PARALLELS:
- D22 (9:5 - "if they repent and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah, then let them go"): D22 gave the PEACE-condition (let them go). D23 gives the BROTHERHOOD-condition (they are your brothers). The ṢALĀH is both the peace-condition (D22) and the brotherhood-condition (D23). The ṢALĀH transforms an enemy into a protected person (D22) and into a brother (D23).
- D14 (5:55 - "your ally is only God and His Messenger and those who believe - those who establish the ṢALĀH"): D14 gave the ALLY-marker. D23 gives the BROTHERHOOD-marker. The ṢALĀH is both an ally-marker (D14) and a brotherhood-marker (D23).
- B7 (9:84 - "do not s-l-l on them"): B7's withholding of ṢALĀH from the disbelievers matches D23's ṢALĀH as the gate to brotherhood. The ṢALĀH is granted to believers and withheld from disbelievers. The ṢALĀH is the divider.
- D4 (2:277 - "those who believe and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah: no fear, no grief"): D4 gave the PROMISE (no fear, no grief). D23 gives the BROTHERHOOD. The ṢALĀH is both a promise (D4) and a brotherhood (D23).
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME two-pillar formula. D23 repeats C1's formula in the treaty-context.

FINDING: D23 gives the ṢALĀH as the CONDITION FOR BROTHERHOOD: "if they repent and ESTABLISH THE SALAH and GIVE THE ZAKAH, then they are YOUR BROTHERS IN RELIGION." The ṢALĀH is the GATE to brotherhood: the polytheist who repents AND establishes the ṢALĀH AND gives the zakah becomes a BROTHER in religion. The ṢALĀH is the act that transforms an enemy into a brother. And the ṢALĀH is for those who KNOW (li-qawmin yaʿlamūn) - the same as D12 (4:162 "the firm in knowledge... establishers of the ṢALĀH"). The ṢALĀH requires knowledge.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The BROTHERHOOD-CONDITION (9:11) - the ṢALĀH is the condition for brotherhood. HOLD for the final doc's brotherhood-table. (ii) The KNOWLEDGE (9:11) - the ṢALĀH is for those who know. HOLD for the final doc's knowledge-table. (iii) The PEACE-CONDITION (9:5) + BROTHERHOOD-CONDITION (9:11) - the ṢALĀH is both the peace-condition and the brotherhood-condition. HOLD for the final doc's peace-brotherhood table. (iv) The ṢALĀH now has NINETEEN forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20), peace-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace, D22), and brotherhood-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers in religion, D23). All nineteen are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D24 (9:18 - ???) next.

## D24 - 9:18 - innama ya'muru masajida Allahi man amana bi-llahi wa-l-yawmi al-akhiri wa aqama-s-salata wa ataz-zakata wa lam yakhsha illa Allah

ARABIC (flow): 9:17 "It is not for the polytheists to maintain the mosques of God while they witness against themselves with disbelief. Those - their deeds have become worthless, and in the Fire they will abide eternally" -> **9:18 "THE MOSQUES OF GOD ARE ONLY TO BE MAINTAINED BY THOSE WHO BELIEVE IN GOD AND THE LAST DAY AND ESTABLISH THE SALAH AND GIVE THE ZAKAH AND FEAR NONE BUT GOD. It is expected that those will be of the guided"** -> 9:19 "Have you made the providing of water for the pilgrims and the maintenance of the Sacred Mosque equal to [the deeds of] one who believes in God and the Last Day and strives in the path of God? They are not equal in the sight of God. And God does not guide the wrongdoing people."

WORD FORM: yaʿmuru (maintain) masājida (mosques of) Allāhi (God) + man (whoever) āmana (believes) bi-llāhi (in God) wa-l-yawmi al-ākhiri (and the Last Day) wa aqāma (and establishes) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) wa ātā (and gives) al-zakāta (the zakah) wa lam yakhsha (and does not fear) illā Allāha (except God) + fa-ʿasā (it is expected that) ulāʾika (those) an yakūnū (will be) min al-muhtadīn (of the guided).

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah at-Tawba (9). The verse sits in the mosque-maintenance passage (9:17-19): the polytheists cannot maintain God's mosques (9:17), the mosques are only maintained by those who believe and establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah and fear none but God (9:18), and providing water for pilgrims is not equal to believing and striving (9:19).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE MOSQUE-MAINTAINER: "the mosques of God are only to be maintained by those who... establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah and fear none but God." The ṢALĀH-establishers are the MOSQUE-MAINTAINERS. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who maintains God's mosques. The ṢALĀH and the mosque-maintenance are paired: the ṢALĀH is the act; the mosque is the site. The same as C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site) and C13 (3:39 - MIḤRĀB, the sanctuary).
- THE ṢALĀH + ZAKAH PAIR (AGAIN): "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah" - the SAME two-pillar formula as C1 (98:5), C2 (2:43), etc. D24 adds FEAR-NONE-BUT-GOD as the third element: believe + establish ṢALĀH + give zakah + fear none but God.
- THE FEAR-NONE-BUT-GOD (lam yakhsha illā Allāha): the ṢALĀH-establisher fears God ALONE. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears none but God. The same as D8 (4:77 "the Hereafter is better for one who fears God") and D18 (6:72 "establish the ṢALĀH and fear Him"). The ṢALĀH is the act of the God-fearer.
- 9:17 "It is not for the polytheists to maintain the mosques of God" - the ṢALĀH is the marker of who maintains God's mosques: the believers establish the ṢALĀH and maintain the mosques; the polytheists do not. The ṢALĀH is the act of the mosque-maintainer.
- 9:19 "Have you made the providing of water for the pilgrims and the maintenance of the Sacred Mosque equal to [the deeds of] one who believes in God and the Last Day and strives in the path of God?" - the ṢALĀH is NOT equal to providing water or maintaining the Sacred Mosque. The ṢALĀH is a DIFFERENT act: it is the act of belief, establishment, and fear of God. The ṢALĀH is not just a physical maintenance; it is a spiritual act.

PARALLELS:
- C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site): the MOSQUE (masjid) is the site where the ṢALĀH is established. The ṢALĀH-establisher maintains the mosque (D24) and stands at the MUSSALLA (C5). The site and the act are paired: the mosque is the site; the ṢALĀH is the act.
- D14 (5:55 - "those who establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah while they bow"): D14 gave the ṢALĀH as the ALLY-MARKER. D24 gives the ṢALĀH as the MOSQUE-MAINTAINER-MARKER. The ṢALĀH is both an ally-marker (D14) and a mosque-maintainer-marker (D24).
- D8 (4:77 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah; fear God, not people"): D8 gave the ṢALĀH as a PEACE-ACT for one who fears God. D24 gives the ṢALĀH as a MOSQUE-MAINTENANCE-ACT for one who fears God alone. The ṢALĀH is both a peace-act (D8) and a mosque-maintenance-act (D24).
- B1 (24:41 - "the birds know their ṢALĀH"): the bird's ṢALĀH is innate (known by creation). The human ṢALĀH is established (commanded). The mosque-maintainer (D24) establishes the ṢALĀH in the mosque; the bird establishes the ṢALĀH in the sky. Both are the same stem: the ṢALĀH is the standing-state, whether in a mosque or in the sky.
- 9:17 "It is not for the polytheists to maintain the mosques of God" - the ṢALĀH is the marker of who maintains God's mosques. The same as 9:84 (B7 - "do not s-l-l on them") and 9:11 (D23 - "repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers"). The ṢALĀH is the divider: those who establish it maintain the mosques; those who don't are excluded.

FINDING: D24 gives the ṢALĀH as the MOSQUE-MAINTENANCE act: "the mosques of God are only to be maintained by those who believe in God and the Last Day and ESTABLISH THE SALAH and GIVE THE ZAKAH and FEAR NONE BUT GOD." The ṢALĀH-establisher is the MOSQUE-MAINTAINER. The ṢALĀH is the act that maintains God's mosques. The ṢALĀH is both the act performed in the mosque (C13's miḥrāb) and the act that maintains the mosque (D24). And the ṢALĀH is for those who FEAR NONE BUT GOD - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears God alone. The ṢALĀH-establisher is expected to be of the guided (9:18).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The MOSQUE-MAINTENANCE (9:18) - the ṢALĀH-establisher maintains God's mosques. HOLD for the final doc's mosque-table. (ii) The FEAR-NONE-BUT-GOD (9:18) - the ṢALĀH is for one who fears God alone. HOLD for the final doc's fear-table. (iii) The ṢALĀH + ZAKAH pair is STABLE across 16+ suras - the UNIVERSAL formula. HOLD for the final doc's formula-table. (iv) The ṢALĀH is both the act performed in the mosque (C13) and the act that maintains the mosque (D24). HOLD for the final doc's site-table.

VERDICT: studied - see D25 (9:54 - those who are not pleased with the ṣalāh) next.

## D25 - 9:54 - wa la ya'tuna-s-salata illa wa hum kusala

ARABIC (flow): 9:53 "Say: spend willingly or unwillingly, it will not be accepted from you, for you are a transgressing people" -> **9:54 "And nothing prevents their expenditures from being accepted from them except that they DISBELIEVED IN GOD AND HIS MESSENGER, and they do NOT COME TO THE SALAH EXCEPT WHILE THEY ARE LAZY, and they do not spend except while they are UNWILLING"** -> 9:55 "So do not be amazed by their wealth and their children; God only intends to punish them through them in this worldly life and that their souls should depart while they are disbelievers" -> 9:56 "And they swear by God that they are of you, but they are not of you; but they are a people who are divided."

WORD FORM: lā yaʾtūna (they do not come) al-ṣalāta (to the ṣalāh) illā (except) wa-hum (while they are) kusālā (lazy) + wa lā yunfiqūna (and they do not spend) illā (except) wa-hum (while they are) kārihūn (unwilling). The ṢALĀH is approached LAZILY (kusālā).

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah at-Tawba (9). The verse sits in the hypocrite-passage (9:53-56): the hypocrites' expenditures are not accepted (9:53), they come to the ṢALĀH only lazily and spend only unwillingly (9:54), their wealth and children are a punishment (9:55), they are divided from the believers (9:56).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE LAZINESS (kusālā): "they do NOT COME TO THE SALAH EXCEPT WHILE THEY ARE LAZY." The ṢALĀH is approached LAZILY (kusālā). The ṢALĀH is not a desired act for the hypocrite; it is a burden. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is LAZY toward God. The ṢALĀH is the act that the hypocrite does without willingness - the same as D5 (2:45 "heavy except on the humble") but here the heaviness is not borne with humility but with laziness.
- THE UNWILLINGNESS (kārihūn): "they do not spend except while they are UNWILLING." The ṢALĀH (coming to it) and spending (giving) are both done UNWILLINGLY. The ṢALĀH is done lazily; spending is done unwillingly. Both are the same: the hypocrite does both without desire. The ṢALĀH + SPENDING pair is done without willingness by the hypocrite - the same pair that the believer does WILLINGLY (D1 2:3, D21 8:3).
- THE ACCEPTANCE-CONDITION: "nothing prevents their expenditures from being accepted from them except that they disbelieved in God and His Messenger" - the ṢALĀH is not accepted when done lazily. The ṢALĀH requires WILLINGNESS, not just physical presence. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who comes WILLINGLY, not lazily. The same as D5 (2:45 "heavy except on the humble") - the ṢALĀH is light for the humble and heavy for the proud/lazy.
- 9:53 "spend willingly or unwillingly, it will not be accepted from you, for you are a transgressing people" - the ṢALĀH is not accepted when done without willingness. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who comes willingly.
- 9:56 "they are not of you, but they are a people who are divided" - the ṢALĀH-dividers: those who come lazily are divided from the community. The ṢALĀH is the act that unifies; the lazy ṢALĀH divides. The same as D3 (2:177 "those are the ones who have been truthful, and those are the righteous") - the ṢALĀH is the marker of truthfulness and righteousness; the lazy ṢALĀH is the marker of hypocrisy.

PARALLELS:
- D5 (2:45 - "heavy except on the humble"): D5 gave the ṢALĀH's WEIGHT (heavy except for the humble). D25 gives the ṢALĀH's LAZINESS (the hypocrite comes lazily). The ṢALĀH is heavy for the proud (D5) and lazy for the hypocrite (D25). The ṢALĀH is light for the humble (D5) and willing for the believer. The same act, different attitudes.
- D15 (5:58 - "when you call to the ṢALĀH, they take it as ridicule"): D15 gave the ṢALĀH's MOCKERY (the disbelievers mock the call). D25 gives the ṢALĀH's LAZINESS (the hypocrites come lazily). The ṢALĀH is both mocked (D15) and approached lazily (D25) - two forms of rejection.
- D3 (2:177 - "those are the ones who have been truthful, and those are the righteous"): D3 gave the ṢALĀH as the marker of truthfulness and righteousness. D25 gives the ṢALĀH as the marker of hypocrisy (lazy approach). The ṢALĀH is both the marker of truthfulness (D3) and the marker of hypocrisy (D25). The ṢALĀH is the divider.
- B7 (9:84 - "do not s-l-l on them"): B7's withholding of ṢALĀH from the disbelievers matches D25's lazy approach to the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is withheld from the disbelievers (B7) and approached lazily by the hypocrites (D25). The ṢALĀH is the act that divides: granted to believers, withheld from disbelievers, approached lazily by hypocrites.
- A7 (19:59 - "they lost the ṢALĀH and followed desires"): the ṢALĀH-loser follows desires. D25's hypocrite comes to the ṢALĀH lazily and spends unwillingly. Both are forms of ṢALĀH-rejection: the ṢALĀH-loser loses the ṢALĀH; the hypocrite comes to it lazily.

FINDING: D25 gives the ṢALĀH's LAZINESS-DIMENSION: "they do NOT COME TO THE SALAH EXCEPT WHILE THEY ARE LAZY." The ṢALĀH is approached LAZILY by the hypocrite. The ṢALĀH is not a desired act for the hypocrite; it is a burden. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who comes WILLINGLY (the believer) vs LAZILY (the hypocrite). And the ṢALĀH + SPENDING pair is done UNWILLINGLY by the hypocrite: "they do not come to the ṢALĀH except while lazy, and they do not spend except while unwilling." Both are the same: the hypocrite does both without desire. The ṢALĀH is the divider: the believer comes willingly; the hypocrite comes lazily.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The LAZINESS (kusālā) - the ṢALĀH is approached lazily by the hypocrite. HOLD for E-cluster (the transformative check: does the ṢALĀH transform laziness into willingness?). (ii) The UNWILLINGNESS (kārihūn) - the ṢALĀH and spending are done unwillingly by the hypocrite. HOLD for E-cluster. (iii) The ACCEPTANCE-CONDITION (9:53-54) - the ṢALĀH is not accepted when done without willingness. HOLD for the final doc's acceptance-table. (iv) The ṢALĀH now has TWENTY forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20), peace-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace, D22), brotherhood-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers in religion, D23), mosque-maintaining (ṢALĀH-establisher maintains God's mosques, D24), and laziness-targeted (hypocrite comes lazily, D25). All twenty are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D26 (9:71 - believing men and women: establish salah + enjoin right + forbid wrong) next.

## D26 - 9:71 - wa-l-mu'minuna wa-l-mu'minatu ba'duhum awliya'u ba'din ya'muruna bi-l-ma'rufi wa yanhawna 'ani al-munkari wa yuqimu-s-salata wa yu'tu-na-z-zakata wa yuti'una Allaha wa rasulahu

ARABIC (flow): **9:71 "The believing men and believing women are allies of one another. They ENJOIN what is right and FORBID what is wrong and ESTABLISH THE SALAH and GIVE THE ZAKAH and OBEY God and His Messenger. Those - God will have mercy upon them. Indeed God is Exalted in Might and Wise"** -> 9:72 "God has promised the believing men and believing women gardens beneath which rivers flow, wherein they will abide eternally, and good dwellings in gardens of perpetual residence; but the greatest bliss is the pleasure of God. That is the supreme success."

WORD FORM: yaʾmurūna (enjoin) bi-l-maʿrūfi (what is right) + yanha (forbid) ʿani al-munkari (what is wrong) + yuqīmūna (establish) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + yuʾtūna (give) al-zakāta (the zakah) + yuṭīʿūna (obey) Allāha (God) wa rasūlahu (and His Messenger). The ṢALĀH is act #3 of a five-act formula.

CONTEXT: MADANI, Surah at-Tawba (9). The verse sits in the hypocrite-passage (9:67-72): the hypocrites (9:67-68), the believers are allies of one another (9:71), they enjoin right, forbid wrong, establish ṢALĀH, give zakah, obey God and His Messenger (9:71), those - God will have mercy on them (9:71), God has promised them gardens (9:72).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE FIVE-ACT FORMULA: (1) enjoin what is right (amr bi-l-maʿrūf), (2) forbid what is wrong (nahy ʿan al-munkar), (3) ESTABLISH THE SALAH, (4) GIVE THE ZAKAH, (5) OBEY God and His Messenger. The ṢALĀH is act #3 of a five-act formula. The same as C19 (31:17: establish ṢALĀH + enjoin right + forbid wrong + patience) but with obedience to God and His Messenger added. The ṢALĀH is the MIDDLE act: enjoin right and forbid wrong are the SOCIAL acts; establish ṢALĀH and give zakah are the RELIGIOUS acts; obey God and His Messenger is the OBEDIENCE act.
- THE ALLIANCE (awliyāʾ): "the believing men and believing women are allies of one another." The ṢALĀH-establishers are ALLIES of one another. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is allied with other believers. The ṢALĀH is the community-bond: the ṢALĀH is the act that binds the community together. The same as C2 (2:43 "bow with those who bow") - the ṢALĀH is a community-act.
- 9:72 "God has promised the believing men and believing women gardens beneath which rivers flow" - the ṢALĀH-establishers are PROMISED gardens. The ṢALĀH is rewarded with gardens (jannāt). The same as C1 (98:7 "those are the best of creatures" -> 98:8 gardens of Eden) and D4 (2:277 "for them is their reward with their Lord").
- "Those - God will have mercy upon them" (sayarḥamuhum Allāhu) - the ṢALĀH-establishers receive MERCY (raḥmah). The same as B2 (ṢALAWĀT + raḥmah), B3 (ṢALĀH + raḥmah), B5 (ṢALĀH + sakan). The ṢALĀH always ends in mercy on the God-side.
- "Indeed God is Exalted in Might and Wise" (ʿazīzun ḥakīmun) - the ṢALĀH is given by the Mighty and Wise God. The ṢALĀH is a mighty and wise act - the same as D12 (4:162 "the firm in knowledge... establishers of the ṢALĀH").

PARALLELS:
- C19 (31:17 - "establish the ṢALĀH, enjoin right, forbid wrong, be patient"): C19's four-command formula (ṢALĀH + enjoin + forbid + patience) and D26's five-act formula (enjoin + forbid + ṢALĀH + zakah + obey) are the SAME structure with obedience added. The ṢALĀH is the MIDDLE act in both.
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): C1's two-pillar formula (ṢALĀH + zakah) is embedded in D26's five-act formula. The two-pillar formula is the CORE; the five-act formula is the FULL character.
- D3 (2:177 - birr: 10-item character list): D3's 10-item list and D26's 5-act formula are the SAME structure: the ṢALĀH is embedded in a comprehensive character. D3's list is longer; D26's is more focused (enjoin, forbid, ṢALĀH, zakah, obey).
- D25 (9:54 - "they do not come to the ṢALĀH except while they are lazy"): D25 gave the ṢALĀH's LAZINESS (hypocrites come lazily). D26 gives the ṢALĀH as the act of the ALLIED (believers are allies of one another and establish the ṢALĀH). The ṢALĀH is both the act of the lazy (D25) and the act of the allied (D26). The ṢALĀH is the divider.
- B3 (33:43 - divine ṢALĀH -> SALĀM-greeting): D26's "God will have mercy upon them" is the God-side's response to the human ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH-chain: human ṢALĀH (D26) -> divine mercy (D26) -> gardens (9:72).
- 9:72 "the greatest bliss is the pleasure of God" - the ṢALĀH is the act that earns God's pleasure (riḍwān). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who seeks God's pleasure, not just gardens.

FINDING: D26 gives the ṢALĀH as the MIDDLE act of a FIVE-act formula of the believer: (1) enjoin right, (2) forbid wrong, (3) ESTABLISH THE SALAH, (4) give zakah, (5) obey God and His Messenger. The ṢALĀH is the RELIGIOUS act in the middle of the SOCIAL acts (enjoin/forbid) and the OBEDIENCE act (obey God and His Messenger). And the ṢALĀH-establishers are ALLIES of one another (awliyāʾ) - the ṢALĀH is the community-bond. The ṢALĀH-establishers receive MERCY (raḥmah) and are PROMISED gardens (jannāt). The ṢALĀH is both a community-bond and a mercy-earner.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The FIVE-ACT FORMULA (9:71) - enjoin right + forbid wrong + ṢALĀH + zakah + obey God and His Messenger. HOLD for the final doc's formula-table. (ii) The ALLIANCE (awliyāʾ) - the ṢALĀH-establishers are allies of one another. HOLD for the final doc's community-table. (iii) The MERCY (sayarḥamuhum) - the ṢALĀH-establishers receive mercy. HOLD for the final doc's mercy-table. (iv) The GARDENS (9:72) - the ṢALĀH-establishers are promised gardens. HOLD for the final doc's reward-table. (v) The ṢALĀH now has TWENTY-ONE forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20), peace-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace, D22), brotherhood-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers in religion, D23), mosque-maintaining (ṢALĀH-establisher maintains God's mosques, D24), laziness-targeted (hypocrite comes lazily, D25), and community-bond (believers are allies who establish ṢALĀH, D26). All twenty-one are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D27 (10:87 - Musa&Harun in Egypt: establish salah in houses as qibla) next.

## D27 - 10:87 - wa awḥaynā ilā Mūsā wa akhīhi an tabawwāʾ li-qawmikumā bi-Miṣra buyūtan wa-jʿalū buyūtakum qiblatan wa-aqīmū al-ṣalāta

ARABIC (flow): 10:84 "And Moses said: O my people, if you have believed in God, then put your trust in Him, if you are submitters (muslimīn)" -> 10:85 "They said: Upon God we have put our trust. Our Lord, do not make us a trial for the wrongdoing people" -> 10:86 "And save us by Your mercy from the disbelieving people" -> **10:87 "And We inspired to Moses and his brother: Take for your people houses in Egypt and MAKE YOUR HOUSES A QIBLA and ESTABLISH THE SALAH. And give good tidings to the believers"** -> 10:88 "And Moses said: Our Lord, You have given Pharaoh and his chiefs splendor and wealth in the worldly life, Our Lord, to lead [people] astray from Your way. Our Lord, obliterate their wealth and harden their hearts so that they will not believe until they see the painful punishment" -> 10:89 "He said: Your supplication has been answered; so remain steadfast and do not follow the way of those who do not know."

WORD FORM: tabawwāʾ (take/settle) li-qawmikumā (for your people) bi-Miṣra (in Egypt) buyūtan (houses) + wa-jʿalū (and make) buyūtakum (your houses) qiblatan (a qibla/direction of prayer) + wa-aqīmū (and establish) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + wa bashshir (and give good tidings to) al-muʾminīn (the believers). The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED in houses that are made into a QIBLA (direction of prayer).

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah Yunus (10). The verse sits in the Moses-Pharaoh passage (10:84-89): Moses commands his people to trust in God (10:84), they put their trust in God (10:85), God inspires Moses and his brother to take houses in Egypt and make them a qibla and establish the ṢALĀH (10:87), Moses prays against Pharaoh (10:88), God answers and commands steadfastness (10:89).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE QIBLA (direction of prayer): "MAKE YOUR HOUSES A QIBLA" (wa-jʿalū buyūtakum qiblatan). The ṢALĀH has a DIRECTION (qibla): the houses of the Israelites in Egypt are made into a qibla (direction of prayer). The ṢALĀH is directed toward a specific direction. The ṢALĀH is not just a standing-state; it is a DIRECTED standing-state. The same as C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site) and C13 (3:39 - MIḤRĀB, the sanctuary). The ṢALĀH has both a SITE (houses) and a DIRECTION (qibla).
- THE ESTABLISH-VERB (aqīmū) AGAIN: "establish the ṢALĀH" - the same aqīmū as C1 (98:5), C2 (2:43), etc. The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED, not performed. The ṢALĀH is established in houses that are made into a qibla. The establishing and the direction are paired: you establish the ṢALĀH toward a specific direction.
- "GIVE GOOD TIDINGS TO THE BELIEVERS" (wa bashshir al-muʾminīn): the ṢALĀH is good news for the believers. The ṢALĀH is the act that brings glad tidings. The same as 9:99 (B6 - "God will admit them to His mercy") and 9:103 (B5 - "your ṣalāh is their tranquility"). The ṢALĀH is the act that brings good news.
- 10:84 "if you are submitters (muslimīn)" - the ṢALĀH is the act of the submitters. The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-God as submission. The same as C5 (2:128 "make us submitters") and C12 (6:163 "I am the first of the submitters").
- 10:89 "remain steadfast (istaqīmā) and do not follow the way of those who do not know" - the ṢALĀH requires steadfastness (istaqāma). The same as C18 (20:132 "be steadfast upon it"). The ṢALĀH is not a one-time act; it requires steadfastness.

PARALLELS:
- C5 (2:125 - MUSSALLA, the standing-site): the MUSSALLA is the standing-site at the House. D27's houses-in-Egypt are made into a QIBLA (direction of prayer). The standing-site (MUSSALLA) and the direction (QIBLA) are paired: the MUSSALLA is the place; the QIBLA is the direction. The ṢALĀH has both a SITE and a DIRECTION.
- C13 (3:39 - Zakariyya standing-praying in the miḥrāb): the STANDING-POSTURE (qāʾim) is the physical form. D27's QIBLA is the directional form. The ṢALĀH has both a posture (standing) and a direction (qibla).
- C1 (98:5 - "establish the ṢALĀH and give the zakah"): the SAME establish-verb (aqīmū). The ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED (made-stand) in houses that are made into a qibla. The establishing and the direction are paired.
- C10 (20:14 - "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance"): C10 gave the ṢALĀH's PURPOSE (remembrance). D27 gives the ṢALĀH's DIRECTION (qibla). The ṢALĀH has both a purpose (remembrance) and a direction (qibla).
- A19 (87:15 - fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name): the human act of standing-toward-the-Name is what the ṢALĀH (established toward a qibla) IS. The ṢALĀH is the standing-toward-God in a specific direction.
- 10:84 "if you are submitters (muslimīn)" - the ṢALĀH is the act of the submitters. The same as C5 (2:128 "make us submitters") and C12 (6:163 "I am the first of the submitters").

FINDING: D27 gives the ṢALĀH's DIRECTION-DIMENSION (qibla): "make your houses a QIBLA and ESTABLISH THE SALAH." The ṢALĀH has a DIRECTION (qibla): the houses of the Israelites in Egypt are made into a qibla (direction of prayer). The ṢALĀH is directed toward a specific direction. The ṢALĀH has both a SITE (houses) and a DIRECTION (qibla). The ṢALĀH is not just a standing-state; it is a DIRECTED standing-state. And the ṢALĀH is good news for the believers (10:87 "give good tidings to the believers").

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The QIBLA (direction of prayer) - the ṢALĀH has a direction. HOLD for the final doc's direction-table. (ii) The SITE + DIRECTION pairing (houses + qibla) - the ṢALĀH has both a site and a direction. HOLD for the final doc's site-direction table. (iii) The STEADFASTNESS (10:89 "remain steadfast") - the ṢALĀH requires steadfastness. HOLD for E-cluster. (iv) The ṢALĀH now has TWENTY-TWO forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20), peace-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace, D22), brotherhood-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers in religion, D23), mosque-maintaining (ṢALĀH-establisher maintains God's mosques, D24), laziness-targeted (hypocrite comes lazily, D25), community-bond (believers are allies who establish ṢALĀH, D26), and directed (has a qibla, D27). All twenty-two are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see D28 (13:22 - ???) next.
