## E7 - 23:9 - alladhina hum 'ala salawatihim yuhafizun

ARABIC (flow): 23:1 "Successful are the believers" -> 23:2 "Those who are HUMBLE (khāshiʿūn) in their ṢALĀH" -> 23:3 "And those who turn away from idle talk (laghw)" -> 23:4 "And those who are doers of the zakah" -> 23:5-7 "And those who guard their private parts, except from their wives or what their right hands possess..." -> 23:8 "And those who are faithful to their trusts and their covenants" -> **23:9 "And those who GUARD (yuḥāfiẓūna) their SALAWAT (plural)"** -> 23:10-11 "Those are the inheritors who will inherit the Firdaws; they will abide therein eternally."

WORD FORM: yuḥāfiẓūna (they guard/preserve) + ʿalā (upon) + ṣalawātihim (their ṣalawāt, plural). The ṢALAWĀT (plural) are GUARDED. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who GUARDS his ṢALAWĀT.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-Mu'minun (23). The verse sits in the success-passage (23:1-11): successful are the believers (23:1), those who are humble in their ṢALĀH (23:2), those who turn away from idle talk (23:3), those who are doers of the zakah (23:4), those who guard their private parts (23:5-7), those who are faithful to trusts and covenants (23:8), those who GUARD their ṢALAWĀT (23:9), those are the inheritors of Firdaws (23:10-11).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE GUARD-VERB (yuḥāfiẓūna): "those who GUARD their ṢALAWĀT." The ṢALAWĀT (plural) are GUARDED. The same "guard" (ḥāfiẓ) as C16 (2:238 "guard the ṢALAWAT and the middle ṢALĀH") and D19 (6:92 "they maintain their ṢALĀH"). The ṢALĀH is a TRUST to be preserved.
- THE PLURAL (ṣalawātihim): "their ṢALAWĀT" (plural). The ṢALĀH is GUARDED in its PLURAL form: the renewed cycles (ṣalawāt) are guarded. The same as B2 (2:157 "upon them are ṢALAWĀT from their Lord") and B6 (9:99 "ṣalawāt of the Messenger"). The plural = renewed cycles, and the renewed cycles are GUARDED.
- THE SINGULAR vs PLURAL: E6 (23:2) used the SINGULAR (ṣalātihim - their ṢALĀH, the standing-state); E7 (23:9) uses the PLURAL (ṣalawātihim - their ṢALAWĀT, the renewed cycles). The ṢALĀH is both a STATE (singular) and a CYCLE (plural), and BOTH are guarded. The standing-state and the renewed cycles are both preserved.
- 23:10-11 "Those are the inheritors who will inherit the Firdaws" - the ṢALĀH-guarders are the INHERITORS of Firdaws. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who inherits Firdaws. The same as E6 (23:2 - the humble in ṢALĀH are inheritors of Firdaws).

PARALLELS:
- C16 (2:238 - "guard the ṢALAWAT and the middle ṢALĀH"): C16 gave the ṢALĀH's GUARDING (preserve the timed prayers, especially the middle one). E7 repeats it: the ṢALAWĀT (plural) are guarded. The ṢALĀH is both guarded (C16) and the object of guarding (E7). The guarding and the guarded are paired.
- D19 (6:92 - "those who believe in the Hereafter believe in it, and they maintain their ṢALĀH"): D19 gave the ṢALĀH as a MAINTAINED trust. E7 repeats it: the ṢALAWĀT are guarded. The ṢALĀH is both maintained (D19) and guarded (E7). The maintaining and the guarding are the same.
- E6 (23:2 - "those who are humble in their ṢALĀH"): E6 gave the ṢALĀH's HUMILITY. E7 gives the ṢALĀH's GUARDING. The ṢALĀH is both humble (E6) and guarded (E7). The humility and the guarding are paired: the humble guard their ṢALĀH.
- C13 (3:39 - Zakariyya standing-praying in the miḥrāb): the STANDING-POSTURE (qāʾim) is the physical form. E7 gives the ṢALĀH's GUARDING. The ṢALĀH is both standing (C13) and guarded (E7). The standing and the guarding are paired: the stander guards his standing.
- 23:9 "those who guard their ṢALAWĀT" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who guards his ṢALAWĀT. The same as C16 (2:238 "guard the ṢALAWAT"). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who preserves his standing-state.

FINDING: E7 gives the ṢALĀH's GUARDING-DIMENSION: "those who GUARD their ṢALAWĀT." The ṢALAWĀT (plural, renewed cycles) are GUARDED. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who GUARDS his ṢALAWĀT. The ṢALĀH is a TRUST to be preserved. The ṢALĀH-humble (E6) are the ṢALĀH-guarders (E7). The humility and the guarding are paired: the humble guard their ṢALĀH. And the ṢALĀH-guarders are the INHERITORS of Firdaws (23:10-11).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The GUARD-VERB (yuḥāfiẓūna) - the ṢALĀH is a trust to be preserved. HOLD for the final doc's trust-table. (ii) The PLURAL (ṣalawātihim) - the renewed cycles are guarded. HOLD for the final doc's cycle-table. (iii) The HUMILITY + GUARDING pair (E6 + E7) - the humble guard their ṢALĀH. HOLD for the final doc's humility-table. (iv) The ṢALĀH now has THIRTY-NINE forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20), peace-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace, D22), brotherhood-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers in religion, D23), mosque-maintaining (ṢALĀH-establisher maintains God's mosques, D24), laziness-targeted (hypocrite comes lazily, D25), community-bond (believers are allies who establish ṢALĀH, D26), directed (has a qibla, D27), God-facing (seeks God's face, repels evil with good, D28), urgent (must be done before the Day, D29), leadership (ṢALĀH-establisher is a leader who guides by God's command, D30), heart-trembling (heart trembles when God is mentioned, D31), first-act-of-the-empowered (ṢALĀH is the first act when given authority, D32), no-difficulty (the religion has no difficulty; the ṢALĀH is easy, D33), non-distracted (neither trade nor sale distracts from the ṢALĀH, D34), successional (the ṢALĀH is the act of the successors, D35), household-clock (the ṢALĀH is the time-marker of the household's privacy rules, D36), Hereafter-certain (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is certain of the Hereafter, D37), turning-to-God (the ṢALĀH is the turning-to-God act, D38), Hereafter-certain-repeat (31:4 = 27:3, D39), unseen-God-fearer (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears God in the unseen, D40), eternal-trade (the ṢALĀH is the trade that never perishes, D41), response-consultative (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who responds to God and consults, D42), forgiven (the ṢALĀH is the act of the forgiven, D43), economic-clock (the ṢALĀH is the temporal boundary of the economic life, D44), hypocrite-lazy-show-little (the ṢALĀH is the act of the lazy, show-off, little-rememberer, E3), mocker-whistling-clapping (the ṢALĀH is turned into whistling and clapping by the mockers, E4), humble (the ṢALĀH is the act of the humble, E6), and guarded-renewed (the ṢALAWĀT, the renewed cycles, are guarded, E7). All thirty-nine are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see E8 (29:45 - the salah prevents immorality and wrongdoing) next.

## E8 - 29:45 - inna-s-salata tanha 'an al-fahsha'i wa-l-munkari

ARABIC (flow): 29:43 "And these examples - We present them to the people, but none will understand them except those of knowledge" -> 29:44 "God created the heavens and the earth in truth. Indeed in that is a sign for the believers" -> **29:45 "RECITE what has been revealed to you of the Book and ESTABLISH THE SALAH. Indeed, the SALAH PREVENTS (tanha) from IMMORALITY (fahsha') and WRONGDOING (munkar). And the remembrance of God is greater. And God knows what you do"** -> 29:46 "And do not argue with the People of the Book except in a way that is best, except for those who commit injustice among them. And say: we believe in what has been revealed to us and what has been revealed to you, and our God and your God is one, and we are submitters to Him" -> 29:47 "And thus We have sent down to you the Book. And those to whom We gave the Book believe in it. And among these [polytheists] are those who believe in it. And none reject Our verses except the disbelievers."

WORD FORM: aqim (establish) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) + inna (indeed) al-ṣalāta (the ṣalāh) tanha (prevents) ʿan (from) al-faḥshāʾi (immorality) wa-l-munkari (and wrongdoing) + wa-dhikru (and the remembrance of) Allāhi (God) akbaru (is greater) + wa-Allāhu (and God) yaʿlamu (knows) mā (what) taṣnaʿūn (you do).

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-'Ankabut (29). The verse sits in the knowledge-passage (29:43-47): the examples are understood by those of knowledge (29:43), God created the heavens and the earth in truth (29:44), RECITE what has been revealed and ESTABLISH THE SALAH - the ṢALĀH PREVENTS from immorality and wrongdoing; the remembrance of God is greater (29:45), do not argue with the People of the Book except in a way that is best (29:46), those to whom We gave the Book believe in it (29:47).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE PREVENTION (tanha): "the SALAH PREVENTS (tanha) from IMMORALITY (fahsha') and WRONGDOING (munkar)." The ṢALĀH is the act that PREVENTS (tanha = forbids/prevents) from immorality (fahsha') and wrongdoing (munkar). The ṢALĀH is the TRANSFORMATIVE act: it prevents the stander from immorality and wrongdoing. The ṢALĀH is the act of TRANSFORMATION.
- THE REMEMBRANCE IS GREATER (wa-dhikru Allāhi akbaru): "the remembrance of God is greater." The ṢALĀH is the vehicle of remembrance (dhikr), and the remembrance is GREATER than the prevention. The ṢALĀH prevents evil, but the remembrance of God is greater than the prevention. The ṢALĀH is the act that produces both prevention (tanha) and remembrance (dhikr), and the remembrance is the greater of the two. The ṢALĀH is both a prevention-act and a remembrance-act, and the remembrance is greater.
- "RECITE what has been revealed to you of the Book and ESTABLISH THE SALAH" - the ṢALĀH is ESTABLISHED (aqim) alongside the RECITATION (tilāwah) of the Book. The ṢALĀH is the act that accompanies the recitation: the recitation is the God-ward act; the ṢALĀH is the standing-act. The two are paired: recite + establish. The ṢALĀH is the standing-form of the recitation.
- 29:44 "God created the heavens and the earth in truth" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who acknowledges God's creation. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who stands in God's creation. The same as C10 (20:14 "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance") - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who stands in God's creation and remembers Him.
- 29:47 "those to whom We gave the Book believe in it" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who believes in the Book. The ṢALĀH is the act of the Book-believer. The same as D20 (7:170 "those who hold fast to the Book and establish the ṢALĀH").

PARALLELS:
- C17 (11:114 - "the good deeds remove the evil deeds"): C17 gave the ṢALĀH's SIN-REMOVAL (yudhhibna). E8 gives the ṢALĀH's PREVENTION (tanha). The ṢALĀH both removes sin (C17) and prevents sin (E8). The removal is after the sin; the prevention is before the sin. The ṢALĀH is both a cleansing act (removes) and a preventive act (prevents). The same as C10 (20:14 - "establish the ṢALĀH for My remembrance") - the ṢALĀH is the act that produces remembrance, which prevents evil.
- D3 (2:177 - birr: 10-item character list): D3 gave the ṢALĀH as one element of a 10-item character. E8 gives the ṢALĀH as the act that PREVENTS immorality and wrongdoing. The ṢALĀH is both an element of character (D3) and a preventive act (E8).
- D5 (2:45 - "heavy except on the humble"): D5 gave the ṢALĀH's WEIGHT (heavy except for the humble). E8 gives the ṢALĀH's PREVENTION (prevents immorality and wrongdoing). The ṢALĀH is both heavy (D5) and preventive (E8). The weight and the prevention are paired: the heavy act (ṢALĀH) prevents the heavy sin (immorality and wrongdoing).
- C19 (31:17 - "establish the ṢALĀH, enjoin right, forbid wrong, be patient"): C19 gave the ṢALĀH as command #1 of a four-command formula. E8 gives the ṢALĀH as the act that PREVENTS wrongdoing. The ṢALĀH is both commanded (C19) and preventive (E8). The command and the prevention are paired: the ṢALĀH is commanded because it prevents.
- A7 (19:59 - "they lost the ṢALĀH and followed desires"): the ṢALĀH-loss leads to desire-following -> fire-delivery. E8 gives the POSITIVE: the ṢALĀH PREVENTS immorality and wrongdoing. The ṢALĀH is the anti-desire act: the ṢALĀH prevents the desires that lead to fire-delivery. The same as C10 (20:16 - "let not one who follows his desire divert you from it").

FINDING: E8 gives the ṢALĀH's PREVENTION-DIMENSION: "the SALAH PREVENTS from IMMORALITY and WRONGDOING." The ṢALĀH is the act that PREVENTS (tanha) from immorality (fahsha') and wrongdoing (munkar). The ṢALĀH is the TRANSFORMATIVE act: it prevents the stander from immorality and wrongdoing. And the remembrance of God is GREATER (akbaru): the ṢALĀH is the vehicle of remembrance, and the remembrance is greater than the prevention. The ṢALĀH is both a prevention-act and a remembrance-act, and the remembrance is the greater of the two. The ṢALĀH is the act that produces both prevention (tanha) and remembrance (dhikr), and the remembrance is the greater.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The PREVENTION (tanha) - the ṢALĀH prevents from immorality and wrongdoing. HOLD for the final doc's prevention-table. (ii) THE REMEMBRANCE IS GREATER (akbaru) - the remembrance of God is greater than the prevention. HOLD for the final doc's remembrance-table. (iii) THE RECITE + ESTABLISH PAIR (tilāwah + iqāma) - the ṢALĀH accompanies the recitation. HOLD for the final doc's recitation-table. (iv) The ṢALĀH now has FORTY forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20), peace-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace, D22), brotherhood-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers in religion, D23), mosque-maintaining (ṢALĀH-establisher maintains God's mosques, D24), laziness-targeted (hypocrite comes lazily, D25), community-bond (believers are allies who establish ṢALĀH, D26), directed (has a qibla, D27), God-facing (seeks God's face, repels evil with good, D28), urgent (must be done before the Day, D29), leadership (ṢALĀH-establisher is a leader who guides by God's command, D30), heart-trembling (heart trembles when God is mentioned, D31), first-act-of-the-empowered (ṢALĀH is the first act when given authority, D32), no-difficulty (the religion has no difficulty; the ṢALĀH is easy, D33), non-distracted (neither trade nor sale distracts from the ṢALĀH, D34), successional (the ṢALĀH is the act of the successors, D35), household-clock (the ṢALĀH is the time-marker of the household's privacy rules, D36), Hereafter-certain (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is certain of the Hereafter, D37), turning-to-God (the ṢALĀH is the turning-to-God act, D38), Hereafter-certain-repeat (31:4 = 27:3, D39), unseen-God-fearer (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears God in the unseen, D40), eternal-trade (the ṢALĀH is the trade that never perishes, D41), response-consultative (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who responds to God and consults, D42), forgiven (the ṢALĀH is the act of the forgiven, D43), economic-clock (the ṢALĀH is the temporal boundary of the economic life, D44), hypocrite-lazy-show-little (the ṢALĀH is the act of the lazy, show-off, little-rememberer, E3), mocker-whistling-clapping (the ṢALĀH is turned into whistling and clapping by the mockers, E4), humble (the ṢALĀH is the act of the humble, E6), guarded-renewed (the ṢALAWĀT, the renewed cycles, are guarded, E7), and preventive (the ṢALĀH prevents from immorality and wrongdoing, E8). All forty are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see E9 (70:22-23 - those who are constant in prayer, and E10 70:34 - those who guard their salah) next.

## E9 - 70:22-23 - illa al-musallin, alladhina hum ala salatihim da'imun

ARABIC (flow): 70:19 "Indeed man was created anxious" -> 70:20 "When evil touches him, impatient" -> 70:21 "And when good touches him, withholding" -> **70:22 "EXCEPT THE PRAYERS (musallin)"** -> **70:23 "Those who are CONSTANT (dāʾimūn) in their prayer (ṣalātihim)"** -> 70:24 "And those in whose wealth is a known right" -> 70:25 "For the asker and the deprived" -> 70:26 "And those who believe in the Day of Recompense" -> 70:27 "And those who are fearful of the punishment of their Lord" -> 70:28 "Indeed, the punishment of their Lord is not to be felt secure from" -> 70:29-31 "And those who guard their private parts..." -> 70:32 "And those who are faithful to their trusts and their covenants" -> 70:33 "And those who are upright in their testimonies" -> 70:34 "And those who GUARD (yuḥāfiẓūna) their SALAWAT (ṣalawātihim)" -> 70:35 "Those will be in gardens, honored."

WORD FORM: muṣallīn (prayers, active participle plural of ṣallā) + dāʾimūn (constant, active participle plural of dāma) + ʿalā (upon) + ṣalātihim (their ṣalāh) - the ṢALĀH is the act of the CONSTANT: "those who are CONSTANT (dāʾimūn) in their prayer." The ṢALĀH is a CONSTANT state, not a momentary act.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-Ma'arij (70). The verse sits in the human-nature passage (70:19-35): man was created anxious (70:19), impatient when evil touches him (70:20), withholding when good touches him (70:21), EXCEPT THE PRAYERS (70:22), those who are CONSTANT in their prayer (70:23), those who give from their wealth (70:24-25), those who believe in the Day of Recompense (70:26), those who fear God's punishment (70:27-28), those who guard their private parts (70:29-31), those who are faithful to trusts (70:32), those who are upright in testimony (70:33), those who GUARD their ṢALAWĀT (70:34), those will be in gardens, honored (70:35).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE CONSTANT (dāʾimūn): "those who are CONSTANT (dāʾimūn) in their prayer." The ṢALĀH is the act of the CONSTANT: the ṢALĀH is not a momentary act; it is a CONSTANT state. The same as C18 (20:132 "be steadfast upon it") and C16 (2:238 "guard the ṢALAWAT"). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is STEADFAST and CONSTANT.
- 70:19-21 "man was created anxious; when evil touches him, impatient; when good touches him, withholding" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is NOT anxious, NOT impatient, NOT withholding. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is STEADY: the ṢALĀH is the anti-anxiety act. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is constant, not anxious.
- THE GUARD-VERB (yuḥāfiẓūna, 70:34): "those who GUARD their ṢALAWĀT." The ṢALAWĀT (plural, renewed cycles) are GUARDED. The same as E7 (23:9 "those who guard their ṢALAWĀT") and C16 (2:238 "guard the ṢALAWAT"). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who GUARDS his ṢALAWĀT.
- THE INHERITANCE (70:35 "Those will be in gardens, honored"): the ṢALĀH-constant-guarders are the INHERITORS of gardens. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who inherits gardens. The same as E6 (23:10-11 "those are the inheritors of Firdaws").

PARALLELS:
- E6 (23:2 - "those who are humble in their ṢALĀH"): E6 gave the ṢALĀH's HUMILITY. E9 gives the ṢALĀH's CONSTANCY. The ṢALĀH is both humble (E6) and constant (E9). The humility and the constancy are paired: the humble are constant in their ṢALĀH.
- E7 (23:9 - "those who guard their ṢALAWĀT"): E7 gave the ṢALĀH's GUARDING. E9+E10 gives the ṢALĀH's CONSTANCY + GUARDING (70:22-23 + 70:34). The ṢALĀH is both constant (70:23) and guarded (70:34). The constancy and the guarding are paired: the constant guard their ṢALĀH.
- C18 (20:132 - "command your family the ṢALĀH and be steadfast upon it"): C18 gave the STEADFASTNESS. E9 gives the CONSTANCY (dāʾimūn). The ṢALĀH requires both steadfastness (C18) and constancy (E9). The steadfastness and the constancy are the same: the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is steadfast and constant.
- C16 (2:238 - "guard the ṢALAWAT and the middle ṢALĀH"): C16 gave the GUARDING. E10 (70:34) repeats it: the ṢALAWĀT are guarded. The ṢALĀH is both guarded (C16) and the object of guarding (E10). The guarding and the guarded are paired.
- A12 (56:94 - tasliya = kept-up roasting): A12 gave the fire-side's "kept-up" (tasliya). E9 gives the God-side's "constant" (dāʾimūn). The same "kept-up" polarity: the fire-ṣalāh is a kept-up roasting; the God-ṣalāh is a kept-up standing. The same stem, two directions: kept-up-imposed (fire) vs kept-up-voluntary (God).

FINDING: E9+E10 gives the ṢALĀH's CONSTANCY-DIMENSION: "EXCEPT THE PRAYERS, those who are CONSTANT (dāʾimūn) in their prayer." The ṢALĀH is the act of the CONSTANT: man was created anxious, impatient, withholding (70:19-21), but the ṢALĀH-establisher is CONSTANT (dāʾim). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is STEADY, not anxious. And the ṢALĀH is GUARDED (70:34): "those who GUARD their ṢALAWĀT." The ṢALĀH is both constant (70:23) and guarded (70:34). The constancy and the guarding are paired: the constant guard their ṢALĀH. And the ṢALĀH-constant-guarders are the INHERITORS of gardens (70:35).

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The CONSTANCY (dāʾimūn) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is constant, not anxious. HOLD for the final doc's constancy-table. (ii) The GUARDING (yuḥāfiẓūna) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who guards his ṢALAWĀT. HOLD for the final doc's guard-table. (iii) The ANTI-ANXIETY (70:19-21) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is not anxious, impatient, or withholding. HOLD for the final doc's anti-anxiety table. (iv) The INHERITANCE (70:35) - the ṢALĀH-constant-guarders inherit gardens. HOLD for the final doc's reward-table. (v) The ṢALĀH now has FORTY-TWO forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20), peace-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace, D22), brotherhood-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers in religion, D23), mosque-maintaining (ṢALĀH-establisher maintains God's mosques, D24), laziness-targeted (hypocrite comes lazily, D25), community-bond (believers are allies who establish ṢALĀH, D26), directed (has a qibla, D27), God-facing (seeks God's face, repels evil with good, D28), urgent (must be done before the Day, D29), leadership (ṢALĀH-establisher is a leader who guides by God's command, D30), heart-trembling (heart trembles when God is mentioned, D31), first-act-of-the-empowered (ṢALĀH is the first act when given authority, D32), no-difficulty (the religion has no difficulty; the ṢALĀH is easy, D33), non-distracted (neither trade nor sale distracts from the ṢALĀH, D34), successional (the ṢALĀH is the act of the successors, D35), household-clock (the ṢALĀH is the time-marker of the household's privacy rules, D36), Hereafter-certain (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is certain of the Hereafter, D37), turning-to-God (the ṢALĀH is the turning-to-God act, D38), Hereafter-certain-repeat (31:4 = 27:3, D39), unseen-God-fearer (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears God in the unseen, D40), eternal-trade (the ṢALĀH is the trade that never perishes, D41), response-consultative (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who responds to God and consults, D42), forgiven (the ṢALĀH is the act of the forgiven, D43), economic-clock (the ṢALĀH is the temporal boundary of the economic life, D44), hypocrite-lazy-show-little (the ṢALĀH is the act of the lazy, show-off, little-rememberer, E3), mocker-whistling-clapping (the ṢALĀH is turned into whistling and clapping by the mockers, E4), humble (the ṢALĀH is the act of the humble, E6), guarded-renewed (the ṢALAWĀT, the renewed cycles, are guarded, E7), preventive (the ṢALĀH prevents from immorality and wrongdoing, E8), and constant (the ṢALĀH is the act of the constant, not anxious, E9+E10). All forty-two are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see E11 (75:31 - the rejector's portrait) next.

## E11 - 75:31 - fa-la saddaqa wa-la salla

ARABIC (flow): 75:27 "And it is said: who will cure [him]?" -> 75:28 "And he thinks it is the parting" -> 75:29 "And the leg is wrapped with the leg" -> 75:30 "To your Lord, that Day, is the procession" -> **75:31 "He NEITHER BELIEVED nor PRAYED (ṣallā)"** -> 75:32 "But he DENIED and TURNED AWAY (kadhdhaba wa tawallā)" -> 75:33 "Then he went to his family, swaggering" -> 75:34-35 "Woe to you, then woe; then woe to you, then woe" -> 75:36 "Does man think that he will be left neglected?" -> 75:37-40 "Was he not a drop of sperm... then He created and proportioned... and made from it the two sexes, male and female? Is not He able to give life to the dead?"

WORD FORM: fa-lā (neither) ṣaddaqa (he believed) wa-lā (nor) ṣallā (he prayed) - the REJECTOR is defined by TWO failures: he did not believe (ṣaddaqa) and he did not pray (ṣallā). The ṢALĀH is the act of the BELIEVER: the rejector neither believes nor prays.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-Qiyama (75). The verse sits in the Day-of-Resurrection passage (75:27-40): the curing question (75:27), the parting (75:28), the leg wrapped with the leg (75:29), the procession to God (75:30), HE NEITHER BELIEVED NOR PRAYED (75:31), he denied and turned away (75:32), he went to his family swaggering (75:33), woe to you (75:34-35), does man think he will be left neglected? (75:36), God's creation (75:37-40).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE TWO-FAILURE DEFINITION: "He NEITHER BELIEVED nor PRAYED (ṣallā)." The rejector is defined by TWO failures: he did not believe (ṣaddaqa) and he did not pray (ṣallā). The ṢALĀH is the act of the BELIEVER: the rejector neither believes nor prays. The ṢALĀH is the marker of belief: those who believe pray; those who reject do not pray. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who believes.
- THE DOUBLE-VERB PAIR (75:32 "kadhdhaba wa tawallā" = denied-and-turned-away): the SAME pair as A4 (4:115: nuwallīhi mā tawallā - God steers him as he turned), A13 (58:8 - secret-counsel mockery), A21 (92:16 - denied and turned away). The rejector DENIES and TURNS AWAY. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT deny and does NOT turn away.
- 75:33 "Then he went to his family, swaggering" - the rejector goes to his family in arrogance. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT swagger but HUMBLES himself (E6: khāshiʿūn). The ṢALĀH is the act of the humble, not the arrogant.
- 75:36 "Does man think that he will be left neglected?" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT think he will be neglected. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who knows he will be reckoned. The same as C10 (20:15 "the Hour is coming... so that every soul may be recompensed for what it strives").
- 75:37-40 "Was he not a drop of sperm... then He created and proportioned... and made from it the two sexes, male and female? Is not He able to give life to the dead?" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who acknowledges God's creation. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who recognizes that God created him and can give life to the dead. The same as A19 (87:1-2 "glorify the Name of your Lord, the Most-High, who created and proportioned").

PARALLELS:
- A7 (19:70 - صِلِيًّا "most scorched"): the Surah-19 bridge: 19:59 (lost ṢALĀH -> followed desires) -> 19:68 (kneeling at fire) -> 19:70 (ṣalāh-loss -> fire-delivery). E11 (75:31) is the SAME pattern: the rejector neither believed nor prayed -> fire-delivery. The ṢALĀH-loss is the ENTRY-POINT to fire-delivery.
- A4 (4:115 - "nuwallīhi mā tawallā"): the tawalla-verb (turn away) appears in both A4 (fire-delivery: God steers him as he turned) and E11 (rejector: denied and turned away). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT turn away.
- A21 (92:16 - "kadhdhaba wa tawalla"): the double-verb (deny-and-turn) matches E11. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT deny and does NOT turn.
- E3 (4:142 - "when they rise to the ṢALĀH, they rise lazily, showing off to the people"): E3 gave the ṢALĀH of the hypocrite (lazy, show-off, little remembrance). E11 gives the ṢALĀH of the rejector (neither believed nor prayed). The ṢALĀH is both the act of the hypocrite (E3) and the act of the rejector (E11). The ṢALĀH is the divider: the believer prays; the hypocrite comes lazily; the rejector does not pray at all.
- B7 (9:84 - "do not s-l-l on them"): B7's withholding of ṢALĀH from the disbelievers matches E11's rejector who does not pray. The ṢALĀH is withheld from the disbeliever (B7) and not performed by the rejector (E11). The ṢALĀH is the act of the believer.
- 9:99 (B6 - "those who believe... take what they spend as ṢALAWĀT of the Messenger"): the believer takes spending as ṢALAWĀT. E11's rejector neither believes nor prays. The ṢALĀH is the act of the believer.

FINDING: E11 gives the ṢALĀH as the act of the BELIEVER: "He NEITHER BELIEVED nor PRAYED (ṣallā)." The rejector is defined by TWO failures: he did not believe and he did not pray. The ṢALĀH is the marker of belief: those who believe pray; those who reject do not pray. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who believes. And the rejector DENIES and TURNS AWAY (75:32) - the same double-act as the fire-people (A4, A13, A21). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT deny and does NOT turn away.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The TWO-FAILURE DEFINITION (75:31) - the rejector is defined by: did not believe + did not pray. HOLD for the final doc's rejector-table. (ii) The DOUBLE-VERB PAIR (75:32) - deny-and-turn. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT deny and does NOT turn. HOLD for the final doc's double-verb table. (iii) The SWAGGERING (75:33) - the rejector goes to his family swaggering. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT swagger but humbles himself. HOLD for the final doc's humility-table. (iv) The NEGLECTED-THINKING (75:36) - the rejector thinks he will be left neglected. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who knows he will be reckoned. HOLD for the final doc's reckoning-table. (v) The ṢALĀH now has FORTY-THREE forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20), peace-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace, D22), brotherhood-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers in religion, D23), mosque-maintaining (ṢALĀH-establisher maintains God's mosques, D24), laziness-targeted (hypocrite comes lazily, D25), community-bond (believers are allies who establish ṢALĀH, D26), directed (has a qibla, D27), God-facing (seeks God's face, repels evil with good, D28), urgent (must be done before the Day, D29), leadership (ṢALĀH-establisher is a leader who guides by God's command, D30), heart-trembling (heart trembles when God is mentioned, D31), first-act-of-the-empowered (ṢALĀH is the first act when given authority, D32), no-difficulty (the religion has no difficulty; the ṢALĀH is easy, D33), non-distracted (neither trade nor sale distracts from the ṢALĀH, D34), successional (the ṢALĀH is the act of the successors, D35), household-clock (the ṢALĀH is the time-marker of the household's privacy rules, D36), Hereafter-certain (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is certain of the Hereafter, D37), turning-to-God (the ṢALĀH is the turning-to-God act, D38), Hereafter-certain-repeat (31:4 = 27:3, D39), unseen-God-fearer (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears God in the unseen, D40), eternal-trade (the ṢALĀH is the trade that never perishes, D41), response-consultative (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who responds to God and consults, D42), forgiven (the ṢALĀH is the act of the forgiven, D43), economic-clock (the ṢALĀH is the temporal boundary of the economic life, D44), hypocrite-lazy-show-little (the ṢALĀH is the act of the lazy, show-off, little-rememberer, E3), mocker-whistling-clapping (the ṢALĀH is turned into whistling and clapping by the mockers, E4), humble (the ṢALĀH is the act of the humble, E6), guarded-renewed (the ṢALAWĀT, the renewed cycles, are guarded, E7), preventive (the ṢALĀH prevents from immorality and wrongdoing, E8), constant (the ṢALĀH is the act of the constant, not anxious, E9+E10), and rejector-neither-believed-nor-prayed (the ṢALĀH is the act of the believer; the rejector neither believes nor prays, E11). All forty-three are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see E12 (96:10 - persecution of a praying servant) next.

## E12 - 96:9-10 - ara'ayta alladhi yanhā 'abdan idhā ṣallā

ARABIC (flow): 96:1 "Read in the name of your Lord who created" -> 96:2 "Created man from a clinging substance" -> 96:3 "Read, and your Lord is the Most Generous" -> 96:4 "Who taught by the pen" -> 96:5 "Taught man that which he knew not" -> 96:6 "No! Indeed, man transgresses" -> 96:7 "Because he sees himself self-sufficient" -> 96:8 "Indeed, to your Lord is the return" -> **96:9 "Have you seen the one who forbids"** -> **96:10 "a servant when he prays (ṣallā)?"** -> 96:11 "Have you seen if he is upon guidance" -> 96:12 "Or enjoins righteousness?" -> 96:13 "Have you seen if he denies and turns away?" -> 96:14 "Does he not know that God sees?" -> 96:15 "No! If he does not cease, We will drag him by the forelock" -> 96:16 "A lying, sinning forelock" -> 96:17 "Then let him call his associates" -> 96:18 "We will call the angels of punishment" -> 96:19 "No! Do not obey him. But prostrate and draw near."

WORD FORM: yanhā (forbids) + ʿabdan (a servant) + idhā (when) + ṣallā (he prays) - the ṢALĀH is the act that is PERSECUTED: the persecutor forbids a servant from praying. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is PERSECUTED for praying. The ṢALĀH is the act that provokes persecution.

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-'Alaq (96). The verse sits in the persecution-passage (96:9-19): the one who forbids a servant when he prays (96:9-10), is he upon guidance? (96:11), or enjoins righteousness? (96:12), or denies and turns away? (96:13), does he not know that God sees? (96:14), if he does not cease, We will drag him by the forelock (96:15-16), let him call his associates (96:17), We will call the angels of punishment (96:18), do not obey him; prostrate and draw near (96:19).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE PERSECUTION (yanhā ʿabdan idhā ṣallā): "Have you seen the one who forbids a servant when he prays?" The ṢALĀH is the act that is PERSECUTED: the persecutor forbids a servant from praying. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is PERSECUTED for praying. The ṢALĀH is the act that provokes persecution. The same as 9:84 (B7 - "do not s-l-l on them") - the ṢALĀH is withheld from the disbelievers; here, the ṢALĀH is forbidden to the servant. The ṢALĀH is the act that the enemies oppose.
- 96:11 "Have you seen if he is upon guidance" - the persecutor may be upon guidance (irony: the one who forbids prayer may think he is guided). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who IS upon guidance.
- 96:12 "Or enjoins righteousness?" - the persecutor may enjoin righteousness (another irony). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who enjoins righteousness.
- 96:13 "Have you seen if he denies and turns away?" - the persecutor DENIES and TURNS AWAY. The same double-verb pair as A4 (4:115), A13 (58:8), A21 (92:16), E11 (75:32): deny-and-turn-away. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT deny and does NOT turn away.
- 96:14 "Does he not know that God sees?" - the persecutor does not know that God sees. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who knows that God sees. The same as A17 (84:15 "his Lord was EVER SEEING of him") and C13 (14:38 "You know what we conceal and what we reveal"). The ṢALĀH is the act of the God-seeing.
- 96:19 "No! Do not obey him. But prostrate and draw near (iqtarib)" - the ṢALĀH is the act of PROSTRATION and DRAWING NEAR: "prostrate and draw near." The ṢALĀH is the act of one who prostrates and draws near to God. The same as A19 (fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name) and B3 (divine ṢALĀH -> exodus darkness->light). The ṢALĀH is the act of drawing near to God.
- 96:1-5 "Read in the name of your Lord who created... taught by the pen... taught man what he knew not" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is TAUGHT by God. The ṢALĀH is the act of the taught.
- 96:6-8 "man transgresses because he sees himself self-sufficient... to your Lord is the return" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT see himself as self-sufficient. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who knows he will return to God.

PARALLELS:
- E11 (75:31 - "He neither believed nor prayed"): E11 gave the ṢALĀH as the act of the BELIEVER (the rejector neither believes nor prays). E12 gives the ṢALĀH as the act that is PERSECUTED: the persecutor forbids a servant from praying. The ṢALĀH is both the act of the believer (E11) and the act of the persecuted (E12). The ṢALĀH is the act that provokes persecution.
- B7 (9:84 - "do not s-l-l on them"): B7's withholding of ṢALĀH from the disbelievers matches E12's forbidding of the ṢALĀH. The ṢALĀH is both withheld (B7) and forbidden (E12). The ṢALĀH is the act that is opposed.
- A13 (58:8 - "when you call to the ṢALĀH, they take it as ridicule"): A13 gave the ṢALĀH-call as the mockers' target. E12 gives the ṢALĀH as the act that is forbidden: the persecutor forbids a servant from praying. The ṢALĀH is both mocked (A13) and forbidden (E12). Two forms of the ṢALĀH-opposition.
- D15 (5:58 - "when you call to the ṢALĀH, they take it as ridicule and amusement"): D15 gave the ṢALĀH-call as the mockers' target. E12 gives the ṢALĀH as the act that is forbidden. The ṢALĀH is both mocked (D15) and forbidden (E12).
- 96:19 "prostrate and draw near" - the ṢALĀH is the act of PROSTRATION and DRAWING NEAR. The same as A19 (fa-ṣallā toward the Mentioned Name) and B3 (divine ṢALĀH -> exodus darkness->light). The ṢALĀH is the act of drawing near to God.

FINDING: E12 gives the ṢALĀH as the act that is PERSECUTED: "Have you seen the one who forbids a servant when he prays?" The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is PERSECUTED for praying. The ṢALĀH is the act that provokes persecution. And the ṢALĀH is the act of PROSTRATION and DRAWING NEAR (96:19 "prostrate and draw near"). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who prostrates and draws near to God. And the persecutor DENIES and TURNS AWAY (96:13) - the same double-act as the fire-people (A4, A13, A21, E11). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT deny and does NOT turn away.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The PERSECUTION (96:9-10) - the ṢALĀH is the act that is persecuted. HOLD for the final doc's persecution-table. (ii) The PROSTRATION-AND-DRAWING-NEAR (96:19) - the ṢALĀH is the act of prostration and drawing near. HOLD for the final doc's posture-table. (iii) The DOUBLE-VERB PAIR (96:13) - deny-and-turn. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who does NOT deny and does NOT turn. HOLD for the final doc's double-verb table. (iv) The ṢALĀH now has FORTY-FOUR forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20), peace-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace, D22), brotherhood-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers in religion, D23), mosque-maintaining (ṢALĀH-establisher maintains God's mosques, D24), laziness-targeted (hypocrite comes lazily, D25), community-bond (believers are allies who establish ṢALĀH, D26), directed (has a qibla, D27), God-facing (seeks God's face, repels evil with good, D28), urgent (must be done before the Day, D29), leadership (ṢALĀH-establisher is a leader who guides by God's command, D30), heart-trembling (heart trembles when God is mentioned, D31), first-act-of-the-empowered (ṢALĀH is the first act when given authority, D32), no-difficulty (the religion has no difficulty; the ṢALĀH is easy, D33), non-distracted (neither trade nor sale distracts from the ṢALĀH, D34), successional (the ṢALĀH is the act of the successors, D35), household-clock (the ṢALĀH is the time-marker of the household's privacy rules, D36), Hereafter-certain (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is certain of the Hereafter, D37), turning-to-God (the ṢALĀH is the turning-to-God act, D38), Hereafter-certain-repeat (31:4 = 27:3, D39), unseen-God-fearer (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears God in the unseen, D40), eternal-trade (the ṢALĀH is the trade that never perishes, D41), response-consultative (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who responds to God and consults, D42), forgiven (the ṢALĀH is the act of the forgiven, D43), economic-clock (the ṢALĀH is the temporal boundary of the economic life, D44), hypocrite-lazy-show-little (the ṢALĀH is the act of the lazy, show-off, little-rememberer, E3), mocker-whistling-clapping (the ṢALĀH is turned into whistling and clapping by the mockers, E4), humble (the ṢALĀH is the act of the humble, E6), guarded-renewed (the ṢALAWĀT, the renewed cycles, are guarded, E7), preventive (the ṢALĀH prevents from immorality and wrongdoing, E8), constant (the ṢALĀH is the act of the constant, not anxious, E9+E10), rejector-neither-believed-nor-prayed (the ṢALĀH is the act of the believer; the rejector neither believes nor prays, E11), and persecuted-prostrate-draw-near (the ṢALĀH is the act that is persecuted, and the act of prostration and drawing near, E12). All forty-four are "state" features.

VERDICT: studied - see E13 (107:4-7 - those who pray but refuse to help, and who show off in their salah) next.

## E13 - 107:4-7 - fa-waylu li-l-musallina alladhina hum 'an salatihim sahun alladhina hum yura'un wa yamna'una-l-ma'un

ARABIC (flow): 107:1 "Have you seen the one who denies the religion?" -> 107:2 "That is the one who drives away the orphan" -> 107:3 "And does not encourage the feeding of the poor" -> **107:4 "So WOE TO THOSE WHO PRAY (musallin)"** -> **107:5 "Those who are HEEDLESS of their prayer (salatihim)"** -> **107:6 "Those who make SHOW (yurāʾūn)"** -> **107:7 "And withhold small kindnesses (ma'un)."**

WORD FORM: muṣallīn (prayers, active participle plural of ṣallā) + sāhūn (heedless, active participle plural of sahw) + ʿan (from) ṣalātihim (their ṣalāh) + yurāʾūn (they show off, active participle plural of rāʾā) + yamnaʿūna (they withhold, active participle plural of manaʿa) + al-māʿūna (small kindnesses). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is HEEDLESS (sāhūn), SHOWS OFF (yurāʾūn), and WITHHOLDS (yamnaʿūna).

CONTEXT: MAKAN, Surah al-Ma'un (107). The verse sits in the religion-denier passage (107:1-7): the one who denies the religion (107:1), drives away the orphan (107:2), does not encourage feeding the poor (107:3), WOE TO THOSE WHO PRAY (107:4), those who are HEEDLESS of their prayer (107:5), those who make SHOW (107:6), and withhold small kindnesses (107:7).

GRAMMAR & LEXICON:
- THE HEEDLESSNESS (sāhūn): "those who are HEEDLESS of their prayer." The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is HEEDLESS of it. The ṢALĀH is the act of the heedless: he prays but is heedless. The same as E3 (4:142 - "when they rise to the ṢALĀH, they rise lazily, showing off to the people, and they do not remember God except a little"). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is heedless.
- THE SHOW (riyāʾ, yurāʾūn): "those who make SHOW." The ṢALĀH is the act of one who shows off. The ṢALĀH is the act of the show-off. The same as E3 (4:142 - "showing off to the people"). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who shows off.
- THE WITHHOLDING (yamnaʿūna al-māʿūn): "and withhold small kindnesses." The ṢALĀH is the act of one who withholds small kindnesses. The ṢALĀH is the act of the withholder. The same as 9:98 (B6 - "they take what they spend as a loss") and 4:37 (those who are stingy). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who withholds.
- 107:1-3 "the one who denies the religion... drives away the orphan... does not encourage the feeding of the poor" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who denies the religion, drives away orphans, and does not feed the poor. The ṢALĀH is the act of the social-failure. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is socially irresponsible.
- "WOE TO THOSE WHO PRAY" (fa-waylun li-l-muṣallīn): "woe to those who pray." The ṢALĀH is the act that earns WOE when done with heedlessness, show, and withholding. The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is WOEFUL. The same as A-cluster (fire-delivery for ṢALĀH-loss) and B7 (9:84 - "do not s-l-l on them"). The ṢALĀH is the act that, when done hypocritically, earns woe.

PARALLELS:
- E3 (4:142 - "when they rise to the ṢALĀH, they rise lazily, showing off to the people, and they do not remember God except a little"): E3 gave the ṢALĀH of the hypocrite (lazy, show-off, little remembrance). E13 gives the ṢALĀH of the heedless show-off withholder: "those who are heedless of their prayer, those who make show, and withhold small kindnesses." The ṢALĀH is both the act of the lazy show-off (E3) and the act of the heedless show-off withholder (E13). The ṢALĀH is the act of the hypocrite.
- E4 (8:35 - "their ṢALĀH at the House was not but whistling and hand-clapping"): E4 gave the ṢALĀH as the act of the mocker. E13 gives the ṢALĀH as the act of the heedless show-off withholder. The ṢALĀH is both the act of the mocker (E4) and the act of the heedless show-off withholder (E13). The ṢALĀH is the act of the hypocrite.
- E8 (29:45 - "the ṢALĀH prevents from immorality and wrongdoing"): E8 gave the ṢALĀH as the PREVENTIVE act. E13 gives the ṢALĀH as the act of one who does NOT prevent: the heedless show-off withholder does NOT prevent immorality and wrongdoing. The ṢALĀH is both the preventive act (E8) and the act of one who does not prevent (E13). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who either prevents or does not prevent.
- D5 (2:45 - "heavy except on the humble"): D5 gave the ṢALĀH's WEIGHT (heavy except for the humble). E13 gives the ṢALĀH as the act of one who is HEEDLESS: the heedless one does not feel the weight. The ṢALĀH is both heavy (D5) and heedless (E13). The ṢALĀH is the act of one who either feels the weight (humble) or is heedless of it (hypocrite).
- 107:1-3 "the one who denies the religion... drives away the orphan... does not encourage the feeding of the poor" - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who denies the religion and is socially irresponsible. The ṢALĀH is the act of the social-failure. The same as D3 (2:177 - the ṢALĀH is one element of a 10-item righteous character). The ṢALĀH without the social ethics is the hypocrite's ṢALĀH.

FINDING: E13 gives the ṢALĀH as the act of the HYPOCRITE: "woe to those who pray, those who are HEEDLESS of their prayer, those who make SHOW, and withhold small kindnesses." The ṢALĀH is the act of one who is HEEDLESS (sāhūn), SHOWS OFF (yurāʾūn), and WITHHOLDS (yamnaʿūna). The ṢALĀH is the act of the hypocrite: he prays but is heedless, shows off, and withholds. The ṢALĀH is the act of the social-failure: the one who denies the religion, drives away orphans, and does not feed the poor. The ṢALĀH is the act that earns WOE when done hypocritically. The ṢALĀH is the act that, when done with heedlessness, show, and withholding, is WOEFUL.

CARRIED FORWARD: (i) The HEEDLESSNESS (sāhūn) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is heedless of it. HOLD for the final doc's heedlessness-table. (ii) The SHOW (yurāʾūn) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who shows off. HOLD for the final doc's show-table. (iii) The WITHHOLDING (yamnaʿūna al-māʿūn) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who withholds small kindnesses. HOLD for the final doc's withholding-table. (iv) The SOCIAL-FAILURE (107:1-3) - the ṢALĀH is the act of one who denies the religion and is socially irresponsible. HOLD for the final doc's social-failure table. (v) The WOE (fa-waylun) - the ṢALĀH earns woe when done hypocritically. HOLD for the final doc's woe-table. (vi) The ṢALĀH now has FORTY-FIVE forms: physical (standing, C13), temporal (timed, C14), vocal (moderated voice, C15), preservative (guarded, C16), personal (commanded to the self, C19), purifying (purification-package, C20), eased (adjusted to capacity, C21), adaptive (shortened/adapted to circumstances, D1), patient (borne with divine presence, D6), pure (gated on purity, accessible via tayammum, D7), washed (wuḍūʾ, D13), bowing (rukūʿ, D14), called (publicly announced, D15), enemy-targeted (Satan averts from it, D16), legal (anchors legal proceedings, D17), fear-paired (ṢALĀH + taqwā, D18), Book-holding (holds fast to the Book, D20), peace-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = peace, D22), brotherhood-condition (repent + ṢALĀH + zakah = brothers in religion, D23), mosque-maintaining (ṢALĀH-establisher maintains God's mosques, D24), laziness-targeted (hypocrite comes lazily, D25), community-bond (believers are allies who establish ṢALĀH, D26), directed (has a qibla, D27), God-facing (seeks God's face, repels evil with good, D28), urgent (must be done before the Day, D29), leadership (ṢALĀH-establisher is a leader who guides by God's command, D30), heart-trembling (heart trembles when God is mentioned, D31), first-act-of-the-empowered (ṢALĀH is the first act when given authority, D32), no-difficulty (the religion has no difficulty; the ṢALĀH is easy, D33), non-distracted (neither trade nor sale distracts from the ṢALĀH, D34), successional (the ṢALĀH is the act of the successors, D35), household-clock (the ṢALĀH is the time-marker of the household's privacy rules, D36), Hereafter-certain (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who is certain of the Hereafter, D37), turning-to-God (the ṢALĀH is the turning-to-God act, D38), Hereafter-certain-repeat (31:4 = 27:3, D39), unseen-God-fearer (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who fears God in the unseen, D40), eternal-trade (the ṢALĀH is the trade that never perishes, D41), response-consultative (the ṢALĀH is the act of one who responds to God and consults, D42), forgiven (the ṢALĀH is the act of the forgiven, D43), economic-clock (the ṢALĀH is the temporal boundary of the economic life, D44), hypocrite-lazy-show-little (the ṢALĀH is the act of the lazy, show-off, little-rememberer, E3), mocker-whistling-clapping (the ṢALĀH is turned into whistling and clapping by the mockers, E4), humble (the ṢALĀH is the act of the humble, E6), guarded-renewed (the ṢALAWĀT, the renewed cycles, are guarded, E7), preventive (the ṢALĀH prevents from immorality and wrongdoing, E8), constant (the ṢALĀH is the act of the constant, not anxious, E9+E10), rejector-neither-believed-nor-prayed (the ṢALĀH is the act of the believer; the rejector neither believes nor prays, E11), persecuted-prostrate-draw-near (the ṢALĀH is the act that is persecuted, and the act of prostration and drawing near, E12), and heedless-show-withholding (the ṢALĀH is the act of the heedless, show-off, withholder, E13). All forty-five are "state" features.
